AdvCiv / K-Mod / AdvCiv-SAS historical bug archaeology progress
Created 2026-08-19
Maintained by wonderingabout + AI/LLM helpers

Purpose
-------
Systematically review the historical AdvCiv and then K-Mod commit diffs for inherited bugs, regressions, suspicious behavior changes, and useful fixes that may still matter to AdvCiv-SAS. After completing those inherited-history sweeps, separately review AdvCiv-SAS branch history for surviving regressions and flaws in executable source code.

Primary sources
---------------
For an ordinary clone/Codex/IDE session, use the tracked greppable mirror:
  LLM_Helpers/context/commit_diffs/INDEX.txt
  LLM_Helpers/context/commit_diffs/PATH_HISTORY_INDEX.txt
  LLM_Helpers/context/commit_diffs/<segment>_<practical-count>_<short-sha>.diff

For a light-source handoff, `LLM_Helpers/context/commit_diffs/` is generated fresh from that snapshot's current Git ancestry at the same canonical path used locally by Codex/code agents and inside the light-source ZIP. Current source and Git remain authoritative; normal full-history ZIP creation refreshes the Git-ignored local generated copy after a successful archive, and `python LLM_Helpers/refresh_commit_diffs.py` refreshes it independently.

This progress file and the permanent commit-rewrite map are kept in Git for future clone/LLM archaeology and export-ignored from player/release archives. The reproducible `commit_diffs/` corpus itself is Git-ignored local generated context and is excluded from its own historical patches to prevent recursive history-of-history growth.

Post-sweep supporting-source note (2026-08-22): the local `Civ4CE-main` checkout from https://github.com/f1rpo/Civ4CE was identified only after the systematic AdvCiv/K-Mod/AdvCiv-SAS sweeps were complete, so it was not used as evidence for or to certify the findings already recorded here. Its readme describes a chronological reconstruction from installer archives, and its `BtS/CvGameCoreDLL` tree provides a useful additional vanilla-lineage source comparison. The downloaded checkout has no `.git` metadata, however, and its tip retains historical material such as an otherwise unreferenced `UnofficialPatch.h`; use relevant files as supporting comparison evidence rather than assuming that the local tree alone proves exact patch ancestry. Civ4CE supplements rather than replaces the locally installed latest base AdvCiv source (currently 1.14), which remains the direct comparison for current base-AdvCiv behavior; current AdvCiv-SAS source/Git and the recorded AdvCiv/K-Mod lineage remain authoritative.

History inventory at tracker creation
-------------------------------------
AdvCivPreSAS: 3,094 commits, practical 4343 -> 1250

  full=1,249
Total historical commits in planned systematic sweep: 4,343

Default sweep order
-------------------
1. AdvCivPreSAS newest -> oldest: practical 4343 down to 1250.
2. KMod newest -> oldest: practical 1249 down to 1.
3. When a finding points across a lineage boundary, trace that specific code farther immediately rather than waiting for the broad sweep to reach it.
4. Do not "fix" a merely suspicious historical choice without understanding current semantics and gameplay impact.
5. After the K-Mod sweep reaches practical 1, run a separate newest-to-oldest systematic sweep over AdvCiv-SAS branch history with the same evidence standard. The SAS phase has its own source-touching-SHA denominator and is not included in the completed 4,343 inherited-history total.
6. The SAS phase is intentionally source-focused: review runtime/game C++ (`*.cpp`, `*.h`) and Python (`*.py`) changes, including map scripts, while normally ignoring XML, Markdown/text documentation, art/binary assets and generated logs. Mixed commits are still reviewed for their executable-source hunks. Developer/CI helper Python can be audited separately but does not consume the primary runtime-source denominator.
7. SAS diffs can contain large CRLF/EOL-only churn, often alongside work produced through Codex/ChatGPT or other tooling. Do not skip such commits or discount them by authorship: treat practical number + SHA as identity and compare the before/after runtime source after normalizing line endings/whitespace noise where necessary. The goal is to reveal the substantive code hidden inside an enormous EOL patch, not to exclude the commit from review.
8. Freeze each SAS sweep inventory at its starting snapshot HEAD so bug-fix commits made during the sweep do not continuously move the denominator. Later commits can be reviewed as a small follow-up delta.

Follow-up source review corrected the initial living-city diagnosis: automatically generated cities store their canonical XML key in `m_szName`, and `getName()` translates it on demand, so a living city and the same generated candidate change display together. The past-name registry instead froze `getName()` display text on disband and, after practical 4345, on rename; a later canonical candidate was translated afresh in the current language and could evade that old display string. The fixed repair stores raw canonical keys/literal names in the existing past vector, validates generated keys directly and compares both exact identity and current rendered text. This needs no new city field, vector shape or old-save migration; the DLL compiled and fresh save file 484 completed its autoplay smoke test.

Recording rules
---------------
- Record clean progress as ranges rather than one line per harmless commit.
- For dense K-Mod/SAS archaeology, persist each completed checkpoint immediately instead of waiting for the whole segment or chat turn to finish. Use roughly 200 commits as a maximum checkpoint size and keep smaller live cursors while working when practical.
- Tooling/methodology note: this sweep was initially carried out primarily with ChatGPT in an interactive chat/tool environment (among other practical reasons, Codex usage availability was limited at the time). Interactive or otherwise non-persistent environments can sometimes interrupt a long review before a natural stopping point; more persistent coding-agent environments such as Codex or Claude Code may not share the same practical limitation.
- In an interactive/non-persistent environment, if a large commit or unresolved investigation is still in progress after roughly 20 minutes, save an OPEN checkpoint before continuing: preserve the last certified cursor, current candidate(s), partial findings/dispositions and exact next step. The ~20-minute interval is an empirical safety margin learned during this sweep, not a review-time limit or correctness rule; very large commits may span as many OPEN checkpoints as needed without advancing the certified cursor until the commit is genuinely closed.
- Never advance the certified cursor past an unfinished checkpoint merely because lower-numbered commits received exploratory spot checks.
- For every suspect/finding worth preserving, record segment, practical commit number, SHA, title, relevant file/function, classification and final disposition.
- Keep separate counts for open suspects, confirmed defects, actual fixes, inherited/pre-SAS bugs and SAS-specific regressions.
- A summary-only or partial archived diff is not automatically "clean"; if its title/path is relevant, inspect adjacent history/current source or obtain fuller source before closing it.
- Update Known Issues only for findings that deserve durable player/developer documentation; this tracker can hold weaker suspects and dead ends.
- Prefer accuracy over finding a bug in every commit. Most commits should be harmless.

  Reviewed 12 runtime-source SHAs: practical 6168, 6167, 6166, 6165, 6164, 6163, 6162, 6127, 6126, 6125, 6124 and 6123.
  Practical-number gaps are expected because the denominator counts actual runtime-source SHAs rather than every branch commit or integer label.
  Practical 6168 -> 6163 are the recent archaeology fixes KI#211, KI#210, KI#209, KI#208, KI#207 and KI#206. Their surgical source changes were re-read against current descendants; no follow-up regression was found.
  Practical 6162 is the large upstream/1.14 merge. Reviewed all 12 runtime-source files rather than treating the merge title as sufficient. Particular checks included the experience-sorted five-pass AI unit-upgrade loop and iterator erasure, worker stale-build target guard with route exemption, `AI_ejectBestDefender` rounded divisions, founding-border cleanup, UWAI peace-demand scaling, and PerfectMongoose smoothing rewrite. No surviving defect established. The optimized PerfectMongoose disk traversal matches its own `getFilledCirclePoints` Euclidean-disk geometry for ordinary radii; its documented full-wrap semantic difference is outside normal map/radius use.
  Practical 6127 rechecked both KI#205 fixes (World-Wonder production-rank direction and game-speed-adjusted cheap-Wonder cap); current semantics remain consistent.
  Practical 6126's Military Advisor adaptive leader-button layout uses Python-2 integer arithmetic intentionally and retains a one-column floor; no overflow/crash path established for supported layouts.
  Practical 6125's Great-General immediate Instructor join exception applies only when already at the candidate city, so it removes a travel-danger asymmetry without exposing a movement path.
  Practical 6124 changes production-overflow plumbing/logging only; output parameters are initialized on entry, all production completion call sites receive the exact `handleOverflow` result, and no gameplay overflow calculation was altered.
  Practical 6123's Great-General safety/birth/retreat changes and logging hooks were checked for inverted safety predicates, stale path-end references and replay/log null handling; no new surviving defect established.
  Next: practical 6121 / 24104e1ce9.

SAS-C002 - practical 6121 -> 6114 source batch - COMPLETE
  Reviewed 7 runtime-source SHAs: practical 6121, 6119, 6118, 6117, 6116, 6115 and 6114.
  Practical 6121 only adds the builder base-XML work-rate line to basic unit help; the display is gated on a positive base work rate and does not change gameplay state.
  Practical 6119's AI Auto Play / SASGameRecord completion-cause plumbing was traced through scheduled countdown completion, explicit Python user/victory/defeat endings, active-player handoff and recorder reset state. The scheduled old=1 -> new=0 transition is logged once with its explicit cause, later zero-state calls do not duplicate the end, invalid Python cause values fall back to OTHER, and no surviving state/cause mismatch was established.
  Practical 6118 is recorder/map-log settings only; its layer-enable array, starting-player overlay and static/dynamic layer gating were checked for index/count mismatches without finding a behavioral defect.
  Practical 6117's CITYBILLBOARD_SCALE updater correctly extends its regex over intervening XML comments, and the OneArea resource second-pass `areas_tried.clear()` is the intended KI#202 repair.
  Practical 6116 replaces one offensive-production floor with a seven-era table. XML values are clamped to 0..100 and the current era is clamped to the configured array bounds before indexing; no era overflow or stale single-value path survives.
  Practical 6115 makes AdvCiv's relative-strength tech-trade discount independently tunable. A 0% effect algebraically returns neutral multiplier 1, 100% returns the inherited multiplier, and the independent SAS military-trade valuation remains active; no inverted interpolation was found.
  Practical 6114 is the larger map-generation tunability commit and received the bulk of this checkpoint. Both Python shared generators and C++ map-element paths were checked branch-by-branch for uninitialized optional state, divide-by-zero, random-index initialization and AdvCiv/BTS fallback asymmetry. `CvRandom::shuffle(std::vector<int>&)` initializes its caller-sized vector with 0..N-1 before shuffling, so the optional resource tie-randomization path is safe. `CvRandom::get(0)` deterministically returns 0, so a zero river-direction randomness value disables that term rather than causing a modulo/divide failure. The OneArea `iTarget` division is reached only while current bonus count is below a positive target; the BTS fallback ignores the AdvCiv-only sorted/second-pass machinery correctly. The desert-lake relative-percent define is documented as positive and the current 25% default keeps the compensated roll range positive. Optional river shuffled indices are allocated only for the AdvCiv path and safely deleted when NULL for BTS. No surviving map-generation defect was established in this commit.
  Next: practical 6113 / 86b81c25b7.

  Reviewed 2 runtime-source SHAs: practical 6113 and 6112.
  Practical 6113 is a very large civ-specific-building/art/XML commit, but its runtime C++/Python hunks only tidy authorship/source comments in existing SevoPedia and DLL code; no executable runtime statement changes in those files, so the large non-source payload is intentionally outside this phase.
  Practical 6112 splits the primary-area Worker signed reserve into independently tunable one-city, two-city and three-plus-city values. The selection executes only inside the existing primary-area `iCities > 0` guard, the percentage contribution is nonnegative, and the final result remains clamped to at least one Worker per city. No zero-city fallthrough, indexing or floor-regression defect was established.

SAS-C004 - practical 6111 -> 6100 source batch - COMPLETE

SAS-C005 - practical 6097 -> 6095 source batch - COMPLETE
  Reviewed 3 runtime-source SHAs: practical 6097, 6096 and 6095.
  Practical 6097 refines inherited risky-stack sacrifice selection so an already-selected current/non-obsolete sacrifice can be replaced by a sufficiently valuable obsolete expendable. The replacement loop preserves the inherited movement, attack and no-blitz eligibility and deliberately uses the same `canMoveInto(..., true)` form as `AI_getBestGroupSacrifice`; it only substitutes after the inherited sacrifice path has produced a candidate and does not broaden ordinary attack eligibility. The added combat/GameRecord hooks are diagnostic only. No surviving defect established.
  Practical 6096 audits and makes turn-valued SAS define names explicit about Normal-speed scaling versus intentionally unscaled literal/path turns. Exact-key checks found no superseded runtime/XML define name left behind and all renamed keys resolve in the current descendant. Existing production/build/disband gates retain their established TrainPercent scaling; path-turn/contact limits are deliberately unscaled. New victory-denial countdown gates use `getSASVictoryDelayTurnsFromNormalGameSpeed`, which reads the same `VictoryDelayPercent` field as `CvGame::victoryDelay`; the current 20-Normal-turn thresholds scale consistently across the eight SAS game speeds. No stale define, zero-default, double-scaling or wrong game-speed field was established.
  Practical 6095 adds generic cross-city allocation for fresh non-combat food-production units. The helper only considers safe same-area cities that can train the exact unit and finish no slower than the current city; it includes the current city, so lack of a better candidate naturally resolves to KEEP rather than a waiting deadlock. The three passes first establish maximum eligible population, then whether a suitably close stagnant candidate exists, then select the largest qualifying candidate with production turns only as an equal-population tie-break. `foodDifference(true, false)` intentionally reports a city already producing a food-production unit as non-growing/stagnant, matching the documented pump behavior. Existing invested production and Settlers are exempt at the caller. No null, zero-turn, cross-area or unintended slower-delegation defect was established.

SAS-C006 - practical 6094 -> 6093 - CERTIFIED; KI#213 broader repair deferred
  Certified practical 6094 / SHA 8227c7d328 as the 37th reviewed runtime-source SHA and practical 6093 / SHA b3b83cf78f as the 38th. The 6093 review found KI#213; the commit can now be certified because the defect and preferred remediation are understood even though the broader repair is intentionally deferred.
  Practical 6094 adds the first empire offensive-production-capacity floor, changes the primary-area Worker reserve from unsigned +1 to signed -1, widens UWAI target-contact tuning through XML, and adds war/attack diagnostics. The offensive-capacity helper excludes zero-production cities, includes every productive city in the denominator and only current ATTACK/ATTACK_CITY unit production in the numerator; an empty-production empire returns 0 rather than dividing. The choosing city therefore contributes capacity without falsely counting itself as already committed. `AI_chooseLeastRepresentedUnit` may fail without changing production, and the branch returns only after a unit was actually selected. The signed Worker reserve is still bounded by the ordinary one-Worker-per-city floor and the separate one-city early-growth cap in `AI_neededWorkers`. The `CvSelectionGroupAI` additions are WAR-level-3 diagnostics after attacker selection; nullable oldest-unit reporting is guarded. UWAI's scheme-gate refactor evaluates both `canSchemeAgainst` booleans before updating the hireability cache, but `canSchemeAgainst` does not consume that cache, so the order change is behaviorally neutral. No surviving defect was established in 6094.
  Practical 6093 introduced the KI#198 worked-land Worker-demand fallback and a stronger shared primary-area Worker floor. The floor/minimum/scrap synchronization itself is internally consistent and is later retuned by 6094/6112. Its worked-land fallback nevertheless exposed the deeper split described below between the intentionally-empty legacy city land-build cache and the authoritative custom Worker build chooser.
  KI#213 confirmed: `CvCityAI::AI_updateWorkersHaveAndNeeded` counts a worked unimproved land plot despite an empty legacy `AI_getBestBuild` cache only when `!kPlot.isFeature()`. SAS's `AI_getImprovementValue` deliberately returns 0/`NO_BUILD` immediately for every land plot so custom `CvUnitAI::AI_bestCityBuild` owns ordinary land improvements. Current `AI_bestCityBuild` explicitly has a no-bonus Flood Plains branch (normally Cottage; Cottage/Farm under low-food pressure), so an unimproved worked Flood Plains tile is a real custom Worker job. Flood Plains are nevertheless a feature and their ordinary improvement does not pass through the legacy land cache; `AI_updateWorkersHaveAndNeeded` therefore classifies such a tile as `iWorkedUnimprovableCount` instead of `iUnimprovedWorkedPlotCount`. The exact 6093 fallback line survives unchanged in current SAS.
  The consequence is not merely naming: local `AI_getWorkersNeeded` can remain lower or even zero despite a valid Flood Plains job. `CvPlayerAI::AI_neededWorkers` sums that city demand, Worker redistribution/ferry logic uses it to rank destinations, and KI#192 intentionally permits a zero-demand secondary area to evacuate its last Worker. A sufficiently otherwise-complete secondary landmass can therefore be treated as finished while a worked Flood Plains tile is still validly improvable by the custom Worker AI. Primary-area standing floors may mask the global Worker-count symptom but do not repair the local demand contract.
  The initially-considered narrow Flood-Plains/feature-preserving fallback was deliberately rejected as a partial duplicate of Worker policy. Root architectural cause: `CvCityAI::AI_getBestBuild` / `AI_updateBestBuild` remain a legacy city-side cache whose ordinary land results SAS intentionally disables, while `CvUnitAI::AI_bestCityBuild` has become the authoritative land-improvement policy; several non-unit systems still consume the old cache. Preferred pending repair: extract the city/plot build-policy evaluation from the unit-bound Worker pipeline into a shared city/player-side evaluator, let the city best-build cache and Worker selector consume the same policy, and keep unit-only legality/pathing/reservations/mission execution in `CvUnitAI`. Directly calling `CvUnitAI::AI_bestCityBuild` from city demand is unsuitable because that function depends on an actual Worker, and Worker demand must work when no Worker exists. Do not hardcode Flood Plains unless the broader unification proves impractical.
  Next: practical 6092 / c30e4e1ef0.

SAS-C015 - practical 6058 -> 5951 - CERTIFIED; inherited Work Boat demand defect found
  Certified 96 runtime-source SHAs from practical 6058 / 0b7a1566d7 through practical 5951 / 856ac747bc. A mechanical recount of the commit-diff source hunks gives 96 source-bearing SHAs in this contiguous reviewed range; together with the prior 68, the certified SAS counter is therefore 164 / 1,317. This corrects an earlier conversational estimate of 173 before the durable checkpoint was written.
  Practical 6058's war-cause/GameRecord propagation was traced through declarations, queued war cascades, sponsored wars, random events, votes and nukes. The added cause queue stays aligned with the existing war queues and untouched callers retain defaults; no diagnostics-driven gameplay state change was established.
  Practical 6054's decisive-peace margin initially looked capable of suppressing emergency peace. Deeper arithmetic rejected the candidate: emergency evaluation first forces utility at least 100 points below the peace threshold, which under the intended/default margin still enters the decisive-peace branch directly.
  Practical 6050/6049's large-army regrouping/defender-ejection changes were checked against group lifetime semantics. `AI_setUnitAIType` detaches the unit through `joinGroup(NULL)`, and the multi-defender recall leaves at least one unit in the original group, so the retained group pointer remains valid while defenders are ejected. No surviving group/state bug was established.
  The first-Settler sequence around practical 6015 -> 6008 was traced forward rather than judging intermediate heuristics in isolation. Later commits intentionally supersede earlier return-site choices; the surviving current path remained coherent under the reviewed mission/transport invariants.
  The citizen/culture block around practical 5997 -> 5994 was checked for sign/gate mistakes. The citizen fallback still requires the full city-aware `AI_jobChangeValue` to be positive, and the culture shortcut suppresses serious culture only when the team has a genuinely shorter active countdown. No surviving defect was established.
  A new inherited defect was then confirmed while auditing practical 5951's Work Boat demand/availability lineage. `CvCityAI::AI_neededSeaWorkers` chooses its primary area through `waterArea(true)` but unconditionally adds `secondWaterArea()`. `CvPlot::secondWaterArea`, however, defines the excluded primary through plain `waterArea()` while itself ignoring impassable water. With an adjacent impassable-Ice area plus another accessible sea, `waterArea(true)` and `secondWaterArea()` can therefore resolve to the same accessible area and its unimproved sea bonuses are counted twice.
  Current SAS availability accounting already guards `pSecondSeaWorkerArea != pPrimarySeaWorkerArea`, including the KI#157-era production logic, so this duplicate need can produce a false local deficit such as needed=2 versus available=1 for one actionable seafood target. The defect is geometric/edge-case rather than a general explanation for excess Work Boats, but the duplicate count is deterministic when the area identity collision occurs.
  Lineage cross-check: the asymmetric `waterArea(true)` + `secondWaterArea()` demand pattern is already present at K-Mod practical 3 / 2414225e64 and remains present in the user-supplied Base AdvCiv 1.14 source. Current SAS retains it. This is therefore classified as an inherited Better BTS AI/K-Mod/AdvCiv defect rather than a SAS-introduced regression.
  Surgical repair identified for the later KI#216 batch: in `AI_neededSeaWorkers`, add the same pointer-identity guard already used by availability accounting before adding the second area's demand. Do not globally redefine `secondWaterArea`, whose established semantics have unrelated callers. Implementation/compile testing is intentionally deferred to the user's Codex pass.
  Certified cursor advances to practical 5951 / 856ac747bc.

SAS-C016 - practical 5950 -> 5948 - CERTIFIED; ready-attack upgrade-count defect found
  Certified 3 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5950 / 1f82d3ca4f, 5949 / 42cbc76648 and 5948 / a9515bf013. Together with SAS-C015, the certified SAS counter is now 167 / 1,317.
  Practical 5950's KI#157 Work Boat repair was checked independently rather than treating the historical KI label as proof. Its availability-side primary/second-water-area pointer guard is correct and is exactly the asymmetry that made KI#216 visible on the still-unguarded demand side. The target-selection changes skip already-connected seafood and allow owned disconnected sea bonuses outside a city BFC without establishing a second defect.
  Practical 5949's lower-food-improvement oscillation guard applies only to non-resource plots after the configured era and compares the current/proposed improvement's food yield for the same plot/owner. It prevents strictly lower-food replacements while leaving equal/higher-food changes and resource plots available; no surviving correctness defect was established.
  Practical 5948 introduced `SAS_getBestUpgradeForLog` and uses it behaviorally to count `iCanUpgradeNowUnits` before deciding whether a ready city-attack stack may bypass Base AdvCiv's upgrade wait. The intended condition, documented in the commit itself, is that zero group units can actually upgrade now. However, the loop passes `AI_getUnitAIType()` from the attack-stack unit executing `AI_attackCityMove` for every `pUpgradeLogUnit` in the group instead of using each member's own UnitAI. Current `CvUnitAI::AI_upgrade` explicitly evaluates each unit with that unit's own `AI_getUnitAIType()`.
  A concrete current-SAS counterexample proves the mismatch can change the result: SAM Infantry and Mobile SAM are both naturally `UNITAI_COUNTER`, SAM Infantry upgrades to Mobile SAM, and both XML entries explicitly mark `UNITAI_ATTACK_CITY` as a forbidden UnitAI. Mixed city-attack expeditions are real in current SAS; later KI#188 diagnostics record grouping decisions into attack-city expeditions from 113 `UNITAI_COUNTER` units (plus other roles). For a Counter SAM Infantry inside an attack-city-headed group, the practical-5948 helper therefore obtains zero `AI_unitValue` for both current and upgraded units under `UNITAI_ATTACK_CITY` and reports no candidate, while normal `AI_upgrade()` evaluates the same upgrade under `UNITAI_COUNTER` and can take it when currently available. The group-wide `iCanUpgradeNowUnits` count can thus become 0 when at least one member really can upgrade now, falsely enabling the no-upgrade-wait bypass.
  Surgical repair identified for the later KI#217 batch: evaluate every group member with its own UnitAI (for example through `pUpgradeLogUnit->AI().AI_getUnitAIType()` / deriving the role inside the helper) rather than passing the head's UnitAI. Preserve the existing deterministic 90% estimate and all other bypass gates unless separate testing justifies changing them. Implementation/compile testing is intentionally deferred to the user's Codex pass.
  Certified cursor advances to practical 5948 / a9515bf013.
  Next: practical 5947 / c5b1443fb2, "Fix Base AdvCiv issue: Remote captured-city attack stacks waiting on upgrades...".

SAS-C022 - practical 5851 upstream AdvCiv 1.12 merge / PerfectMongoose meteor pairing - COMPLETE / FINDING
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5851 / 4a2c6394d1, the large merge of upstream AdvCiv 1.12 into the SAS branch. Practical-number gap 5850 through 5818 contains no reachable branch commits in this mirror; the next runtime-source SHA is practical 5817.
  The merge's DLL changes were audited individually rather than accepted as an upstream-fix bundle. The meaningful hunks are overwhelmingly genuine fixes or deliberate upstream changes: ArmamentForecast uses the analyst team's reveal state and avoids a zero-revealed coast division path; City AI corrects rival-victory/religion-building variable mixups and restores the worked-plot value multiplier; specialist-commerce tech valuation stops overwriting its discovery flag on later commerce types; assault-sea grouping iterates the candidate group rather than this unit's current group; invasion/nuclear-war analysis fixes multiplicative and enemy/self variable mistakes; namespace/comment/test changes are mechanical. These corrected forms survive current SAS and, where present in the supplied AdvCiv 1.14 source, match the later upstream descendant.
  PerfectMongoose's filled-disk changes to `GetAverageInHex` / `GetStdDevInHex` address a real ring-versus-disk averaging error; their later performance rewrite was separately audited at SAS-C001. The distinct surviving problem is in the same practical's `PangaeaBreaker.getCirclePoints` change from an ordered list to `set()`.
  `CirclePoint` defines only `__init__(x, y)` and no coordinate equality/hash. Under Civ4's Python object semantics, separately constructed `CirclePoint` instances remain distinct set elements even when their `(x, y)` coordinates are equal. Therefore the new set does not accomplish its stated duplicate removal.
  The change is not merely ineffective. Active map generation calls `PangaeaBreaker.breakPangaeas` when Pangaeas are disallowed; a meteor strike calls `castMeteorUponTheEarth`, which obtains `getCirclePoints`, sorts the returned list only by `y`, then consumes entries two at a time as the left/right endpoints passed to `drawCraterLine`. The pre-5851 ordered list intentionally produced opposite-side pairs after the stable y-sort. Passing through an unordered identity set destroys that same-y ordering while retaining all coordinate duplicates, so the endpoint pairing no longer has a correctness invariant.
  Concrete radius-4 row proof: the midpoint-circle generator emits the y=0 boundary sequence `(+4, -4, +4, -4)` as four distinct objects. With the old ordered list, the stable y-sort preserves those four equal-y entries and pairs `(+4,-4)` twice, drawing the full x=-4..+4 crater row. With the practical-5851 identity set, all four objects still survive but their iteration order is unspecified; an allowed equal-y order such as `(-4,-4,+4,+4)` pairs same-side endpoints and draws only degenerate x=-4 and x=+4 lines for that row. Other radii contain the same duplicate-endpoint structure. Thus crater shape can depend on unordered set iteration rather than the circle geometry.
  This path is live rather than a dead helper: current `generatePlotTypes` invokes `pb.breakPangaeas()` when `maximumMeteorCount > 0`, and `breakPangaeas` repeatedly calls `castMeteorUponTheEarth` while a disallowed Pangaea remains. The supplied Base AdvCiv 1.14 `PerfectMongoose.py` still contains the same identity-set `getCirclePoints` plus y-only endpoint pairing, and current SAS also retains it unchanged. No existing KI/tracker entry was found for this defect.
  Preferred surgical repair for the present architecture: restore an ordered list/`append` in this boundary-point helper rather than trying to make the set truly coordinate-deduplicating. The active meteor rasterizer expects paired boundary points, and the filled-disk averaging callers introduced in the same upstream patch no longer rely on this boundary helper. A broader alternative would be to rewrite `castMeteorUponTheEarth` to derive min/max x per y before deduplicating, but that is unnecessary for the minimal correctness repair. Code/KI implementation is intentionally deferred to the user's Codex pass.
  No second surviving defect was established in the other practical-5851 runtime hunks.
  Certified cursor advances to practical 5851 / 4a2c6394d1.
  Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5817 / 814b748d66.

  Reviewed 7 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5807, 5805, 5804, 5803, 5802, 5801 and 5800. Practical 5806 is developer-helper-only and therefore does not consume the primary runtime-source denominator.
  Practical 5807's broad Claude-generated multiline-to-single-line Python collapse was not accepted from its title.   Practicals 5805 -> 5803 migrate `gc.getInfoTypeForString` lookups to the fail-loud `SASUtils.getInfoTypeOrFail` helper and hoist repeated static lookups. The pass-3 variable cases were audited individually rather than assuming every dynamic-looking key was mandatory. Runtime-generated colors/unit classes come from fixed valid tables; GP, trait, hurry, TechChooser and strategy-overlay keys resolve from fixed lists; the uprising random-event tech/unit-class arguments are hard-coded existing XML tags; Customizable Domestic Advisor specialist names and BUG option/config keys are configuration contracts where a missing XML name is exactly the malformed-data condition the new strict helper is intended to expose. Hoisted static color/process/terrain/feature IDs preserve their values and lifetime.
  Practical 5802 is Summary-tab polish: terminology, icons, legend link and positive-only Best City/Best Defense display gates. Its runtime changes do not repair or introduce the two arithmetic/state defects below.
  Practical 5801 expands the Summary's army/city/battle statistics and replaces one aggregate upgrade-cost total with min/avg/max/total statistics. This introduces KI#220 candidate: `PROMOTION_LEADER` has `iUpgradeDiscount=100`, so Great-General-led units can have a real zero-gold upgrade, but the code appends an upgrade price only when `iPrice > 0`. A free-upgrade unit is therefore omitted from the min/average sample. With one free upgrade and one 75-gold upgrade, the panel reports Min/Avg 75/75 instead of 0/37 (Python-2 integer mean). Total is accidentally unaffected because adding zero changes nothing, and all-free samples also display zero by coincidence; current SAS retains the defect.
  Practical 5800 introduces the Summary tab and its exact DLL support-cost breakdown bindings. This introduces KI#219 candidate: the new `CyPlayer::calculateUnitCostBreakdown` and `calculateUnitSupplyBreakdown` call the internal reference-output `CvPlayer` overloads directly. Those overloads deliberately omit the public wrappers' `if (isAnarchy()) return 0` exemption because they are also used for hypothetical/intermediate calculations. The Summary then inflates and displays their nonzero subtotals as actual Unit Cost, Unit Supply and Total Cost + Supply. During anarchy the ordinary engine-facing `calculateUnitCost()` / `calculateUnitSupply()` return zero, so the Summary can display gold costs the player is not actually being charged. No later SAS diff changes these bindings or special-cases anarchy; current SAS retains the mismatch.
  The remaining 5800 Summary state/navigation and Python binding plumbing was checked: the new page IDs/tab arrays stay aligned, perspective validation is performed before drawing, tuple declarations/bindings match the Python unpack order, free/paid counts use the same internal calculations, and the existing Finance/Domestic-Advisor-style inflation display convention is preserved. No third independent survivor was established in this checkpoint.

SAS-C028 - practical 5773 through 5772 Battles XP/GG and city-capture context audit - COMPLETE / KI#221 DISCOVERED (fixed later in 6172)
  Reviewed 2 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5773 and 5772.
  Practical 5773 adds pre-fight XP and Great-General attachment context to Battle-history rows. The generic combat-details event is emitted synchronously from `CvUnit::resolveCombat` before player-facing `combatLogCalc` and before either lethal `combatResult` or the explicit retreat event. The same-owner duplicate `combatLogCalc` path intentionally preserves the richer pending XP/GG tuple; lethal and retreat recorders then consume and clear both pending contexts. Old save rows remain length-guarded, and the plot-context rewriter preserves any appended unit-context tail. No stale-context or tuple-offset defect was established.
  Practical 5772 adds defended/captured-city plot context. Its attacker-wins coordinate correction correctly records the defender/city plot rather than the post-combat attacker location, and `onCityAcquired` necessarily runs after the battle row has been recorded. However, the later city-capture patch matches rows only by current turn plus winner/new-owner and loser/previous-owner. An undefended enemy city can be conquered by ordinary movement with `acquireCity(..., true, ...)` and therefore fires `cityAcquired(..., bConquest=true)` without any preceding combat result. If those players had an unrelated battle earlier in the same turn, `_patchCityCapturedEntry` rewrites that unrelated newest row to the empty city's X/Y and marks it captured. This is KI#221 candidate.
  Preferred surgical repair for KI#221: require the candidate row's stored battle coordinates to equal the captured city's coordinates (and reject retreat/non-lethal rows for defensive robustness) in addition to the existing turn/owner-direction match. A defended-city capture already stores the losing defender's plot coordinates, so this strengthens attribution without needing a new global pending-capture state.
  Partial inspection of practical 5771/5770 below this hit is orientation only and does not advance the certified cursor; resume them independently.
  Certified cursor advances to practical 5772 / fc82d93007.

In the current SAS unit XML, every unit with a non-`NONE` `<Capture>` tag is a Settler or Worker variant with `iCombat=0`; `CvUnit::canFight()` is `baseCombatStr() > 0`, so these capturable units cannot be the combat defender/loser represented by that row. Captures instead occur when a fighting unit enters the plot and `CvUnit::setXY` iterates the non-defending enemy units, sets their capturing player and kills/converts them. The matcher therefore cannot associate the standard captured civilians with the preceding escort-killing battle, leaving Cap#/Cap blank.
  Preferred surgical repair for KI#222: now that practical 5772 stores attacker-win battle coordinates on the defeated defender's plot, match the capture event to the newest same-turn lethal row with the same capturing/old-owner direction and the captured unit's plot coordinates, rather than requiring the captured civilian type to equal the combat loser type. `pNewUnit` already supplies the capture-plot X/Y. Reject retreat/non-lethal rows. All current capturable unit classes convert to `UNITCLASS_WORKER`, so the existing same-result-type count accumulator remains compatible with multiple civilian captures on one cleared plot.

SAS-C037 - practical 5716 through 5712 World Advisor migration/BFC origin audit + retrospective vassal-observer interaction - COMPLETE / DEFECT LATER FIXED AS KI#224
  Reviewed 5 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5716 / 117cd6da20, 5715 / ec3d2640ec, 5714 / 9abe202a3c, 5713 / 4919f62e5f and 5712 / b5ca948b8e.
  Practical 5716 removes the old Info-screen `Improvements Current` snapshot while retaining the save-persistent Units/Buildings `CyStatistics` counters. The current-map improvement information had already moved into the World Advisor BFC/Territory collectors, where it is more exhaustive; no lifetime counter was silently deleted. Practical 5715's advisor-button art/World-Advisor route reshuffle is presentation/data relocation only.
  Practical 5714 introduces Territory plus the graphical-only/special-map Improvement Sevopedia grouping. Its BFC-vs-Suburbs split forms a unique set of the selected player's city-BFC plot indices and then buckets only plots owned by that player; graphical-only/special-map classification changes documentation/grouping rather than simulation. Practical 5713 adds BFC2 and the shared lazy legend helper; practical 5712 adds BFC1 and the World Advisor reopen/zoom path. Under their original invariant, `self.iActivePlayer`/`self.iActiveTeam` are the real active player/team, so their reveal and bonus filters respect the human observer's knowledge.
  Retrospective cross-generation defect / KI#224 confirmed while tracing those collectors forward: newer practical 5755 adds non-debug vassal perspectives and changes World Advisor so `self.iActiveTeam` becomes the selected vassal's team. BFC1, BFC2 and Territory then used that selected team for `pPlot.isRevealed(...)`; BFC2/Territory additionally used it for `pPlot.getBonusType(...)`, while improvement/route collectors read raw live plot state. The shared vassal-perspective feature explicitly promises that it does not reveal hidden map data. The DLL does not make master/vassal visibility identical: `CvPlot::updateSight` gives a master sight around vassal cities, but vassal-owned plots and vassal units grant ordinary sight to the vassal team, so plots can be revealed to the vassal and not to the active master's team. Teams can also differ in bonus-reveal technologies. Selecting a vassal could therefore make World Advisor aggregate geography/resources that the actual active player did not know.
  Fixed in commit bc5728f1c by keeping the selected vassal as the data-owner/player perspective while deriving a separate observer team from the real active player outside debug mode. Reveal and bonus checks now use that observer; improvement and route counts use its revealed-state APIs, while debug mode retains its reveal bypass and selected-team resource behavior. This restores the documented vassal-view contract without removing the vassal perspective itself.
  Certified cursor advances to practical 5712 / b5ca948b8e.
  Next: practical 5711 / 3ad8ca1d07.

SAS-C039 - practical 5705 through 5700 Sevopedia EventTrigger effects/prereqs/grouping audit - COMPLETE / DEFECT LATER FIXED AS KI#225
  Reviewed 6 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5705 / 964cd251f7, 5704 / f592e66e53, 5703 / 298f4e5250, 5702 / 72e780b452, 5701 / de5c11cc2c and 5700 / db46a59ae5.
  Practical 5705 is presentation/layout polish for `SevoPediaEventTrigger`. Practical 5704 expands EventInfo effect cards with previously omitted flags/scalars/script hooks and plot changes; spot checks against `CvPlot::canApplyEvent/applyEvent` confirm that negative Improvement/Route changes really do remove the current improvement/route even when the XML type is `NONE`, while positive changes require the concrete type. No wrong-sign or field/API mismatch was established in those additions. Practical 5703 adds more trigger prerequisite fields/buttons plus prerequisite-event era chaining; feature/terrain/improvement/bonus/route lists are correctly rendered as alternatives, matching the DLL's plot-trigger loops. Its prerequisite-event link chooses the first trigger containing a shared EventInfo; duplicate EventInfos do exist, but the link is necessarily a best-effort bridge because EventInfo has no standalone Sevopedia page, and no gameplay/pedia state corruption was established.
  Practical 5702 adds direct/indirect EventTrigger groupings and shared multilist connector helpers; practical 5701 moves the generic pedia-link wrapper into `_sevopedia_helpers` and removes temporary debug dumps. Their widget targets and helper argument order survive in current descendants.
  Practical 5700 introduced the earliest-era inference and KI#225. `_SAS_getEventTriggerEarliestEra` / later `_SAS_getEventTriggerEarliestEraAndSource` treated every `BuildingsRequired`, `UnitsRequired`, `ReligionsRequired` and `CorporationsRequired` entry as an AND-like hard constraint and took the maximum era across the full list. The DLL instead treats these lists as eligibility pools governed by the corresponding `iNum*` requirement: `CvUnit::getTriggerValue` accepts any listed unit class and `pickTriggerUnit` only needs one valid unit; player/city building, religion and corporation checks count/accept qualifying alternatives rather than requiring every listed member.
  Concrete current-XML proofs: `EVENTTRIGGER_MAN_NAMED_JED` has `iNumUnits=1` with Scout (Ancient/Hunting) OR Explorer (Renaissance/Astronomy), so it can satisfy its unit gate in Ancient while the old pedia helper took the maximum and grouped it as Renaissance (indirect). `EVENTTRIGGER_BLESSED_SEA` has `iNumReligions=1` with Paganism (Ancient/Polytheism) plus Buddhism/Daoism (Classical), so one Ancient religion suffices but the old grouping was pushed to Classical. `EVENTTRIGGER_EXPERIENCED_CAPTAIN` requires one Drydock (Medieval/Machinery) and one eligible naval unit; Galley is Ancient/Sailing, but later alternatives extend through Modern, causing the old max-all-alternatives inference to group the trigger in Modern even though the combined building+unit gates can already be met in Medieval.
  This was a grouping/UI correctness defect, not simulation behavior: the helper explicitly claimed to compute the earliest era at which a trigger can fire, but its aggregation semantics disagreed with the engine and deterministically placed some triggers too late. Fixed in the current implementation batch by using the earliest eligible building/unit class, the Nth-earliest distinct religion/corporation type for player-level counts, and the total-count plus one-listed-type semantics for city triggers, while still taking the maximum against truly AND-gated direct/indirect constraints.
  The helper also intentionally skips route-derived eras and can therefore leave route-only triggers in the Any-era bucket. That limitation is explicit in the code and is not promoted to a separate KI in this checkpoint; it can be reconsidered as an accuracy enhancement when repairing the broader grouping helper.
  Certified cursor advances to practical 5700 / db46a59ae5.
  Next: practical 5699 / 823694fffa.

SAS-C040 - practical 5699 through 5698 Sevopedia EventTrigger link/effect-semantics audit - COMPLETE / NEW SURVIVING DEFECT
  Reviewed 2 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5699 / 823694fffa and 5698 / 218460e3fb.
  Practical 5699 wraps EventTrigger asset descriptions in the existing Sevopedia literal-link mechanism. The wrapper is presentation-only and current link dispatch still reverse-matches the same info descriptions before jumping to a pedia page; no simulation state or list-index change was introduced. Practical 5698 expands the EventInfo effect summary. Its `bGoldToPlayer` wording is imprecise in the generic comment because the transfer direction also depends on the sign of the event gold, but current XML has only the negative-gold `bGoldToPlayer=1` case, for which the displayed "Gift Gold to Other Player" direction is correct; no current defect is counted for that comment.
  Practical 5698 does contain KI#226 candidate. It labels `CvEventInfo::getMinPillage()/getMaxPillage()` as a "Pillage Gold" range and appends the gold-commerce symbol. The DLL uses those fields as a count of improvements to destroy, not an amount of gold: `CvPlayer::applyEvent` and `CvCity::applyEvent` choose a pillage count in that range and remove that many eligible improvements, while `CvGameTextMgr`'s native event help renders the same values as pillage counts. Current XML contains live examples such as `EVENT_LOOTERS_1` with 1/1 and other events with 2-4 and 3-5 pillage ranges. The current Sevopedia therefore reports an objectively wrong effect/unit for those events.
  Preferred surgical repair for KI#226: rename/render this effect as an improvement-pillage/destruction count (singular value or range as appropriate) and remove the gold icon. Reuse the engine's existing pillage wording where practical so city/global variants remain semantically aligned. Code/KI implementation is intentionally deferred to the user's Codex pass.

SAS-C046 - practical 5667 aligned-scoreboard scroll / BUG Max Players interaction audit - COMPLETE / NEW SURVIVING DEFECT
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5667 / af7f9f3532 (`avoid scoreboard vertical overflow, and add +/- scroll buttons`).
  The new scroll implementation correctly prevents the scoreboard from running above the available HUD height in the ordinary/default `Max Players = 0` case and supplies independent aligned/non-aligned drawing paths. The surviving defect is narrower: in the aligned BUG path (default `Align Icons = True`), the outer interface computes/clamps `iScoreScrollOffset` and shows +/- buttons from the full visible-player count, then `Scoreboard.draw()` calls the pre-existing `Scoreboard.sort()`, whose `Max Players` option can truncate `_playerScores` before the new scroll slice is applied. `draw()` can only re-clamp its local copy of the offset; the caller retains the uncapped offset/count and uses them for button state.
  The BUG option is live and supported in `Assets/Config/Advanced Scoreboard.xml`: `Align Icons` defaults true and `Max Players` supports 0 through 50 (default 0). A concrete case with 40 visible players, room for 30 scoreboard rows and `Max Players = 10` proves the mismatch: the caller sees 40 and exposes scrolling; `Scoreboard.sort()` reduces the drawable list to 10; `draw()` re-clamps every requested offset to 0 because 10 <= 30; therefore the same 10 players remain visible while the external offset and +/- button state change. The default zero cap masks the regression.
  The non-aligned path is not classified as the same defect: it does not use the BUG `Scoreboard.sort()` / `Max Players` machinery and practical 5667 did not newly remove that cap there. KI#228 is specifically the new aligned-scoreboard scrolling feature consuming a different effective list length than the aligned BUG renderer.
  Current practical 6176 retains the mismatch: `_scoreboardVisiblePlayers(scores)`/`iTotalCount` include all visible players, while `Scoreboard.sort()` still applies positive `ScoreOpt.getMaxPlayers()` before `Scoreboard.draw()` slices. Later active-player centering/button additions also consume the uncapped outer list, so the correct modern repair is to make count, centering, button state and drawing consume one shared post-sort/post-`Max Players` effective player list rather than adding another independent clamp.
  Certified cursor advances to practical 5667 / af7f9f3532.
  Next: practical 5666 / be1f2fad48.

  Reviewed 6 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5646 / d01fbb4c76, 5645 / 88cda735b8, 5644 / b9bebfca30, 5643 / ad18001b54, 5642 / deba579c9d and 5640 / 3a0a232bd3. Practical 5641 / 4d872a762b changes art/font/XML/docs only and is intentionally outside the runtime-source denominator.

F038 - KI#241: Foreign Advisor Treaties rows can hide resource terms while canceling the whole mixed deal - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Origin: SAS practical 5478 / 2078e7e8e9 (`Rename Foreign Advisor's Active Tab to Treaties Tab, beautify and enhance with icons`) replaces `CyGameTextMgr().getDealString(deal, ...)` in `CvExoticForeignAdvisor.drawActive` with per-item formatting and explicitly skips every `TRADE_RESOURCES` item. The row remains `WIDGET_DEAL_KILL` keyed only by `deal.getID()`.
  Confirmed current survivor: a `CvDeal` can and does contain resources together with other annual terms. `CvDeal::startTrade` returns `bSave=true` for both `TRADE_RESOURCES` and `TRADE_GOLD_PER_TURN`, and `CvDeal::addTradeItems` stores all saved items from both offer lists in the same deal object. More strongly, AdvCiv's ordinary end-of-round `CvPlayerAI::AI_foldDeals` deliberately folds opposite AI GPT-for-resource deals into one replacement `CvDeal` containing resource(s) plus the net GPT delta through `implementAndReturnDeal`. This is therefore a normal reachable game state, not merely an engine-permitted synthetic bundle.
  The cancellation widget operates on that complete object. `CvDLLWidgetData::doDealKill` resolves only `GC.getGame().getDeal(widgetDataStruct.m_iData1)` and opens cancellation for the deal ID; it has no trade-item sub-ID. Its hover/help path likewise calls `GAMETEXT.getDealString` for the full deal. Consequently a mixed deal such as resource + 10 GPT can be rendered by the Treaties tab only as `10 GPT` while clicking that row cancels the resource too. Before practical 5478 the row used the full deal string, so visible terms and cancellation scope matched. Current SAS practical 6182 retains the resource-skipping row construction.
  This is distinct from the intentional decision not to clutter Treaties with resource-only agreements: a resource-only deal is skipped entirely and therefore offers no misleading cancellation row. The defect occurs when hidden resource terms share a `CvDeal` with at least one displayed term.
  Surgical direction: preserve the reduced Treaties presentation only if the clickable row still makes whole-deal scope explicit. The simplest correctness repair is to render the full deal whenever a displayed deal also contains one or more resource terms (optionally with the new icon formatter for all terms), or otherwise remove/replace whole-deal cancellation from a partial row. Do not keep a partial term list attached to `WIDGET_DEAL_KILL` without indicating the hidden terms.
  Fixed in the current Foreign Advisor batch: resource-only deals remain omitted and deals without resources retain the current icon-rich formatter, but a visible mixed deal containing any resource now formats every item from both sides with resource/gold/treaty icons and Civ4's localized perspective grammar. A compact leader button beside the localized player name makes direction easier to scan. Displayed terms therefore match the complete deal canceled by `WIDGET_DEAL_KILL`; turn suffixes and one-row-per-deal behavior remain unchanged. Save file 478 first confirmed the complete plain-text form in screenshot 0071, then the final icon-enhanced form rendered correctly in-game; the refreshed `PythonErr.log` is empty. Static Python syntax and branch checks also pass.
  Classification: SAS-specific Foreign Advisor UI/action-scope regression introduced at practical 5478 and retained through the supplied practical-6182 source snapshot.

F037 - KI#240: Medieval+ Copper hard exclusion overrides live strategic and corporation value - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Origin: SAS practical 5484 / 923840f152 (`(Tremendously Improved) Do not buy dominated or equivalent strategic bonuses (era and bonus-aware)`) adds an early hard-denial block to `CvPlayerAI::AI_bonusTrade`. For Copper it marks every Medieval-or-later buy as dominated, regardless of whether the recipient has Iron: `eBonus == B_COPPER && (bHaveIron || bMedievalPlus)`.
  Confirmed current survivor: Copper is not universally dominated in that era range. Base AdvCiv 1.14's generic Privateer already accepts `BONUS_COPPER` OR `BONUS_IRON`; current SAS retains that resource alternative and places `UNIT_PRIVATEER` at Medieval `TECH_PROFESSIONAL_ARMY`. Thus a Medieval AI with no Iron can have a currently trainable strategic unit unlocked by importing Copper, yet practical 5484 returns `DENIAL_JOKING` before ordinary recipient value can preserve the offer. The hard rule therefore contradicts its own "dominated/equivalent" premise for a generic, reachable unit path rather than merely missing a rare future possibility.
  Corporation valuation independently proves the early hard return is too broad. Current `CORPORATION_4` and `CORPORATION_5` both consume Copper and produce positive yields/commerce from their prerequisite bonuses. `AI_corporationBonusVal` assigns positive marginal value when such a corporation is present, and the pre-existing `AI_bonusTrade` duplicate-resource filter deliberately hides additional copies only when `AI_corporationBonusVal(eBonus, true) <= 0`. The new 5484 block runs before that safeguard and can deny even a first Copper import, or later corporation-useful copies, throughout the corporation eras.
  The issue existed in substance at introduction, not only through later SAS drift: Copper-or-Iron Privateers are inherited from Base AdvCiv, and the hard `bMedievalPlus` branch was introduced by 5484 itself. Current SAS practical 6182 retains the same unconditional branch.
  Surgical direction: do not treat era alone as proof that Copper has zero recipient value. At minimum remove the unconditional `|| bMedievalPlus` Copper exclusion so a Copper-less/Iron-less AI reaches the normal value logic; additionally exempt resources with positive `kPlayer.AI_corporationBonusVal(eBonus, true)` from the hard dominated-resource gate so the existing corporation-aware contract is preserved. The other explicit substitute rules can remain independently tunable.
  Fixed in commit d2d788086: Copper is now hard-dominated only when the recipient actually has Iron, and the hard denial is skipped when `kPlayer.AI_corporationBonusVal(eBonus, true)` is positive. Corporation valuation is queried only after a resource matches a dominated rule. This preserves the remaining substitute rules while allowing live Privateer and corporation value to reach ordinary recipient valuation. The DLL built successfully and a general in-game smoke run completed with an empty refreshed `PythonErr.log`; current Privateer/corporation XML and the source paths validate the corrected behavior without claiming a separately constructed diplomacy-save reproduction.
  Classification: SAS-specific AI trade-logic regression introduced at practical 5484 and retained through the supplied practical-6182 source snapshot.

F036 - KI#239: dominated-strategic AI-buy hard block leaks onto human recipients - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Origin: SAS practical 5484 / 923840f152 inserts the dominated/equivalent strategic-resource block near the start of `CvPlayerAI::AI_bonusTrade` under `iChange >= 0`, but does not restrict it to an AI recipient even though the commit title, KI#109 documentation, Main Changes guide and define all describe preventing the AI from buying inefficient resources.
  Confirmed current survivor: `kPlayer = GET_PLAYER(eToPlayer)` is the recipient/buyer and `bPlayerHuman = kPlayer.isHuman()`. The new block executes before AdvCiv's later `if(!bPlayerHuman)` value gate whose explicit contract says "Allow human to import resources that they probably don't need, but make sure that the AI doesn't." Consequently an AI seller can return `DENIAL_JOKING` for a human recipient solely from the AI-buy heuristic; e.g. a first-copy Copper import is hard-denied to a human in Medieval+ even though the later human-recipient path would otherwise let the player decide whether it is useful.
  This denial is not merely advisory text. `CvPlayer::buildTradeTable` calls the resource owner's `getTradeDenial`, which routes resources to `AI_bonusTrade(..., eWhoTo, 1)`, and when either participant is human it omits resources whose denial is `DENIAL_JOKING`. The affected AI-owned resource therefore disappears from the human diplomacy trade inventory. `AI_checkCancel` also calls `AI_bonusTrade(..., iChange=0)` for existing resource exports, so the same scope leak can cancel an AI-to-human resource deal after the heuristic becomes true.
  Surgical direction: scope the hard dominated-buy block to a non-human recipient, e.g. require `!bPlayerHuman` together with `iChange >= 0`. This still suppresses inefficient purchases whenever the buyer is AI, including AI purchases from a human seller, while restoring the pre-existing human-recipient trade/value contract and preventing heuristic-only cancellation of human imports.
  Fixed in commit d2d788086 by requiring `!bPlayerHuman` at the hard dominated-resource gate. AI recipients retain the efficiency rule for both AI and human sellers, while human recipients return to the pre-existing human-controlled import path. The successful DLL build and general in-game smoke run with an empty refreshed `PythonErr.log` validate integration; the explicit recipient direction and trade-table/cancellation call paths validate the scope correction without claiming a separately constructed diplomacy-save reproduction.
  Classification: SAS-specific diplomacy/trade regression introduced at practical 5484 and retained through the supplied practical-6182 source snapshot.

F035 - KI#238: SAS_Parallel_Lines extra players reuse the final custom starting slot - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Origin: SAS practical 5486 / 0870e0adc8 (`Add SAS_Parallel_Lines.py, update 48 civs DLL - Add quotes in Assets rebalancing`) introduces `PrivateMaps/SAS_Parallel_Lines.py`. Its layout size and `line_slots` capacity are derived from `CvWorldInfo.getDefaultPlayers()` for the selected world size, while `findStartingPlot` independently enumerates the actual alive civ players.
  Confirmed current survivor: the sum of `line_slots` is exactly the world default-player count. For any actual alive-player index beyond that capacity, the slot-selection loop exhausts without a match but leaves `iLine`/`iPosInLine` at the last line's last slot. Thus every excess player resolves to the same final custom start used by the last in-capacity player. Example: Standard / 3 Players Per Line builds `[3, 3, 2]`; player index 8 (the ninth player) falls through to line 2, position 1, duplicating player index 7.
  This is a reachable Custom Game/setup state rather than malformed input. In current DLL code, `CvGame::getRecommendedPlayers()` uses world `getDefaultPlayers()` as a recommendation, while actual player life is independently initialized from `SS_TAKEN` / `SS_COMPUTER` slots across `MAX_CIV_PLAYERS`. `SAS_Parallel_Lines.findStartingPlot` itself enumerates those actual alive players rather than enforcing the world default.
  The engine does not repair the duplicate: `CvPlayer::findStartingPlot` immediately returns any non-null Python starting plot, and `CvPlayer::setStartingPlot` records it without a uniqueness test. Consequently the normal C++ taken-start search is bypassed for the duplicated Python result.
  Sibling SAS maps already show the appropriate defensive contract: `SAS_Spiky_Avenues` and `SAS_Large_Facing_Islands` call `CyPythonMgr().allowDefaultImpl()` when the actual player index exceeds their custom slot list instead of reusing a slot.
  Surgical direction: before mapping `iIndex` into `line_slots`, compare it with the total custom slot capacity (or otherwise detect that no slot was matched); if out of range, call `CyPythonMgr().allowDefaultImpl()` and return. This preserves the designed custom starts for in-capacity players and lets the existing DLL choose a non-taken fallback for extras. A separate used-start set could further harden custom fallback searches, but is not required to fix this proven overflow path.
  Fixed and validated in commit 6fc4f59e6 by delegating actual player indexes beyond the summed custom capacity to the DLL before the old final-slot fallthrough. Screenshot 0065 confirms a Standard nine-player / 3 Players Per Line game generated three populated lines with distinct positions; the refreshed `PythonErr.log` remained empty.
  Classification: SAS-specific regression/design bug introduced at practical 5486.

F034.2 - KI#237.2: runtime-sized city Great Person bar retains a fixed 230px text budget - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Classification: inherited Base AdvCiv scalable-HUD regression introduced by AdvCiv practical 3784 / e491d6d576 when the scalable-hud branch was merged into 1.06; retained by Base AdvCiv 1.14 and the audited pre-fix AdvCiv-SAS source.
  K-Mod/BUG passed 230px to the city-screen `getGreatPeopleText()` caller for its fixed layout. Practical 3784 introduced the runtime-scaled `CityRightPanelContents` and full-width `GreatPeopleBar`, but retained 230 in the relocated caller. At wider resolutions the rendered bar therefore has room for more GP types than the fitting helper is allowed to consider.
  Save file 478 screenshots 0060 and 0061 expose the mismatch in Poverty Point: the wider map GP bar shows all 53% / 42% / 5% types, while the city GP bar omits the fitting 5% Scientist because its caller still reports only 230px. Fixed in the current implementation batch by passing `gRect("GreatPeopleBar").width()`, matching the map caller's runtime-width contract. Post-fix screenshots 0062 and 0063 confirm that the map and city bars now both render 53% / 42% / 5%; the refreshed `PythonErr.log` remained empty.


F034 - KI#237: Maximum-types Great Person progress text accepts one candidate beyond its width limit - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Classification: inherited K-Mod/BUG-era Main Interface display bug retained by Base AdvCiv 1.14 and the audited pre-fix AdvCiv-SAS source; discovered while auditing practical 5505.
  `GPUtil.getGreatPeopleText()` builds `szNewTypes` by appending the next Great Person icon/percentage, but measures `szText + szTypes`, the previously accepted list, before assigning `szTypes = szNewTypes`. The fit test is therefore one candidate late: the first item that pushes the rendered text beyond `iGPBarWidth - 10` is accepted. Maximum types is the current default, mixed-Great-Person-point cities provide multiple candidates, and the top-bar form also includes the variable city name.
  Fixed in the current implementation batch by measuring the candidate string containing `szNewTypes` before accepting it, preserving the current probability order and stop-on-first-nonfit behavior. Static syntax and a narrow regression fixture confirm that the old control flow accepted an over-limit candidate while the corrected flow rejects it. Save file 478 screenshots 0062 and 0063 provide the in-game Maximum-types smoke test after the fix, with identical 53% / 42% / 5% data in both GP bars and an empty refreshed `PythonErr.log`. The separate fixed 230px city-caller mismatch exposed during testing is tracked as KI#237.2 / F034.2.

F029 - KI#232: Policy Advisor vassal perspective can use the vassal team's hidden holy-city/HQ knowledge - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Policy Advisor information-visibility regression introduced by practical 5755 / 1fabcaa557 over reveal-sensitive Religion/Corporation rows integrated in practicals 5610-5611; survives current practical 6177.
  The Religion tab's global holy-city row and Corporation tab's global headquarters row intentionally hide the city while it is unrevealed. In practicals 5610-5611 this is safe because `iActivePlayer` is reset to the real active player before drawing, so `pHolyCity.isRevealed(gc.getPlayer(self.iActivePlayer).getTeam(), ...)` / the corresponding HQ check use the human observer's team.
  Practical 5755 adds non-debug vassal perspectives and changes Policy's screen-open plus Religion/Corporation refresh paths to preserve a selected vassal through `getAdvisorValidPerspectivePlayer(..., bAllowVassalPerspective=True)`. It does not alter the old reveal gates. Consequently those global rows now ask what the selected vassal team has revealed. If that vassal has explored an unrelated player's holy city or corporate HQ while the human/master has not, the human can select the vassal and learn the hidden foreign city name/owner.
  This is reachable because a vassal relationship does not merge team map knowledge. `CvTeam::setVassal` updates city sight, and `CvPlot::updateSight` grants the master adjacent sight around vassal cities, but ordinary plot/unit exploration remains team-specific. The shared advisor helper and Main Changes Guide explicitly state that vassal advisor perspectives may expose subordinate domestic/world/policy state but must not reveal unrelated players or hidden map data.
  KI#224 fixed the same subject-vs-observer class of mistake only in `CvWorldAdvisorScreen` by introducing a real-active-player observer team; `CvPolicyAdvisorScreen` was not changed and current practical 6177 still uses the selected `self.iActivePlayer` team for both holy-city and HQ reveal tests. Keep KI#232 separate so the already-fixed World-Advisor issue remains accurately scoped.
  Fixed in the current implementation batch with a Policy Advisor observer-team helper. Outside debug, Religion Holy City and Corporation Headquarters `isRevealed` checks now use the real active player's team while `self.iActivePlayer` remains the selected vassal for permitted subject data; debug mode retains selected-team reveal behavior.
