F028 - KI#231: Policy Advisor corporation generated yield/commerce row prints stored hundredths as whole units - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Policy Advisor corporation-display correctness regression introduced by practical 5614 / 516fdce425; survives current practical 6177.
  Practical 5614 adds `getCorporationGeneratedText(kCorpInfo)` and writes each nonzero `getYieldProduced(iYield)` / `getCommerceProduced(iCommerce)` directly with `%d` plus the corresponding GameFont symbol. These `CvCorporationInfo` getters do not return display units: the XML values are stored in hundredths. For example, current corporation XML contains `iYieldProduced=100` for a +1 yield effect, `50` for +0.50 and `iCommerceProduced=150` for +1.50.
  The DLL's authoritative `CvGameTextMgr::setCorporationHelp()` first scales each stored rate by the current world size's `getCorporationMaintenancePercent()`, then divides/formats by 100, including two decimal places when needed. `CvCity::getCorporationYieldByCorporation` / `getCorporationCommerceByCorporation` use the same stored hundredths and world-size scaling in gameplay. Thus the Policy tab currently presents raw storage values such as `100` or `150` as if they were +100/+150 yield or commerce per resource and can also disagree with non-100% world-size scaling.
  Fixed in the current implementation batch by applying the active map's world-size corporation percentage with C++-matching truncation toward zero, then formatting the scaled hundredths as exact signed integer or two-decimal display units before the existing yield/commerce symbols. Values scaled to zero remain omitted, and corporation gameplay/XML values are unchanged. Screenshot 0043 directly confirms correct fractional/signed output such as `+0.50`, `+0.25`, `+0.75`, `+1`, `+1.50` and `+1.75` across the seven Corporation columns; the refreshed `PythonErr.log` was empty.


F027 - KI#230: nonpositive Leader item-width cutoff reversed its documented disable behavior - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Sevopedia Leader/UI configuration regression introduced by practical 5632 / 6bc421f184.
  `SAS_SEVOPEDIA_LEADER_ITEMS_NO_REDUCE_MIN_WIDTH` is documented in `GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml` as the screen-width threshold at/above which Leader pages stop reducing the item-list width, with `<=0` meaning the custom/font-dependent width stays enabled regardless of resolution. The Leader page's panel-width calculation follows that semantics: it applies the base-width override only when `iNoReduceMinWidth > 0` and the screen reaches the threshold.
  The same practical adds a separate main-list helper, `SevoPediaMain.SAS_getLeaderItemsWidthByCurrentUIFont()`, but its first branch is simply `if screenWidth >= self.SAS_SEVOPEDIA_LEADER_ITEMS_NO_REDUCE_MIN_WIDTH: return self.SAS_W_ITEMS_BASE`. For a configured value of 0 or -1 every normal positive screen width satisfies the branch, so the main list is forced to the unreduced base width rather than keeping the custom width. The two consumers can therefore disagree and the documented hard-disable value behaves backwards.
  Fixed in commit 895c46242 by mirroring the already-correct Leader-side condition in `SevoPediaMain`: the high-resolution base-width override now applies only when the threshold is `> 0` and the current resolution reaches it. The XML comment was also corrected to describe the existing at-or-above (`>=`) threshold exactly. With the cutoff set nonpositive and label font 3, runtime testing showed the Leader item list retained its 200px custom width and remained visibly narrower than the Civilization list's 230px base width, directly validating the documented disable behavior.


F026 - KI#229: Hall of Fame Date header lost date-typed sorting during font upscaling - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Hall-of-Fame UI correctness regression introduced by practical 5657 / e51f6d39ad.
  Before practical 5657, the sortable Hall of Fame table writes the replay Date column with Civ4's dedicated `screen.setTableDate(...)`. The same screen independently provides a Sort-by-Date dropdown whose row ordering uses numeric `replayInfo.getFinalTurn()`. Practical 5657's text-upscaling pass replaces the Date cell with `SASTextScale.setTableTextLabel(...)` and stores a formatted `CyGameTextMgr().getDateStr(...)` display string, while leaving `screen.enableSort(self.TABLE_ID)` active. The table-header sort therefore no longer receives the date-typed cell that the original implementation intentionally supplied.
  The distinction is not merely historical style. Base AdvCiv 1.14 still has `enableSort` plus `setTableDate` for the Date column, and Civ4 exposes separate `setTableDate`, `setTableInt` and `setTableText` table APIs; SAS's own text helpers document text cells as lexical and integer cells as numeric. Current practical 6176 still writes the Date column through `setTableTextLabel`. Thus the dropdown can remain chronologically correct while a user click on the Date column header sorts the formatted BC/AD/date strings as text rather than using date semantics.
  Fixed in commit 895c46242 by adding shared `SASTextScale.setTableDateLabel`, which applies the configurable label font through Civ4's dedicated `setTableDate` cell type, and using it for the Hall of Fame Date column. The existing Sort-by-Date dropdown remains unchanged. Runtime testing confirmed that the Hall of Fame renders and the clickable Date header changes between ascending/descending sort states without an error; the available Hall of Fame contained only one entry, so multi-row chronological order could not be visually exercised in that test.


F025 - KI#228: aligned scoreboard scrolling disagreed with BUG Max Players cap - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS main-interface/BUG scoreboard regression introduced by practical 5667 / af7f9f3532.
  Practical 5667 adds a caller-owned scoreboard scroll offset and screen-height row cap. In the default aligned BUG path, `CvMainInterface` computes `iTotalCount` from every currently visible player and clamps/shows the new scroll controls from that total, then calls `Scoreboard.draw(screen, offset, maxRows)`. Inside `draw`, the pre-existing `Scoreboard.sort()` still honors the configurable positive `ScoreOpt.getMaxPlayers()` by truncating `_playerScores`; only after that does the new code clamp its local `iScrollOffset` to the truncated length and slice the rows.
  These two counts can disagree. With 40 visible players, 30 available rows and BUG `Max Players = 10`, the outer interface exposes scrolling over 40, while `Scoreboard.draw` has only 10 rows and locally forces every offset back to zero. The user can therefore change the external offset/button states without changing the displayed ten-player window. `Align Icons` defaults true; `Max Players` is a supported 0..50 option whose default 0 explains why the problem is normally hidden.
  Current descendants retain the split and later scoreboard autocenter controls also use the uncapped outer list. Preferred repair: centralize the aligned scoreboard's effective sorted/capped player list (or expose its effective count/order) and derive scroll clamp, active-player centering, +/- availability and drawing from that same post-`Scoreboard.sort`/post-`Max Players` population. Do not paper over only the button condition with a separate min() that could leave centering and drawing on different orders.
  This is UI correctness only; player/team visibility and game simulation are not changed.
  Fixed/validated/committed in SAS practical 6177 / 820f1a2e1a by exposing and reusing the aligned BUG scoreboard's post-sort/post-`Max Players` player-ID order for total count, active-player centering, scroll clamping, +/- availability and drawing. The non-aligned path remains unchanged. Quick new-game and late-game save-file testing passed and `PythonErr.log` remained empty.

F024 - KI#227: Vote Sevopedia duplicated the trade-route resolution effect as a requirement - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Sevopedia Vote-page correctness regression introduced by practical 5692 / fb18bad9c4.
  The initial Vote page adds `voteInfo.getTradeRoutes()` to `placeRequirements()` as `TXT_KEY_PEDIA_SAS_VOTE_TRADE_ROUTES`, while the same page also adds the exact field to `placeSpecial()` as `TXT_KEY_PEDIA_SAS_VOTE_EFFECT_TRADE_ROUTES_ALL_CITIES`. The latter is the correct semantic location. In the DLL, `CvGame::processVote` applies/removes `kVote.getTradeRoutes()` through the global `changeTradeRoutes(...)` only when processing the vote resolution; the field is not consulted by `canDoResolution` as a prerequisite.
  Current XML gives a direct visible case: `VOTE_SINGLE_CURRENCY` has `iTradeRoutes=1`. Before the fix, its Sevopedia page could therefore say `Trade Routes: 1` under Requirements and also `All Cities Trade Routes: +1` under Effects, falsely implying the reward must already exist before the resolution can be used.
  Fixed in commit ee06f1005 by deleting the Requirements-side `getTradeRoutes()` line and keeping the existing Effects-side rendering, with a comment documenting `CvGame::processVote` semantics. Vote simulation remains unchanged.

F023 - KI#226: EventTrigger effect summary mislabeled improvement-pillage counts as gold - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Sevopedia EventInfo correctness regression introduced by practical 5698 / 218460e3fb.
  The practical adds a "pillage gold range" renderer that reads `eventInfo.getMinPillage()` / `getMaxPillage()` and displays `TXT_KEY_PEDIA_SAS_EVENT_EFFECT_PILLAGE_GOLD` plus the gold-commerce symbol. Those fields do not represent gold. In `CvPlayer::applyEvent`, when `getMaxPillage() > 0`, the DLL computes `iPillage` from the min/max range, searches eligible owned improved plots and removes improvements until the requested count is reached. `CvCity::applyEvent` uses the same fields as a number of city-radius improvements to pillage. The native `CvGameTextMgr` event help likewise formats the values through the `TXT_KEY_EVENT_PILLAGE*` strings as counts, never as treasury gold.
  Current XML made the error directly visible before the fix: `EVENT_LOOTERS_1` uses `iMinPillage=1`, `iMaxPillage=1`, and other live events use ranges such as 2-4 and 3-5. Their Sevopedia choice summaries could therefore show "Pillage Gold: 2-4" with a gold icon even though the actual effect is to destroy/pillage 2-4 improvements.
  Fixed in commit ee06f1005 by displaying `Improvements Pillaged` without a gold-commerce glyph, using one count for equal bounds and a range only when the bounds differ. Event simulation remains unchanged.

F022 - KI#225: EventTrigger earliest-era grouping maxed alternative asset/religion requirements as if every option were mandatory - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Sevopedia correctness regression introduced by practical 5700 / db46a59ae5 in `_SAS_getEventTriggerEarliestEra`; later practicals 5702/5703 extended the presentation/chaining but preserved the same max-all-alternatives inference in `_SAS_getEventTriggerEarliestEraAndSource`.
  The old helper correctly handled direct OR-techs with a minimum and direct AND-techs with a maximum, but then called `_bumpIndirect` for every required building class, unit class, religion and corporation, retaining the maximum era across the entire list. Its comment called these constraints all "AND-ish." The engine semantics are different. `CvUnit::getTriggerValue` accepts a unit when its class matches any `UnitsRequired` entry, and `CvPlayer::pickTriggerUnit` needs only one valid unit when `iNumUnits > 0`. Player-level building checks sum counts across listed building classes and compare the total with `iNumBuildings`; religion/corporation checks likewise count qualifying listed alternatives against `iNumReligions` / `iNumCorporations`. City-level trigger checks accept any listed eligible building/religion/corporation once the relevant count gate is met.
  Current XML made the error directly observable. `EVENTTRIGGER_MAN_NAMED_JED` requires one unit and lists Scout (Ancient) plus Explorer (Renaissance); a Scout can trigger it in Ancient, but the old pedia grouping took Explorer's later era and reported Renaissance. `EVENTTRIGGER_BLESSED_SEA` requires one of Paganism/Buddhism/Daoism; Paganism is Ancient while the other two are Classical, so the earliest possible religion gate is Ancient but the old grouping reported Classical. `EVENTTRIGGER_EXPERIENCED_CAPTAIN` requires one Drydock (Medieval) and one unit from a large alternative naval list beginning with Ancient Galley and extending through Modern ships; the actual combined gates can be met in Medieval, while maxing all unit alternatives pushed the old pedia grouping to Modern.
  Fixed in the current implementation batch by aggregating each family according to its threshold semantics: earliest eligible building/unit class, Nth-earliest distinct religion/corporation type at player level, and total-count plus one-listed-type for city triggers. Count requirements without an explicit religion/corporation list now use all corresponding XML types, and types without a technology prerequisite remain valid earliest alternatives. True AND constraints and prerequisite-event chains still use the outer maximum; the separate route-only approximation remains explicit and unchanged.

F021 - KI#224: World Advisor vassal perspective could use the vassal team's hidden map/resource knowledge - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS World Advisor information-visibility regression introduced by practical 5755 / 1fabcaa557 when non-debug vassal perspectives were added to collectors originally introduced in practicals 5712-5714.
  Before practical 5755, the World Advisor's `self.iActiveTeam` was the real active player's team, so BFC/Territory calls such as `pPlot.isRevealed(self.iActiveTeam, False)` and `pPlot.getBonusType(self.iActiveTeam)` naturally used the human observer's knowledge. Practical 5755 allowed the active player to select an alive vassal and set `self.iActiveTeam` from that selected player. BFC1/BFC2/Territory therefore gated plots by the vassal team's reveal state; BFC2/Territory also revealed bonuses according to the vassal team's technologies.
  This was not harmless because master/vassal teams do not share full map sight. `CvPlot::updateSight` explicitly extends a vassal city's sight to its master, but the ordinary owned-plot and unit branches grant sight to the owning/unit team, not automatically to the master. A vassal can therefore have territory/exploration revealed that the active master's team has not revealed. `CvPlot::getBonusType(eTeam)` separately returns a bonus only when that observer team has the reveal technology, so a technologically different vassal could expose a resource the active team could not yet identify. The BFC2/Territory code also read raw `getImprovementType()` / `getRouteType()` after using the vassal reveal filter, which could bypass the active observer's last-known improvement/route state.
  The intended contract is explicit in both the practical-5755 shared-helper comment and the current Main Changes Guide: vassal advisor perspective may expose the vassal's relevant domestic/world/policy state but "does not reveal unrelated players or hidden map data." The World Advisor violated that contract.
  Fixed in commit bc5728f1c by separating subject from observer. `self.iActivePlayer` remains the selected vassal for cities, ownership and state, while outside debug the fog/resource observer team comes from `CyGame().getActivePlayer()`. Reveal and bonus checks use that team, improvements/routes use its revealed-state APIs, and debug mode preserves its reveal bypass and selected-team resource behavior.

F020 - KI#223: Domestic Advisor Founded column sorted formatted BC/AD strings lexically instead of chronologically - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Domestic Advisor UI correctness regression introduced by practical 5722 / 7de906fcdd.
  Practical 5722 enables sorting on the new Overview tables and adds a Founded column whose cell is `CyGameTextMgr().getTimeStr(pLoopCity.getGameTurnFounded(), false)` written with `screen.setTableText`. The same practical explicitly documents in `SASTextScale.setTableIntLabel` that Civ4 sorts `setTableInt` cells numerically while `setTableText` cells sort lexically. Thus clicking Founded orders the human-readable strings, not founding time; e.g. strings spanning BC/AD cannot maintain chronological order.
  Before the fix, SAS still wrote the Founded cell with `setTableText` in `updateTable4`; practical 5748 only moved the column from Overview 3 to Overview 4, and no later historical source commit supplied a numeric sort representation. This is not the mixed-content Info-screen Wonders case that practical 5722 deliberately makes textual: Domestic Founded always represents a real city founding date.
  Fixed in commit ee06f1005 by following the existing World Advisor convention: derive the raw signed founding year through `CyGame().getTurnYear` and render it with the shared numeric-table helper. The column now sorts chronologically; Civ4 exposes no separate numeric sort key that would preserve the localized BC/AD display.

F019 - KI#222: Battle-history capture matcher could not associate current capturable civilians with their preceding battle - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS Military Advisor Battles regression introduced by practical 5765 / 254194766d; survives the current source snapshot.
  Practical 5765 adds a DLL `unitCaptured` event after a captured replacement unit is successfully created, and Python searches newest-first for the battle row to decorate with Cap#/captured-unit data. `_matchesCapturedBattle` requires current turn, capturing player as row winner, old owner as row loser, and crucially `entry[4] == iOldUnitType`. `entry[4]` is the defeated combat unit type; `iOldUnitType` is the non-defending unit that was actually captured.
  Current-SAS XML makes that equality impossible for the standard capture mechanic. The only non-`NONE` `<Capture>` units are `UNIT_SETTLER`, `UNIT_WORKER`, `UNIT_INDIAN_FAST_WORKER`, `UNIT_NETHERLANDS_SWIFT_WORKER` and `UNIT_MAYA_QUICK_WORKER`; all have `iCombat=0` and all capture/convert to `UNITCLASS_WORKER`. `CvUnit::canFight()` returns `baseCombatStr() > 0`, and `canDefend()` immediately rejects units that cannot fight, so none of those five units can be the lethal defender/loser stored in a combatResult row.
  The engine capture path confirms the intended association is plot-based instead. In `CvUnit::setXY`, after the fighting unit enters a plot it iterates existing enemy units; non-defending/capturable units can receive `setCapturingPlayer(getOwner())` and are then killed/converted. The `unitCaptured` event is emitted from that unit's `kill` after `initUnit` creates the replacement. Thus a Worker/Settler capture normally follows the escort-clearing battle on the same plot, but its type is necessarily different from that battle's defeated fighting unit.
  Consequence: under current unit data the Battle-history capture matcher cannot patch the ordinary Worker/Settler captures it was introduced to show, so Cap#/Cap can remain blank even though the capture event itself fires correctly. This also explains why practical 5765 had to chase a separate BUG-event-manager shadowing problem without addressing the deeper association predicate.
  Preferred surgical repair: use `pNewUnit.getX()/getY()` and match the newest same-turn lethal battle row at those coordinates with the same capturing-player/old-owner direction; remove the `entry[4] == iOldUnitType` requirement and reject retreat/non-lethal rows. Practical 5772 already changed attacker-win battle coordinates to the loser's plot, eliminating the pre-advance-coordinate reason that practical 5765 originally gave for avoiding plot equality. Multiple captured civilians remain compatible with `_getEntryWithCapture`: every current capturable source unit converts through `UNITCLASS_WORKER`, so repeated capture events for a cleared plot resolve to the same capturing civilization's Worker unit type and increment the existing count. Fixed in SAS practical 6172 / a659595e27 with the plot-based lethal-row association; Known Issues documentation was added in the same commit.

F018 - KI#221: undefended city conquest could relabel an unrelated same-turn battle as a city capture - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS battle-history regression introduced by practical 5772 / fc82d93007 in `SASBattleHistory.recordCityCaptured`; survives the current source snapshot.
  Practical 5772 patches the just-recorded combat row after `onCityAcquired` so the Military Advisor can distinguish a city that actually fell from a city merely defended in combat. `_matchesCityCapturedBattle` currently requires only `entry[0] == current game turn`, `entry[1] == new owner` and `entry[2] == previous owner`; `_patchCityCapturedEntry` searches newest-first and then rewrites that row's X/Y plus city-context flag.
  The engine does not require a combat to conquer a city. In `CvUnit::setXY`, a fighting unit that moves onto an enemy city plot calls `GET_PLAYER(eNewOwner).acquireCity(pNewCity, true, false, true)` even when the city has no defender; `CvPlayer::acquireCity` then reports `cityAcquired(..., bConquest=true)`. Such an undefended-city conquest therefore reaches `recordCityCaptured` with no new `combatResult` row to patch.
  Concrete corruption case: Player A defeats a Player B unit elsewhere earlier in turn T, then later walks into an undefended B city. The conquest event searches A/B history, finds that earlier same-turn A-won/B-lost battle, and `_setEntryCityContext` overwrites its stored coordinates with the empty city's coordinates and sets `_PLOT_CONTEXT_CITY_CAPTURED`. The Battles tab consequently claims the unrelated earlier fight captured that city. If no earlier same-turn A/B battle exists, nothing is patched, which helps hide the defect.
  Defended captures remain compatible with a stronger match: practical 5772 already corrects attacker-wins battle coordinates to the loser's pre-death plot, i.e. the captured city plot. Preferred surgical repair is therefore to pass `iX/iY` into the matcher and require `entry[5] == iX && entry[6] == iY`, while also rejecting `isRetreatEntry(entry)` / non-lethal rows for defensive robustness. This avoids global pending state and prevents an empty-city conquest from borrowing an unrelated battle. Fixed in SAS practical 6172 / a659595e27 with the coordinate + non-retreat match; Known Issues documentation was added in the same commit.

F017 - KI#220: Military Summary excludes free upgrades from min/average upgrade-cost statistics - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS UI correctness regression introduced by practical 5801 / 1127ae3c5e in `CvMilitaryAdvisor.collectSummaryData`; survives the current snapshot.
  Practical 5801 changes the Summary from a running upgrade-cost total to an `aUpgradeCosts` list used for min/avg/max/total. For each upgradeable unit it finds the cheapest candidate only when `iPrice > 0`, then appends only when `iCheapest > 0`. Current `CvUnit::upgradePrice` can validly return zero: `PROMOTION_LEADER` has `iUpgradeDiscount=100`, so a normal combat unit that has absorbed a Great General can receive a free upgrade. The Summary itself already tracks such GG-led units.
  A zero-price upgrade therefore contributes nothing to the sample instead of contributing a real 0 observation. If the army has one free GG-led upgrade and one ordinary 75-gold cheapest upgrade, the correct cheapest-cost sample is `[0,75]` (Min 0, Avg 37, Max 75, Total 75 under Python-2 integer averaging), but the current code builds `[75]` and reports Min 75 / Avg 75 / Max 75 / Total 75. Total happens to remain correct because adding zero has no effect; an all-free-upgrade sample also happens to show all zeros because the empty-list defaults are zero. Those masking cases help explain why the bug is easy to miss.
  Preferred minimal repair: treat `iPrice >= 0` as a valid candidate and use a sentinel that distinguishes "no candidate" from price 0, then append `iCheapest` whenever a candidate exists. Keep negative only for the internal no-candidate sentinel.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6171 / 83fa938c58 with that sentinel-preserving nonnegative-price repair; Known Issues documentation was added in the same commit.

F016 - KI#219: Military Summary support breakdown bypasses the anarchy zero-cost rule - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS UI correctness regression introduced by practical 5800 / 9e84f1482c in the new Military Advisor Summary / `CyPlayer` breakdown bindings; survives the current snapshot.
  Civ4's public `CvPlayer::calculateUnitCost(int iExtraPop, int iExtraUnits)` and `calculateUnitSupply(int iExtraOutsideUnits)` wrappers return 0 while `isAnarchy()`. Their reference-output overloads intentionally do not contain that wrapper-level exemption; they compute the underlying free/paid counts and normal support subtotals.
  Practical 5800's `CyPlayer::calculateUnitCostBreakdown` and `calculateUnitSupplyBreakdown` call those internal reference-output overloads directly, then Python `collectSummaryData` applies inflation to the returned `iRawUnitCost` / `iRawUnitSupply` and uses those values as the displayed Unit Cost, Unit Supply and Total Cost + Supply. Consequently, opening the Summary during anarchy can show positive support gold even though the engine-facing cost functions explicitly return zero and the player is not charged those costs.
  This is not merely an intermediate-formula difference: the Summary legend describes the rows as the actual DLL formulas and the final line as the bottom-line gold/turn. Search of later SAS diffs found no anarchy special case or replacement of these bindings, and current source retains the direct internal-overload calls.
  Preferred repair direction: preserve useful free/paid counters if desired, but make the Summary's displayed cost components/final totals obey the same anarchy exemption as the public engine-facing cost functions. A Python-side explicit anarchy presentation may be clearer than silently zeroing individual formula operands, because the underlying paid-unit counts remain real while the final charged cost is waived.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6171 / 83fa938c58: the Summary preserves the breakdown counters but zeroes displayed charged support components/totals during anarchy and labels the waived totals accordingly.

F015 - KI#218: PerfectMongoose meteor boundary points pass through an identity set and lose required pairing order - FIXED
  Classification: inherited Base AdvCiv PerfectMongoose regression born upstream at practical 4373 / a61a76dc22, later integrated into SAS ancestry at practical 5851 / 4a2c6394d1; it survived current SAS and the supplied Base AdvCiv 1.14 source until the SAS fix.
  Upstream practical 4373 changes `PangaeaBreaker.getCirclePoints` from an ordered list to `set()` and changes the boundary-point `append(CirclePoint(...))` calls to `add(CirclePoint(...))`, with the stated goal of removing duplicate circle points. The branch-side practical 4381 occurrence has a byte-identical primary-runtime PerfectMongoose patch after diff metadata is stripped, and practical 5851 is the later SAS integration point rather than the absolute birth. `CirclePoint` has no coordinate equality/hash implementation, so separately allocated objects with identical x/y values remain distinct set members. The intended deduplication therefore does not occur.
  `castMeteorUponTheEarth` actively uses this helper during Pangaea breaking. It sorts the returned points only by y and then consumes them two at a time as horizontal crater-line endpoints. The pre-5851 insertion sequence places opposite x endpoints next to each other within each y row; an unordered set discards that ordering while retaining the duplicate identity objects. For radius 4, y=0 is generated as x `+4,-4,+4,-4`; the old stable y-sort produces two full (-4,+4) row pairs, whereas a legal set order `-4,-4,+4,+4` produces two degenerate endpoint lines and leaves the interior of that crater row untouched. The algorithm therefore no longer guarantees the intended rasterized disc.
  The path is live whenever PerfectMongoose is configured to break disallowed Pangaeas: `generatePlotTypes` calls `pb.breakPangaeas`, which calls `castMeteorUponTheEarth` for each strike. Current SAS and Base AdvCiv 1.14 retain the same set plus y-only pairing code.
  Preferred minimal repair: restore list/append ordering for this boundary helper. Do not merely add value equality/hash to `CirclePoint`, because true coordinate deduplication can leave more than two unique midpoint-circle boundary points on a y row while the current rasterizer still blindly pairs consecutive entries. A min/max-x-per-y rasterizer would also be correct but is a broader rewrite.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6171 / 83fa938c58 by restoring the ordered list/append boundary helper while leaving `getFilledCirclePoints` as the separate unique filled-disk helper.

F014 - KI#217: ready-attack upgrade-now count can use the wrong UnitAI for mixed stacks - FIXED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS regression introduced by practical 5948 / a9515bf013 in `CvUnitAI::AI_attackCityMove` / `SAS_getBestUpgradeForLog`; the surgical source/KI repair is staged in the current working tree and awaits user commit validation.
  Practical 5948 added a behavioral bypass for Base AdvCiv upgrade waiting when a ready city-attack stack has a valid non-strong target and `iCanUpgradeNowUnits <= 0`. The commit documentation explicitly describes the condition as "0 units can upgrade now". To compute the group count, it iterates every `pUpgradeLogUnit` but calls `SAS_getBestUpgradeForLog(*pUpgradeLogUnit, AI_getUnitAIType())`; `AI_getUnitAIType()` is the role of the attack-stack unit executing `AI_attackCityMove`, not the iterated member.
  This differs from actual `CvUnitAI::AI_upgrade`, which stores `UnitAITypes const eUnitAI = AI_getUnitAIType()` on the unit being upgraded and evaluates the current/candidate unit values with that role. `CvPlayerAI::AI_unitValue` returns zero immediately for a unit whose XML `NotUnitAI` contains the supplied role, so using the head role is not a harmless scoring approximation.
  Concrete current-SAS proof: `UNIT_SAM_INFANTRY` has default/allowed `UNITAI_COUNTER`, upgrades to `UNIT_MOBILE_SAM`, and both SAM Infantry and Mobile SAM explicitly list `UNITAI_ATTACK_CITY` in `NotUnitAIs`. Current SAS attack-city groups can contain Counter units; later KI#188 diagnostics record 113 `UNITAI_COUNTER` grouping decisions into attack-city expeditions. A Counter SAM Infantry in such a group is therefore evaluated by the practical-5948 helper as Attack City: both current and upgrade values are zero, so no upgrade candidate is reported. The same unit's real `AI_upgrade()` evaluates Mobile SAM as Counter and can upgrade it when Rocketry/gold/other normal requirements are satisfied.
  Consequence: `iCanUpgradeNowUnits` can be understated, including 0 when a mixed-role group member can truly upgrade now. That makes `bSkipUpgradeWaitForReadyAttack` true in a state where practical 5948 intended the stack to keep waiting for a real immediate upgrade, changing attack timing rather than only diagnostics. The defect is role/mixed-stack dependent and does not imply every bypass decision is wrong.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6170 / 642bcd97e0: `SAS_getBestUpgradeForLog` now derives `kUnit.AI().AI_getUnitAIType()` from each iterated member, and both behavior and diagnostic-cost callers use that per-member result. The existing deterministic estimate and separate `canUpgrade(eBestUpgradeForLog)` availability check are otherwise preserved.

F013 - KI#216: Work Boat demand can double-count one accessible water area - FIXED
  Classification: inherited Better BTS AI/K-Mod/Base AdvCiv sea-Worker demand defect; surfaced during the SAS source sweep at practical 5951 / 856ac747bc.
  `CvCityAI::AI_neededSeaWorkers` obtains its first demand area with `waterArea(true)`, which excludes adjacent impassable water, then obtains its second through `secondWaterArea()`. `CvPlot::secondWaterArea()` determines the area to exclude from plain `waterArea()` but rejects impassable adjacent plots while finding its replacement. If plain `waterArea()` selects a larger impassable-Ice-side area A while `waterArea(true)` selects accessible area B, `secondWaterArea()` can also select B because it excludes A. The same accessible area is then passed to `AI_countUnimprovedBonuses` twice.
  Concrete consequence: one currently buildable/reachable seafood target in area B can contribute +1 as the primary and +1 again as the second area, giving `AI_neededSeaWorkers()==2` for one real target. Current SAS availability accounting already deduplicates equal primary/second area pointers, so the false demand can appear as needed 2 versus available 1 and trigger an unnecessary extra Work Boat. Once the real target is improved, demand collapses; the issue is therefore a rare topology-driven overproduction defect rather than persistent global Work Boat inflation.
  The topology is valid in current SAS because Ice is impassable and area calculation distinguishes water connectivity around such tiles. The matching Base AdvCiv 1.14 source supplied during this audit still contains the same demand pairing, and K-Mod practical 3 / 2414225e64 already contains the Better BTS AI ancestry.
  This is distinct from KI#157: that repair deduplicates available primary/second-area Work Boats and fixes availability/target scope, but `AI_neededSeaWorkers` itself still lacks the equality guard.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6170 / 642bcd97e0: preserve both helper semantics and require `pSecondWaterArea != pPrimaryWaterArea` before adding second-area demand, mirroring the already-proven availability guard.

F012 - KI#215: no-site Settler cleanup can unload unrelated transport cargo - FIXED
  Classification: inherited K-Mod/Base AdvCiv cargo-cleanup defect surfaced while auditing SAS practical 6067; earliest available source-bearing boundary is KMod practical 3 / 2414225e64.
  In `CvUnitAI::AI_settleMove`, an AI Settler older than 20 turns with no reachable valid city site first executes `getTransportUnit()->unloadAll()` if carried. `CvUnit::unloadAll` unloads every unloadable passenger on that transport. The existing passenger-side `CvUnit::unload()` instead calls this unit's `canUnload()` and clears only this passenger's transport pointer.
  Practical consequence: at a valid unloading plot, unrelated military/Worker/Settler cargo can be disembarked solely because this Settler is being cleaned up. At sea or another invalid unloading location, `unload()` refuses and the existing `getTransportUnit()==NULL` condition still prevents scrapping, so the safety behavior is preserved. No group-wide unload is required by `setTransportUnit(NULL)` semantics.
  Lineage: exact `getTransportUnit()->unloadAll()` is present in KMod practical 3 and preserved by AdvCiv practical 2268 / d300ec8f97 and practical 1825 / 981e5c8d64; it survives current SAS. SAS practical 6067 did not introduce this line, but its specialized no-site Settler cleanup caused the surrounding inherited path to be re-audited.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6170 / 642bcd97e0: call this Settler's `unload()` instead of the transport's `unloadAll()`, retain the existing `getTransportUnit()==NULL` gate, and document the passenger-local intent.

F011 - KI#214: irrigation-chain constrained search could discard the only route that satisfied the plot limit - FIXED / VALIDATED
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS path-search/state-model defect introduced by SAS practical 6076, SHA 2cc4ccaf5d, in `SAS_findWorkerIrrigationChainStep`.
  The search is Dijkstra-like and ranks nodes by `iCost`, but memoizes only one `aiBestCost[ePlot]` for each plot while also enforcing a separate maximum route length `iMaxPlots` (current default 12). Search cost is not a monotonic proxy for step count: each extra route step after the first costs 350, while overwriting a growth improvement costs 400 per growth level and a generic other improvement costs 2200. A longer detour through empty plots can therefore be cheaper than a shorter route through an existing improvement.
  Concrete counterexample under current defaults: a 2-step route to convergence plot X that crosses one generic improvement costs about 2200 + 350 = 2550; a 7-step empty detour to the same X costs 6 * 350 = 2100. The 7-step state wins `aiBestCost[X]`, so the valid 2-step state is discarded. If an irrigation source lies six additional steps beyond X, the retained cheap route needs 13 plots and is stopped by the 12-plot cap, while the discarded route would reach the source in only 8. The function can therefore return no route even though a legal route exists within `SAS_WORKER_AI_IRRIGATION_CHAIN_MAX_PLOTS`. Equal-cost routes with different step counts can produce the same structural problem because `>= aiBestCost` also discards the alternate state.
  Gameplay impact: the 6076 feature exists specifically to connect dry BFC Farms/food-pressure targets to available irrigation. In a layout where the cheapest detour exceeds the route cap but a more-expensive shorter chain fits, Workers can fail to discover the intended chain and leave a dry Farm unresolved despite an admissible route. The bug is geometric/state-dependent rather than guaranteed every game.
  Fixed in the current validated implementation batch by storing best cost for every bounded `(plot, steps)` state. This preserves least-cost routing subject to the hard step constraint without a heuristic special case. Save file 478 then completed a compiled-DLL Huge Pangaea autoplay through turn 497: Worker level-3 BBAI logging recorded 12,089 selected routes and exercised the exact depth-12 boundary in 344 failed searches without a route invariant failure, assertion or crash; SASGameRecord confirmed the normal Space Race victory endpoint and showed safe 32-bit process-memory headroom.

F010 - KI#213: split city/Worker best-build policies can hide valid custom land jobs from Worker demand - CONFIRMED / BROADER REFACTOR PENDING
  Classification: AdvCiv-SAS regression/partial-fix gap in local Worker-demand accounting.
  Introduction: SAS practical 6093, SHA b3b83cf78f, in `CvCityAI::AI_updateWorkersHaveAndNeeded`.
  The 6093 repair correctly recognized that the legacy city best-build cache is intentionally empty for ordinary SAS land improvements, but its fallback was limited to worked, unimproved, featureless land: `!kPlot.isWater() && !kPlot.isFeature()`.
  Current `CvCityAI::AI_getImprovementValue` exits immediately for every land plot with `NO_BUILD`, while current `CvUnitAI::AI_bestCityBuild` independently handles ordinary land improvements and contains an explicit Flood Plains no-bonus branch. Current XML gives that branch `BUILD_COTTAGE` normally and `BUILD_COTTAGE`/`BUILD_FARM` under low-food pressure. Thus a worked unimproved Flood Plains tile can have a valid SAS Worker build while the legacy cache remains `NO_BUILD`.
  Because Flood Plains are a Feature, the 6093 fallback rejects the tile and increments `iWorkedUnimprovableCount` rather than `iUnimprovedWorkedPlotCount`. The exact fallback survives unchanged at the current snapshot boundary.
  Downstream impact: local `AI_getWorkersNeeded` is understated; area demand, worker city selection/ferrying and the KI#192 completed-secondary-area evacuation logic consume that demand. A secondary area with no other counted work can therefore be considered zero-demand/complete while a worked Flood Plains tile is still improvable. Strong primary-area floors can hide the empire-wide Worker-count symptom but do not correct local assignment semantics.
  Initially considered fixes included an explicit Flood Plains exception and a generic feature-preserving-build test. Both were rejected as additional approximations beside the authoritative Worker policy. Preferred pending architecture: extract the city/plot policy/evaluation portion of `CvUnitAI::AI_bestCityBuild` into shared city/player-side logic; populate/derive `CvCityAI::AI_getBestBuild` from that same policy; then keep Worker-instance concerns (unit `canBuild`, current position/pathfinding, group/mission reservations and execution) in `CvUnitAI`. This should repair KI#213 at the root and reduce future mismatches at other legacy-cache consumers. The refactor is intentionally deferred because it is substantial and deserves dedicated compile/autoplay/log testing rather than being smuggled into the historical sweep. Practical 6093 is therefore certified with a known pending repair.

F003 - KI#206 AI random-event building-change percent normalization - FIXED
  Classification: inherited K-Mod/Base AdvCiv bug
  Discovery route: found by ChatGPT-5.6-Sol in the current-tree XML/raw-cost semantic audit after KI#205, before the systematic historical sweep started.
  Current Base AdvCiv 1.14 still multiplies iBuildingValue by ConstructPercent without the required /100.
  KMod practical 3, SHA 2414225e64 (initial source-bearing K-Mod commit, 27 Dec 2010), already contains the missing normalization in AI_eventValue.
  KMod practical 478, SHA bfc3c3cca7:
    "Added some missing components to the AI's evaluation of event options."
  added /=100 to both analogous unit-event paths but left the building-event path unnormalized.
  Practical 478 is therefore a revealing partial correction, not claimed as the origin of the bug.
  This proves the issue is pre-SAS. The available history does not establish whether the original omission was introduced by K-Mod or inherited from BtS/BBAI before K-Mod practical 3.

F004 - KI#207 UWAI random team-member selection index mismatch - FIXED
  Classification: inherited Base AdvCiv bug
  Discovery route: first previously-unfixed defect found by the systematic newest-to-oldest historical sweep.
  Introduced in AdvCivPreSAS practical 2945, SHA f016996eb6:
    "Let UWAI negotiate with a random team member"
  CvTeam::getRandomMemberAlive generated a 0-based random eligible-member index but compared it with MemberIter::nextIndex(), whose current-element-visible value starts at 1 and whose sequence includes ineligible AI members during human-only selection.
  For three all-AI members the old distribution was 2/3, 1/3, 0 rather than 1/3 each; for two members it was 100%, 0%. Mixed human/AI teams additionally compared a human-only random range against all-member iterator positions.
  The fix keeps the same one synchronized RNG roll and counts eligible members explicitly with a zero-based index.
  AdvCiv practical 3209 later modernized the RNG helper but preserved the faulty comparison. AgentIterator::nextIndex() already had the relevant semantics before practical 2945, so the defect exists from introduction rather than from a later iterator change.
  The sweep had earlier re-encountered the real ExtraFreeOutsideUnits bug at practical 3956, but SAS had already fixed/documented it as KI#92; KI#207 is therefore the systematic sweep's first previously-unfixed surviving defect.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6164, SHA 71cc52033a (the commit-diff archaeology infrastructure was amended into the same commit).

F005 - KI#208 worst-enemy trade-memory recalculation flag overwritten - FIXED
  Classification: inherited Base AdvCiv bug
  Discovery route: second previously-unfixed defect found by the systematic newest-to-oldest historical sweep.
  Introduced in AdvCivPreSAS practical 2833, SHA 98b7a50fac:
    "Minor optimization in update of worst enemy"
  CvTeamAI::AI_updateWorstEnemy tried to avoid an unnecessary second worst-enemy pass by remembering whether trade/grant-memory decay had modified the tentative new worst enemy.
  The loop used bUpdateWorstEnemyAgain = (eOther == eBestTeam), so a later unrelated team with nonzero memory could overwrite an earlier true back to false. The needed recalculation therefore depended on team iteration order and could commit a stale m_eWorstEnemy.
  The fix makes the flag sticky: once eBestTeam is actually modified, later teams cannot clear the required recalculation.
  AdvCiv practical 2998, SHA 8c36049d43 ("Merge branch 0.99 into master"), carried the same branch change into master; no later historical correction was found and the assignment survived into current SAS.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6165, SHA 505a383409.

F006 - KI#209 random-leader reassignment state copied from random-civilization state - FIXED
  Classification: inherited Base AdvCiv bug
  Discovery route: third previously-unfixed defect found by the systematic newest-to-oldest historical sweep.
  Introduced in AdvCivPreSAS practical 2532, SHA 60a15e6434:
    "Handle assignment of random civs and leaders in the DLL"
  CvInitCore::reassignPlayer saved both bRandomCiv and bRandomLeader from m_abCivChosenRandomly, then later restored bRandomLeader into m_abLeaderChosenRandomly. A slot whose civilization and leader random-choice state differed therefore had its saved leader provenance forced to equal its civilization provenance.
  The distinction can matter when choices are independent, notably with GAMEOPTION_LEAD_ANY_CIV; wasLeaderRandomlyChosen and the later setup/re-randomization paths use the leader-specific flag.
  The fix changes the one incorrect read to m_abLeaderChosenRandomly.get(eNewID), preserving both flags independently without changing RNG or assignment policy.
  No later historical correction was found and the exact typo survived into current SAS.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6166, SHA 61c686a4c6.

F007 - KI#210 vote-selection iterator refactor allowed voting-team self-contact - FIXED
  Classification: inherited Base AdvCiv bug
  Discovery route: fourth previously-unfixed defect found by the systematic newest-to-oldest historical sweep.
  Introduced in AdvCivPreSAS practical 2502, SHA cbc25c22bd:
    "Misc. refactoring"
  CvGame::doVoteSelection previously iterated unordered team pairs with iTeam2 = iTeam1 + 1. Practical 2502 replaced the inner numeric loop with a fresh full TeamIter but did not restore the self-exclusion, so a voting team could be paired with itself.
  Because isHasMet(self) starts false, the self-pair can call meet(self). CvTeam::makeHasMet checks eOther == getID() only after setting seen/met state, updating tech share and firing the Python firstContact event, so that late guard does not prevent the bogus self-contact state/event.
  The fix skips equal team IDs directly in doVoteSelection before attempting contact, restoring the lost caller invariant without changing generic meet semantics or foreign-team voting contact.
  No later historical correction was found and the iterator loop survived into current SAS.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6167, SHA fcccb4f5e7.

Resolved audit candidates
-------------------------
S001 - CvUnit::gift / AI_processPeacetimeGrantValue game-speed semantics - NO FIX
  Classification: currently safe under AdvCiv-SAS game-speed data; no SAS defect found.
  Unit gifts feed raw XML unit cost into a common peacetime trade/grant normalization path that divides by ResearchPercent.
  Every current SAS game speed uses the same TrainPercent and ResearchPercent (and also the same ConstructPercent and BuildPercent): 33, 50, 67, 100, 150, 200, 300 and 400 respectively across the eight speeds.
  Therefore the production-vs-research percentage distinction does not create game-speed drift in current SAS. Revisit only if those speed percentages are deliberately decoupled in the future.

S002 - CvPlayerAI::AI_espionageVal mixed production-cost semantics - NO FIX
  Classification: inherited K-Mod rough valuation design; mixed cost APIs alone do not establish a bug.
  KMod practical 161, SHA f9e8a3c4ce, deliberately increased project-destruction valuation while retaining getProductionNeeded(Project).
  KMod practical 960, SHA c4a6ecbc7a, deliberately rewrote destroy-building valuation around raw building cost plus production turns and AI_buildingValue.
  KMod practical 973, SHA 2bbcecb994, refined that formula again and explicitly described AI_espionageVal as a rough commerce-value heuristic; it also replaced the disabled buy-unit mission's relative AI_unitValue component with raw unit production cost.
  The current building formula's important time/utility term already contains production-turn scaling, while projects intentionally use their effective production cost and special strategic multipliers. The buy-unit espionage mission remains disabled.
  Conclusion: the formulas are heterogeneous by design and no current SAS correction is justified merely to make their cost APIs syntactically uniform. Revisit if espionage valuation itself is redesigned, not as an XMLCost bug.

F008 - KI#211 terrain attack sign reversal in unknown-defender AI strength estimates - FIXED
  Classification: inherited K-Mod/Base AdvCiv bug; exact pre-K-Mod authorship unknown.
  Discovery route: fifth previously-unfixed defect found by the systematic newest-to-oldest historical sweep, and the first surviving defect found after entering the K-Mod segment.
  Present in KMod practical 3, SHA 2414225e64 (initial source-bearing K-Mod snapshot, 27 Dec 2010).
  CvUnit::maxCombatStr has a special pAttacker == this mode for estimating an attacker against an unknown defender. City/hills/feature attack modifiers enter iTempModifier and are reversed at the end, but terrain attack was added directly to iModifier and therefore kept the wrong sign.
  Live AI_sumStrength/AI_currEffectiveStr callers reach this mode. GroupPathFinder uses the resulting strength in defended-plot attack-ratio/path costing, AI_cityTargetStrengthByPath uses it for reachable city attack strength, and current SAS low-power attacker ordering also calls the same effective-strength mode.
  Current SAS XML contains 30 unit types with nonzero terrain-attack modifiers. A simple +20% terrain attack case could be estimated as about 83.3% of base rather than the intended 120% before later effective-strength scaling.
  AdvCiv practical 1733, SHA 0d648df6db, touched/preserved the same asymmetric branch. No later historical correction was found before current SAS.
  Fix: change only the terrain branch accumulator from iModifier to iTempModifier. Known-defender semantics stay unchanged; unknown-defender mode now reverses terrain attack together with its sibling attack modifiers.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6168, SHA b4221a1bc7; user compile/run validation succeeded and CI passed. Practical 3 has since been fully certified; the systematic sweep continues at practical 2.


F009 - KI#212 AI-Barbarian CombatDetails attacker/defender field copy-paste - FIXED
  Classification: minor inherited K-Mod/Base AdvCiv semantic/API bug; exact pre-K-Mod authorship unknown.
  Discovery route: sixth previously-unfixed defect found by the systematic historical sweep, during the continuing deep audit of KMod practical 3.
  Present in KMod practical 3, SHA 2414225e64, and still present in current SAS.
  In CvUnit::maxCombatStr, the AI-controlled defender branch for a Barbarian attacker correctly applies the AI-Barbarian strength modifier but stores the reporting value in CombatDetails::iAIBarbarianCombatModifierTB (defender/"This is Barbarian") instead of the corresponding attacker field ...AB. The AB field is separately declared, zeroed, exported to Python and read by CvUtil.py, but was never assigned.
  Current built-in combat logging masks the defect because TB and AB use the same text key/sign handling, and combat strength itself is already computed correctly before the reporting assignment. The definite impact is therefore minor exposed-data/API semantics rather than combat odds or current AI behavior.
  Prepared fix: change only the one CombatDetails write from ...TB to ...AB. No strength arithmetic, RNG, handicap value, text key or balance data changes.
  Fixed in SAS practical 6169 / 56162f50f5. The repair changes only the one CombatDetails write from `...TB` to `...AB`; practical 3 remains fully certified.

    No K-Mod SHA remains. The next planned archaeology stage is the separate newest-to-oldest AdvCiv-SAS history sweep; that later SAS sweep is not included in the 4,343 historical total above.
    Practical 3 is the very large initial source-bearing K-Mod commit (about 8.5 MB of diff across roughly 200 source files) and received its own layered audit rather than being treated like a normal small patch. Audit layers included whole-tree compiler-valid foot-gun searches, K-Mod/karadoc-marked regions, later fix commits used as reverse pointers, and current-SAS descendants of suspicious code.
    Practical 3 audit status: CERTIFIED after five dedicated layered passes. Two previously-unfixed surviving current-SAS defects were established from this commit: KI#211 is fixed/validated/committed; KI#212 has a fix prepared and is awaiting user compile/run/commit validation. Certification means the historical source snapshot itself has now been reviewed to the project evidence standard; KI#212 validation remains a separate current-tree fix step.
    The earlier `iIncome` divisor candidate was re-checked more strongly and closed as safe. Practical 3 computes `iIncome = 1 + GOLD commerce + RESEARCH commerce + max(0, goldPerTurn)`, so the divisor is at least 1 in the introducing code. The modern descendant uses `1 + AI_getAvailableIncome()`; the available-income cache is built from nonnegative available commerce with GPT clamped at zero, and the caller also asserts `iIncome > 0`.
    Whole-tree assignment/condition scans found a genuine historical strategy-hash mutation: `if (iLastStrategyHash &= AI_STRATEGY_DAGGER)` tests with compound assignment and therefore destroys unrelated bits in the saved strategy hash. AdvCiv practical 2179, SHA 3676ec243b, changes this to a non-mutating bit test; current SAS is safe.
    The same scan independently re-found the old crush-strategy test `m_iStrategyHash & !AI_STRATEGY_ALERT2`, which is always false for a nonzero flag and was later fixed by K-Mod practical 76; current SAS is safe.
    Two worker-AI comments in practical 3 describe intended changes that the live code had not actually received: a production-rank comparison remained `<` despite a comment saying it had been changed to `<=` (later fixed by K-Mod practical 220, SHA f5e1f06944), and a danger condition remained `if (bDanger ...)` despite a comment saying it had been changed to `if (!bDanger ...)` (later fixed by practical 103, SHA 5750806e12). Current SAS carries the later corrected behavior.
    Practical 3 also contains a wrong debug assertion bound in `CvGame::getUnitClassCreatedCount`: it checks against `GC.getNumUnitInfos()` rather than the unit-class count. This affects assertion validation rather than the returned array value; the storage/access implementation was later replaced by AdvCiv practical 3746 and current SAS no longer contains that bad assertion.
    A brace-aware audit of all 903 practical-3 assertion/bounds checks found no hidden family of enum-domain mistakes: the `getUnitClassCreatedCount` mismatch above was the only genuine parameter-domain/bound mismatch; earlier broader scanner hits were neighboring compact functions misattributed by the crude scan.
    Confirmed surviving current-SAS defect / KI#211 in `CvUnit::maxCombatStr`: in the special `pAttacker == this` mode used to estimate an attacker against an unknown defender, city, hills and feature attack modifiers are accumulated in `iTempModifier` and then sign-reversed with `iModifier -= iTempModifier`. The terrain-attack branch instead wrote its negative attack modifier directly to `iModifier`, so it uniquely bypassed that reversal. Current `CvSelectionGroupAI::AI_sumStrength` and several current `AI_currEffectiveStr(pPlot, unit)` callers deliberately reach this special mode, including current SAS low-power attacker ordering; `GroupPathFinder` uses `AI_sumStrength` for defended-plot attack-ratio/path cost and `AI_cityTargetStrengthByPath` uses it for reachable city attack strength, so the wrong estimate feeds live AI decisions rather than diagnostics only. Current SAS XML contains 30 unit types with nonzero terrain-attack modifiers, including multiple +20% cases. For a unit with only +20% terrain attack, the special estimate applies -20% instead: roughly 83.3% of base strength rather than the intended 120% before later effective-strength scaling. The exact asymmetric line is present in practical 3, is preserved by AdvCiv practical 1733 and survived current SAS; no correcting historical patch was found. SAS practical 6168, SHA b4221a1bc7, fixes the terrain branch by accumulating it in `iTempModifier`, like the other attack-modifier branches; user compile/run validation succeeded and CI passed.
    Assignment-looking `if (iSpreadExternalValue = 0)` remains a false positive because it exists only in a commented original-BtS reference block; K-Mod's live replacement already uses `== 0`. The other assignment-in-condition form found in the broad scan, `(pWaterArea = ...) != NULL`, is deliberate pointer assignment/testing.
    A further practical-3 culture-layer audit found that `CvCity::setCultureTimes100` had been changed from accepting any changed value to only accepting `iNewValue > iOldValue`, so legitimate culture decreases were silently ignored. K-Mod practical 726, SHA f52e4c54de, explicitly restores the `!=` test and adds handling/comments for negative plot-culture propagation; current SAS carries the corrected behavior.
    The trade-culture Python API was also traced end-to-end. Current `CyCity::getTradeCultureRateTimes100(int iLevel)` still accepts and ignores a destination-culture-level argument, its interface help still describes that argument as meaningful, and current `CvMainInterface.py` still supplies the selected city's culture level. However, practical 3 itself had already deliberately disabled destination-level capping, and current C++ intentionally computes trade culture from the source city only. The stale argument/help contract is therefore a compatibility/API cleanup candidate, but no wrong current gameplay or displayed numeric result is established; do not classify it as a KI on present evidence.
    A focused denominator/arithmetic pass used later K-Mod fixes as reverse pointers. Practical 104, SHA 6d084e7d69, guards a start-of-game `AI_commerceWeight` divisor when the all-team score total can be zero; practical 136, SHA eacd687a2e, guards `AI_knownTechValModifier` when no civilizations have been met. Both are genuine historical divide-by-zero repairs and current SAS contains the corrected logic. Additional scope-aware initialization/division scans in the practical-3 K-Mod-marked code produced only already-fixed or invariant-protected cases in this pass; no further surviving defect was established.
    A further still-live-AI pass found a genuine historical double count in `CvSelectionGroupAI::AI_ejectBestDefender`: practical 3 divided by `100 + cityAttackModifier() + getExtraCityAttackPercent()`, even though `cityAttackModifier()` already includes `getExtraCityAttackPercent()`. AdvCiv practical 2330, SHA 89f68d9b77, explicitly notes the double count and replaces the formula; current SAS keeps the corrected descendant. A systematic scan for sibling aggregate-getter-plus-underlying-extra expressions (city/hills/terrain/feature attack/defense) found no equivalent live duplication in current SAS.
    The same AI-value layer found an unsafe practical-3 Leader-promotion guard: `isHasPromotion(eLeader)` was evaluated before `eLeader != NO_PROMOTION`, so a ruleset with no Leader promotion could query `isHasPromotion(NO_PROMOTION)`. K-Mod practical 199, SHA a865535e85, rewrites this into a direct loop over actual Leader promotions; current SAS uses the rewritten form. This is historical-only.
    The practical-3 global-warming/Python exposure layer was checked separately for wrong getter/member wiring. The GW index/chance/tally, land-defence/sustainability and player-pollution bindings map to the intended C++ functions, and current descendants remain consistent; no second serialization/API wiring defect was found there.
    A reverse-history scan then used later K-Mod commits explicitly labelled fix/bug/crash/OOS/etc. as pointers back into practical 3. Raw exact-line matching is intentionally treated only as a candidate generator because generic statements can be removed by a rewrite and later reappear safely. The first pass produced 852 noisy textual coincidences; requiring a nontrivial removed line to occur exactly once in the same file in both practical 3 and current SAS reduced this to 33 contextual candidates. All 33 were then reviewed in their actual functions. The apparent survivors resolved as corrected surrounding guards/rewrites, commented historical reference code or safe caller invariants: e.g. the Pitboss `AddLaunch` call is behind `IsGraphicsInitialized()`, collateral arithmetic is inside K-Mod's deterministic vector rewrite, K-Mod 623's old cargo-defender formula survives only as commented reference, K-Mod 870's `getCenterUnit()` survives only inside the replacement `getDebugCenterUnit()` helper, and the transport `splitGroup` path can return NULL only for non-positive split size while its caller has already established positive cargo space. No reintroduced defect was confirmed from the completed reverse-fix queue.
    Practical 3 is certified. Practical 2 and practical 1 were then separately verified as empty ancestry/SVN-import scaffolding from their full logs, empty generated diff summaries/coverage and the fact that practical 3 introduces the full source tree as new files. The K-Mod sweep is complete.

K-Mod systematic sweep completion
---------------------------------
All 1,249 archived K-Mod SHAs have now been systematically certified, from practical 1249 through the root practical 1. The sweep found two previously-unfixed surviving defects in the giant practical-3 source snapshot (KI#211 and minor KI#212) in addition to re-proving many genuine historical bugs already corrected by later K-Mod/AdvCiv. KI#211 is fixed/validated/committed; KI#212's fix remains pending user compile/run/commit validation at the time of this checkpoint. That pending current-tree validation does not change completion of the historical K-Mod review.

KMod notable checked candidates / historical notes
---------------------------------------------------
These checks are preserved so later review does not rediscover the same candidates from scratch. Some were first investigated out of order while orienting in K-Mod history and are now also inside the certified contiguous range through practical 3.

KMod practical 1158 - AI_retreatToCity rewrite ancestry - ALREADY COVERED
  The retreat rewrite intersects the unsafe-current-city retreat behavior eventually fixed/documented under KI#204. Treat as known lineage, not a new systematic finding.

KMod practical 1099 - OOS checksum player-index shift - KI#326 / MINOR DIAGNOSTIC CLEANUP IMPLEMENTED
  SAS can iterate 48 civilization-player slots while K-Mod's OOS attitude-cache checksum shifted signed attitude values by the player index. Negative signed operands were questionable already, and indices >= 32 exceeded the width of a 32-bit int; this was only a deliberately lossy diagnostic checksum, not simulation/game state. KI#326 documents it explicitly while retaining the diagnostic-only, no-gameplay-defect classification outside the confirmed-defect counters. Replaced the shifts with position-sensitive unsigned polynomial mixing so all rival slots contribute safely to the diagnostic value.

KMod practical 861 - AI_vassalTrade exclusion rewrite - HISTORICAL BUG, CURRENTLY SAFE
  A rewrite changed the old exclusion of self OR prospective master into an && form that did not preserve the original logic in normal cases. This was a genuine historical bug in that revision, but later AdvCiv refactored the path into relation-filtered enemy iterators and current SAS no longer contains the bad condition.

KMod practical 643 - Great-Person specialist ternary precedence - HISTORICAL BUG, CURRENTLY SAFE
  The expression around getGreatPeopleRate() used a ternary without the necessary parentheses and therefore parsed differently from the apparent intent. KMod practical 876 later corrected the exact expression; current SAS is safe.

KMod practical 3 - initial source snapshot audit - CERTIFIED / KI#211 FIXED / KI#212 FIX PREPARED
  This giant source-bearing commit received five dedicated layered audit passes and is now certified. The current-descendant `iIncome` divisor candidate has been re-proved safe so far because the introducing calculation and modern available-income path keep the denominator positive. Whole-tree condition scans found the genuine `iLastStrategyHash &= AI_STRATEGY_DAGGER` mutation bug, later fixed by AdvCiv practical 2179, and independently re-found the already-fixed crush-strategy `& !AI_STRATEGY_ALERT2` bug. Two worker-AI comment/code mismatches were also genuine historical defects later fixed by K-Mod practical 220 (`<` -> `<=`) and practical 103 (`bDanger` -> `!bDanger`). A brace-aware audit of 903 assertion/bounds checks confirmed that the unit-class-created-count debug assertion was the only real enum-domain/bound mismatch; it was later removed with the storage/access refactor.
  A later reverse-pointer check found another genuine practical-3 maintenance bug: `CvCity::calculateMaintenanceDistance` updated its longest-distance term only for non-government-center cities. With exactly two cities, the non-capital city therefore saw only itself in that term and could get zero distance maintenance. K-Mod practical 1005, SHA 105c7a8c6e, explicitly fixes this by updating the longest-distance term for every city before the government-center test; current SAS retains the correction.
  Practical 3's Advanced Start tech-value loop also contains a historical variable-target mistake: after computing a local improvement-yield `iTempValue`, the early-era Advanced Start bonus multiplies cumulative `iValue` instead of `iTempValue`. This can repeatedly scale value accumulated from unrelated earlier terms. Later K-Mod tech-value rewrites remove the old branch (it is gone by practical 84, SHA ab84668b30); current SAS no longer contains it.
  The practical-3 global-warming state additions `m_iGlobalWarmingIndex` and `m_iGwEventTally` were separately checked for reset/read/write symmetry. Both are initialized and serialized in matching order, so no save/OOS defect was found in that state plumbing.
  A later culture-layer pass found a genuine practical-3 regression in `CvCity::setCultureTimes100`: the setter accepted only increases (`iNewValue > iOldValue`), so culture decreases were ignored. K-Mod practical 726, SHA f52e4c54de, restores the normal `!=` changed-value condition and handles negative plot-culture propagation; current SAS is safe.
  The surviving Python trade-culture wrapper/help contract was checked separately. `CyCity::getTradeCultureRateTimes100(int iLevel)` still accepts an ignored destination-level parameter and current `CvMainInterface.py` still passes one, but practical 3 had already intentionally removed destination-level capping and current C++ returns the source-city trade-culture rate used by gameplay. Treat this as stale compatibility/help surface, not a current numerical behavior bug on present evidence.
  Reverse-pointer arithmetic checks also re-verified two early K-Mod divide-by-zero repairs: practical 104 guards a zero all-team score denominator in `AI_commerceWeight`, and practical 136 guards `AI_knownTechValModifier` when no civilizations are met. Current SAS retains the corrected forms. Broader scope-aware initialization/denominator scans in this pass produced no additional surviving defect.
  A subsequent still-live-AI pass found that practical 3's `AI_ejectBestDefender` divided by `100 + cityAttackModifier() + getExtraCityAttackPercent()` even though the aggregate getter already contains the extra promotion component. AdvCiv practical 2330, SHA 89f68d9b77, explicitly calls out and replaces that double count; current SAS is safe, and a broader scan found no live sibling aggregate-plus-extra duplication. Practical 3 also evaluated `isHasPromotion(eLeader)` before checking `eLeader != NO_PROMOTION`; K-Mod practical 199, SHA a865535e85, rewrites promotion handling around actual Leader promotions, so the no-Leader-promotion hazard is historical-only.
  The GW/Python binding layer was checked for wrong getter/member wiring without finding another live defect. The later reverse-history pass reduced 852 raw fix-line coincidences to 33 unique same-file candidates and completed contextual review of every one; none represented a reintroduced current defect. A final independent sibling/asymmetry pass also checked surviving CombatDetails exposure, persistent-state serialization and NO_* guard ordering. `iDomainDefenseModifier` is a longstanding zero/dead compatibility field with no corresponding live domain-defense calculation rather than a missing current assignment; the apparent surviving unsafe Leader-promotion guard is inside commented BtS reference code while the live K-Mod loop is corrected. Core practical-3 read/write member sets are symmetric for CvGame, CvCity, CvTeam, CvArea, CvSelectionGroupAI and CvPlayerAI; the apparent CvPlayer/CvUnit/CvPlot exceptions are node data, a derived unit-info pointer and a nonserialized danger cache rather than state loss. No third surviving defect was established. Practical 3 is therefore certified; continue at practical 2.
  Confirmed minor surviving current-SAS defect / KI#212: when the attacker is Barbarian and the defender is AI, `CvUnit::maxCombatStr` stores the correct AI-Barbarian strength modifier in `CombatDetails::iAIBarbarianCombatModifierTB` instead of the corresponding attacker-Barbarian `...AB` field. The same copy-paste is already present in practical 3; current `...AB` is otherwise zeroed, exported to Python and read by `CvUtil.py` but never assigned. AdvCiv's `CombatDetails` comment identifies TB as defender/"This is Barbarian" and AB as "Attacker is Barbarian". Current SAS combat logging masks the error because both fields use the same text key/sign handling, and the actual strength math is already correct, so this is explicitly classified as a minor semantic/API defect rather than gameplay AI weakness. The prepared repair changes only the reporting write from `...TB` to `...AB`; user compile/run/commit validation is pending.
  Confirmed surviving current-SAS defect / KI#211: `CvUnit::maxCombatStr`'s unknown-defender attacker mode reverses city/hills/feature attack modifiers through `iTempModifier`, but the terrain-attack branch added the negative modifier directly to `iModifier` and therefore bypassed the reversal. Live `AI_currEffectiveStr(..., sameUnit, ...)`/`AI_sumStrength` callers reach this mode; `GroupPathFinder` uses that sum in defended-plot path costing and city-target strength logic uses it for reachable attack power. Current SAS has 30 terrain-attack-bearing unit types. The asymmetric line is present in practical 3, preserved by AdvCiv practical 1733 and survived current SAS; no later correction was found. SAS practical 6168, SHA b4221a1bc7, fixes the terrain branch by accumulating it in `iTempModifier` like its city/hills/feature siblings, preserving known-defender behavior while allowing unknown-defender mode to reverse it. User compile/run validation succeeded and CI passed. Practical 3 is now certified; the systematic sweep continues at practical 2.

KMod practical 3 - corporation external-spread assignment-looking condition - FALSE POSITIVE
  The alarming `if (iSpreadExternalValue = 0)` is preserved only inside a commented original-BtS reference block. K-Mod's live Unofficial Patch replacement already uses `== 0`; practical 3 therefore documents/fixes the inherited BtS issue rather than introducing a live K-Mod bug.

KMod practical 9 - ROUND_DIVIDE sign test - HISTORICAL BUG, CURRENTLY SAFE
  Practical 9 replaced the multiplication-based sign test with `a / b > 0` to avoid overflow, but integer truncation makes that test false for same-sign proper fractions such as 2/3. AdvCiv practical 1968, SHA b602f31c7d (`Bugfix in K-Mod's ROUND_DIVIDE`), replaces it with the corrected XOR-sign test; current SAS is safe.

KMod practical 7 - global-warming terrain selector initialization - HISTORICAL BUG, CURRENTLY SAFE
  The terrain selector could remain uninitialized after exhausting its random-search attempts. Practical 9 fixes the path; current SAS is safe.

KMod practical 13 - global-warming max-turn division - HISTORICAL BUG, CURRENTLY SAFE
  Chance scaling divided by getMaxTurns(), allowing a zero-divisor path with Time Victory disabled. Practical 20 fixes the case; current SAS is safe.

KMod practical 23 - global-warming responsibility squaring - HISTORICAL BUG, CURRENTLY SAFE
  A potentially negative responsibility factor was squared, which could turn a net-negative contribution positive. Practical 35 later replaces/clamps the transformation; current SAS is safe.

KMod practical 4 - Religion Advisor subset drawing - HISTORICAL BUG, CURRENTLY SAFE
  The practical-4 BUG Religion Advisor customization could populate `self.RELIGIONS` with a selected subset but still drew religion buttons by iterating the full `range(gc.getNumReligionInfos())`. K-Mod practical 777 explicitly fixes the subset options by iterating `self.RELIGIONS`; current SAS no longer contains that old screen implementation.

Current XML gives Bomber (`iAirCombat=16`) and Stealth Bomber (`iAirCombat=20`) `iCombat=0` and `iCollateralDamage=100`; both therefore fail `canFight()` despite having functional collateral. The air-strike path calls `collateralCombat`, which explicitly uses `airBaseCombatStr()` for `DOMAIN_AIR`, proving this is real combat behavior rather than dead XML. Base AdvCiv 1.14's instance-help path still displays collateral whenever `pUnit->collateralDamage() > 0`, and the SAS basic/Sevopedia help branch added in the same 5506 commit correctly treats `u.getCombat() > 0 || u.getAirCombat() > 0` as combat-capable. Result: an actual Bomber/Stealth Bomber unit hover could omit its real collateral-damage/limit/max-units line while its type/Sevopedia help showed it.
  Surgical direction for KI#235: keep the civilian suppression but make the instance gate match the basic-unit semantics, e.g. `(pUnit->canFight() || pUnit->airBaseCombatStr() > 0)` (equivalently base-combat-or-air-combat), rather than broad `canCombat()` because `canCombat()` also admits nukes while the basic-unit gate intentionally requires land/base or air combat strength. No gameplay calculation needed changing. Implemented and runtime-validated later in commit 0d10b3650; see F032 and KI#235.
  No second surviving defect was established in this range. Certified cursor advances through practical 5544 / f1e860eb99. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5541 / c9424a447d.

Practical 5539 similarly scales Specialist page labels/help; the subsequent practical 5540 merely centralizes the same embedded-font stripping into `SASTextScale.normalizeBodyText`, preserving the strip-then-wrap behavior used by Specialist, Promotion and History.
  Practical 5538 includes one genuine Base AdvCiv Sevopedia correctness repair rather than only font work: `placeLeadsTo` previously checked `getPrereqPromotion`, `getPrereqOrPromotion1` and `getPrereqOrPromotion2` but omitted K-Mod's exposed `getPrereqOrPromotion3`, even though the same Promotion page already displays that third OR prerequisite on the selected promotion's Requires side. Adding the fourth comparison makes reverse dependency display symmetric with the supported info model. Current SAS XML does not appear to populate a third OR prerequisite, so the repair is presently future/mod-data correctness rather than a visible current-chain change. The new None fallback is shown only when no dependent promotion is found.
  The rest of practical 5538 is presentation/helper cleanup: Promotion special-help text is stripped of embedded font tags then rewrapped at the configured Body size, unit-combat rows preserve their IDs/buttons, and duplicate inline None rendering is replaced by the shared no-content helper. No promotion eligibility/gameplay rule is changed.
  Practicals 5537 and 5536 establish/refactor `SASTextScale`: `applyFontTag` preserves None, empty strings and already-font-tagged text; the Info/Victory adapters are equivalent to their prior local wrappers apart from harmlessly normalizing string-like input through `unicode()`. Helper imports are introduced before later Sevopedia callers use them, and the 5540 normalization helpers are an exact centralization of regex logic already used by those pages. No import-order, widget, sorting or state regression was established.

SAS-C078 - practical 5509 through 5503 initial shared UI-font implementation audit - COMPLETE; KI#237 FOUND (FIX PREPARED LATER)
  Reviewed 7 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5509 / d613a8b096, 5508 / 4c3506bc39, 5507 / 1ef354763e, 5506 / 4dc561f908, 5505 / 51539c04da, 5504 / 008f512278 and 5503 / 3f762655a2.
  Practical 5503 introduces `SASFontUtils` and replaces hardcoded 1/2/3/4 UI font tags across Domestic/Customizable Domestic, Espionage, Foreign, Main Interface, TechChooser and Economics/Environment screens. The dynamic tag objects themselves do not read XML defines at import/construction time: `_getFontSizeOrDefault` is called lazily only when a tag is rendered/concatenated. Thus early Python module import cannot permanently freeze unavailable GlobalDefines. Practical 5504 adds a cache only after a value passes the 1..4 validity check, so an unavailable/invalid early lookup is not cached. Later certified practical 5744 intentionally changes this permissive fallback contract to fail-fast configuration validation.
  At their native/default sizes the practical-5503 screen replacements are mechanically equivalent to the removed hardcoded tags; widget IDs/data, gameplay getters and table cell APIs remain unchanged. The initial XML deliberately sets Tiny/Body/Label/Title all to 4 as an aggressive upscaling experiment; practical 5517 later restores the native 1/2/3 hierarchy while keeping Title 4, and the numerous later font/layout lineages already certified from the newer side repair the resulting presentation rough edges rather than leaving a current correctness survivor.
