  KI#237 candidate (inherited/pre-SAS, discovered while auditing practical 5505): `GPUtil.getGreatPeopleText` Maximum-types truncation is one candidate late. In each loop iteration it computes `szNewTypes = szTypes + next GP icon/percentage` but builds/measures `szNewText` from the old `szTypes`; only after that old string passes the width test does it assign `szTypes = szNewTypes`. Therefore an item that is itself the first one to push the rendered list over `iGPBarWidth - 10` is accepted and returned. The bug is already present in the earliest available K-Mod practical 4 BUG import (`szNewText ... % szTypes`) and Base AdvCiv 1.14 retains it. The frozen source audited here retained the same control flow. The BUG Main Interface option defaults GP Bar Types to `Maximum`, so this path is ordinary/default UI behavior; mixed-GP-point cities can supply multiple entries, and the top-bar call also includes the variable city name. Preferred surgical repair: measure the candidate (`szNewTypes`) before accepting it, preserving the existing sorted order and stop-on-first-nonfit policy. Any font-scale-aware measurement improvement can be considered separately, but is not required to prove/fix the off-by-one acceptance bug.
  Fixed later in the current implementation batch by applying that surgical repair; see F034 and KI#237.
  No additional surviving defect was established in 5509 -> 5503. Certified cursor advances through practical 5503 / 3f762655a2. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5502 / 4a0682c481.


SAS-C079 - practical 5502 Sevopedia Leader attitude/action preview audit - COMPLETE
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5502 / 4a0682c481. Practical 5501 / 886d380631 changes docs/generated history only and is skipped by the runtime-source denominator.
  Practical 5502 imports clickable Leader attitude previews (Furious/Annoyed/Cautious/Pleased/Friendly) and four animation actions (idle/no-animation, greeting, agree, disagree) into the Sevopedia Leader page. The action constants are valid engine `LeaderheadAction` values; `NO_LEADERANIM` is deliberately -1 and the enum comment states that passing it to leaderhead action playback produces the idle animation. The five attitude values are likewise the standard contiguous attitude enum members.
  Button routing is coherent. The page-specific `handleInput` handles WIDGET_PYTHON events when the event carries the Leader page's Python-file id; `SevoPediaMain.handleInput` provides the separate fallback path only when that page-specific forwarding did not occur, so a single click is not executed twice. Attitude input validates `iData2` against the five-element preview order before changing local preview state; action buttons are created only from the fixed four-value tuple. The original numeric WIDGET_PYTHON ids 6805/6806 have no conflicting Python runtime use and later certified code centralizes them as `SAS_MAGIC_PEDIA_PYTHON_LEADER_ATTITUDE/ACTION`.
  `applyLeaderAttitude` only changes `self.iSelectedAttitude`, recreates the 3D leaderhead widget and redraws the attitude controls. It does not touch `CvPlayer` diplomacy/attitude data. Keeping the selected preview attitude while browsing another leader is therefore persistent cosmetic preview state rather than leaked gameplay state. `applyLeaderAction` only asks the screen to play an animation on the current leader widget.
  The new attitude/action panel uses fixed widget IDs rather than `getNextWidgetName`; the same commit compensates by explicitly deleting those fixed widgets from `SevoPediaMain.deleteAllWidgets`, and `placeAttitudes` also deletes/recreates them before redraw. Current descendants preserve that cleanup and extend it to the later era-art buttons. The renamed Leader legend concept/link keeps the same pedia-description widget semantics while broadening the legend's content.

SAS-C080 - practical 5500 release-boundary runtime audit - COMPLETE

  The Main Interface production-text indentation change is a historical correctness repair. Before 5500 the `if not bProdDetailAdded` fallback sat outside the non-process production branch even though `bProdDetailAdded`, `iProdCur` and `iProdNeed` are initialized only in that branch. 5500 moves the fallback inward beside those variables; current SAS preserves that placement.   The Info Score-tab changes are coherent with the immediately older practical-5493 PowT introduction. Header rows use `WIDGET_GENERAL` and remain excluded from selectable-arrow navigation.

The define is restart-stable like the surrounding Sevopedia configuration.
  Civilization Select/Order audio classification changes from unconditional 3D to `szScript.startswith("AS3D_")`. Every current civilization Select/Order script is AS3D (72 / 72 XML entries), so current behavior is unchanged while a future AS2D script can take the correct 2D path. The Media Player consumes the stored boolean specifically to choose the 2D-vs-3D sound API.
  The small non-source XML hunks were also sanity-checked where they interact with runtime code: the Mehmed audio filename rename matches the renamed asset; Civilization/Leader XML changes are authorship headers; the new Sevopedia defines have valid enabled defaults; and the Paper/Professional Army flavor edits are intentional AI-preference tuning rather than structural prerequisite/data-schema changes.

SAS-C081 - practical 5498 through 5496 Sevopedia-music / BUG-startup causal lineage - COMPLETE
  Reviewed 3 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5498 / a3cf39a3a4, 5497 / b97bbac6e1 and 5496 / 6f94679507. Practicals 5495 and 5494 have no runtime C++/Python change and are skipped by the primary denominator.
  This sequence is best understood as one diagnosis/revert/root-cause lineage rather than three independent fixes. Practical 5496 reacts to intermittent startup traces by adding a `BugPath.initModFolder` `__file__` fallback and by changing TechChooser's direct `WidgetTypes.WIDGET_TECH_CHOOSER_ERA` access to `getattr(..., WIDGET_GENERAL)`. Practical 5497 deliberately reverts both symptom workarounds after identifying the newer Sevopedia music XML resolver's early `BugPath` calls as the causal startup-order disturbance; practical 5498 then repairs Sevopedia's own mod-XML resolution without touching BUG path state early.
  The TechChooser revert is safe in the normal SAS startup contract. `WIDGET_TECH_CHOOSER_ERA` is not a compiled `CyEnumsInterface` enum; BUG declares it in `Assets/Config/BUG Advisors.xml`, and `WidgetUtil.createWidget` dynamically assigns the new value onto `WidgetTypes` while BUG config loads. Thus the observed missing attribute is consistent with BUG initialization having failed before widget registration. Once BUG config loads normally, the direct historical/current access is valid.
  The premature-BugPath failure mechanism is concrete in current BUG code: `findAssetFile()` calls `initSearchPaths()`, which builds the mod/BtS asset search list and then permanently sets `_searchPathsInitDone = True`. If Sevopedia invokes those helpers before `initModFolder()` can resolve the mod directory, the search list can be initialized without the mod Assets path and stay frozen for later `Config/init.xml` lookup. Practical 5497 removes the unnecessary `BugPath.getModDir/getAppDir/findAssetFile` calls from `_SAS_findAssetXmlPath`, eliminating that early side effect.
  Practical 5498 replaces the earlier `__file__`-relative Sevopedia candidate with a BUG-independent mod candidate derived from `os.getcwd()`, `CvModName.modName` (`AdvCiv-SAS`) and `Mods/<mod>/Assets/...`, while retaining the relative `Assets/...` fallback. The helper validates candidates with `os.path.isfile`, prefers the mod Audio2DScripts copy containing `AS2D_OPENING_MENU_01`, and returns the first existing candidate otherwise. Current SAS preserves this resolver.
  The audio/XML removal in practical 5497 is outside the runtime-source denominator but was checked for causal consistency: it changes music-shuffle content/timing while the actual surviving fix remains the resolver/startup-order correction, matching the later KI#110 documentation. No current startup or TechChooser survivor was established from the 5496 workaround reverts.
  No new surviving defect was established in practicals 5498 -> 5496. Certified cursor advances through practical 5496 / 6f94679507. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5493 / 08396a2b15 (5495/5494 skipped as non-runtime-source).

  Practical 5493 is the first implementation of several Score-tab signals that practical 5500 later hardens. The new independent tests append `|ID` master prefixes and `,ID` vassal prefixes, allowing both roles to coexist in one cell; player membership still comes only from the already-visible-player-by-team map.

The optional max-render threshold therefore counts the same alive/team-score/player-score-filtered entries that the table actually shows. When reached, the table expands symmetrically vertically and to screen margins, reserves a scrollbar-width budget, and moves the optional Legend link; otherwise legacy bounds remain. SAS-C083 - practical 5486 SAS map-script batch / Parallel Lines starting-slot audit - COMPLETE; KI#238 FOUND / FIXED AND VALIDATED LATER
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5486 / 0870e0adc8. Practicals 5491 through 5487 were already skipped as non-runtime-source by C082.
  `SAS_Parallel_Lines.py` is new in this commit. Its five custom-map-option definitions are internally index-consistent: Players Per Line has 2 values/default 0, Height Per Player 9/default 3, Inter-Line Bands 2/default 0, Line Thickness 5/default 2, and Revolving World 4/default 0. The selected line thickness is at least 3 plots, so the center-column custom start is not removed by the script's edge-only waviness trims. Plot-type arrays use the generated map dimensions consistently, and the line/gap hard cap cannot collapse below a one-plot water gap.
  Confirmed KI#238: `_line_count` and `_build_layout.line_slots` size the custom starting-slot topology from the world's XML default-player count, but `findStartingPlot` indexes it with the actual alive-player list. The loop has no out-of-capacity guard; after exhausting all slots it retains the final line/final position, so every player beyond the world-default capacity reuses the last custom starting plot. Current SAS practical 6182 retains this exact control flow. The DLL accepts a non-null Python start immediately and `setStartingPlot` does not deduplicate it, so no later engine pass repairs the duplicate. World default players are only a recommendation/tuning value (`CvGame::getRecommendedPlayers`), while actual alive players come from independent taken/computer setup slots; configurations above the world default are therefore legitimate.
  Preferred surgical repair is to mirror the sibling-map contract: if `iIndex` is outside the total `line_slots` capacity, call `CyPythonMgr().allowDefaultImpl()` and return so the DLL's ordinary taken-start-aware search handles the excess player. This preserves custom topology for supported slots without pretending the map has a bespoke extra slot.
  The rest of practical 5486 closes without another surviving runtime-source defect. Boreal's `isAdvancedMap` return 1 is an explicit setup-list visibility decision. Large Facing Islands' new Revolving World option is complete across option count/name/value/description/default/random handling and `getWrapX`. Longworld and Spiky Avenues' new option arrays/defaults are bounded and their grid/layout consumers use the same option helpers. The old `from SAS_WorldSizes import *` helper also exposed map-script callback names, but Longworld, Spiky Avenues and Parallel Lines each define their own `getDescription` and `getGridSize`, overriding those helper callbacks locally; the broader callback-leak bug affected scripts lacking their own override and was repaired later at practical 5897. Longworld/world-size enum-key problems and related SAS map-profile cleanup are later historical repair lineage at 5854/5855, already certified from the newer side, not current survivors from this checkpoint.
  The mixed non-source additions were sanity-checked where they directly interact with these scripts: the Parallel Lines WorldPicker entry has one Random size plus all 11 current world-size entries, and adding the map name to the land-heavy override is configuration/tuning rather than executable Python semantics and is not used to certify this runtime-source SHA.
  Certified cursor advances through practical 5486 / 0870e0adc8. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5485 / 34d4d7d392.


SAS-C084 - practical 5485 opening-menu shuffle-bag / Sevopedia audio-path audit - COMPLETE
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5485 / 34d4d7d392.
  `CvMainMenuInfo::getSoundtrack` introduces a process-local shuffle bag only for the configured trigger soundtrack. Disabled mode and non-trigger menu profiles return the XML soundtrack unchanged. Empty/`NONE` pool entries are skipped, the trigger script is inserted as a nonempty fallback if the configured pool is empty, the remaining bag is refilled only after exhaustion, and `ASyncRand` selects from a guaranteed nonempty vector before erasing exactly the chosen entry. Current source retains this implementation; the current configured pool is smaller than at introduction but remains valid. No out-of-range/empty-bag or synced-game RNG mutation was established.

Those calls could initialize BUG's asset search path before mod-directory discovery was ready, producing the later KI#110 startup/TechChooser symptoms. This is not a current survivor: practical 5497 removes the premature `BugPath` interaction and practical 5498 replaces it with the BUG-independent mod-path resolver; that causal repair lineage was already certified in C081.
  Opening-track grouping itself is coherent: classifying an entry as Opening when either `SoundID` starts `SONG_OPENING` or `ScriptID` starts `AS2D_OPENING_MENU` lets custom opening scripts remain in the Opening group even when their sound IDs vary. Parse counters/debug prints are disabled by default and do not alter grouping state. Current descendants retain the intended classification while using the repaired resolver.

SAS-C085 - practical 5484 dominated/equivalent strategic-resource trade audit - COMPLETE; KI#239 + KI#240 FOUND / FIXED AND VALIDATED LATER
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5484 / 923840f152.
  The commit adds an XML-toggleable early `AI_bonusTrade` exclusion for selected Copper/Iron/Horse/Camel/Elephants combinations and era ranges. The configured bonus and era lookups resolve to live current entries; `iChange >= 0` intentionally covers both proposed buys and cancellation checks, and the recipient/buyer is correctly represented by `kPlayer = GET_PLAYER(eToPlayer)`. The individual Classical Horse/Camel/metal substitute rules and later Horse/Camel obsolescence rules are explicit design heuristics; no enum-order, invalid-info-type or arithmetic defect was established in those branches merely from their balance approximations.
  Confirmed KI#239: the hard exclusion is not scoped to an AI recipient. An AI seller therefore applies the AI-buy optimization to a human buyer before reaching AdvCiv's existing human-recipient carve-out. Resource `DENIAL_JOKING` is explicitly filtered from human trade inventories by `CvPlayer::buildTradeTable`, and `AI_checkCancel` also reaches the gate with `iChange == 0`; this can hide first-copy strategic resources from a human or cancel an existing AI-to-human export solely because an AI purchasing heuristic says the resource is dominated. The commit/docs consistently describe AI buying, so this is unintended scope leakage rather than a documented player restriction. Preferred surgical repair: require `!bPlayerHuman` for the hard dominated-buy gate.
  Confirmed KI#240: the unconditional Medieval+ Copper rule is not semantically valid even for AI buyers. Base AdvCiv 1.14 and current SAS both let the generic Privateer use Copper OR Iron; current SAS places it at Medieval `TECH_PROFESSIONAL_ARMY`, so a Medieval AI without Iron can still gain a live strategic unit from Copper. Corporation 4 and Corporation 5 also consume Copper, while `AI_corporationBonusVal` and the pre-existing duplicate-resource filter explicitly preserve corporation-useful copies. The 5484 early return bypasses both ordinary bonus valuation and that corporation safeguard. Preferred repair: remove era-alone Copper domination and let Copper reach ordinary value logic when Iron is absent, and skip the hard dominated gate when recipient corporation value is positive.
  Both fixes were implemented and validated later in commit d2d788086; see F036/F037 and KI#239/KI#240.
  No third surviving defect was established in practical 5484 after checking the buyer/seller direction, new-trade and cancellation call sites, trade-table filtering, configured strategic/era identifiers, current unit prerequisites and corporation valuation path. The HRE Houfnice is an additional Copper-or-Iron use, but its special-case omission had already been documented elsewhere as a deliberate simplification; it is not counted separately from the generic Privateer/corporation proof above.

SAS-C093 - practical 5460 SAS_Large_Facing_Islands birth audit - COMPLETE; KI#242 FOUND / FIXED AND VALIDATED LATER
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5460 / d185fc9208.
  Practical 5460 introduces `SAS_Large_Facing_Islands.py`, a fixed-island-footprint map whose description and start-placement design explicitly promise that each player starts on a large island. The map creates one custom starting slot per generated island and safely calls the DLL default implementation when the actual alive-player index exceeds that slot list; unlike KI#238, it does not fall through onto and duplicate the final custom slot.
  KI#242 candidate: the birth profiles nevertheless under-provision islands for two ordinary world sizes relative to the game's own default player counts. Arena uses `(1, 1)` = 1 island while `WORLDSIZE_ARENA` has 2 default players; Small uses `(1, 4)` = 4 islands while `WORLDSIZE_SMALL` has 5 default players. On an ordinary default-player game, the overflow player therefore cannot satisfy the map's one-player-per-large-island contract. `allowDefaultImpl()` cannot create an absent island: the DLL's normal start search may choose an otherwise legal plot on an already-used landmass, but it does not reserve an entire island for each existing start.
  The mismatch is present from 5460 rather than introduced by later world-size work. Later edits to `_world_profile` at practicals 5855, 5864 and 5971 only replace the dictionary's raw integer world-size keys with successively safer constants/enums; the `(1, 1)` Arena and `(1, 4)` Small values remain unchanged through the supplied current tree. Later `CIV4WorldInfo.xml` edits likewise do not change Arena's 2 or Small's 5 default-player values.
  The remaining map birth logic was checked separately. Generated plot-array dimensions and island rectangles are in bounds for the supported profiles; custom start centers are unique and correspond one-to-one with generated islands; same-island fallback indexes the matching island; and the original two custom map options have coherent counts/defaults/value bounds. The mandatory WrapX birth behavior makes its wrap-edge horizontal coast connector legitimate.
  The historical `from SAS_WorldSizes import *` import does not add a second callback leak at 5460, but the original C093 wording was too broad and is corrected here: `SAS_WorldSizes.py` had already become a playable helper map at practical 5415 and therefore exported `getDescription()` and `getGridSize()`. `SAS_Large_Facing_Islands.py` defines both callbacks itself after the import, so those imported names are overwritten locally; no other conflicting map callback was exported by the helper at this point. The broader import-star callback-leak problem affected scripts that lacked their own replacement callback and was later repaired by moving reusable helpers to `SAS_WorldSizeUtils`; current Large Facing Islands imports the safe helper module.
  Fixed and validated later in commit 6fc4f59e6: Arena now uses `(1, 2)` and Small `(1, 5)`, providing one island per default player without a broader start-placement rewrite. Screenshots 0066 and 0067 confirm the two- and five-island layouts; the refreshed `PythonErr.log` remained empty.
  No additional surviving source defect was established in practical 5460. Certified cursor advances through practical 5460 / d185fc9208. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5459 / 3aaba12341.
