SAS-C095 - practical 5458 Water.py birth audit - COMPLETE; KI#243 AND KI#244 CANDIDATES FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5458 / 936bb50818.
  Practical 5458 introduces the adapted `PrivateMaps/Water.py`. Its ordinary generator structure was audited separately from the two findings: the generated plot array uses exactly width*height entries; water percentage is clamped to 20..40; peak percentage is clamped to 0..100 and uses the same low-fractal-value peak threshold direction as `CvMapGeneratorUtil.FractalWorld`; `findStartingArea` intentionally keeps starts on the biggest land area; `addLakes`/`normalizeAddLakes` deliberately suppress the engine's extra lake pass because the script already generates many lakes; and the script has no custom-map-option index surface. The historical `from SAS_WorldSizes import *` imports that helper map's `getDescription`/`getGridSize`, but Water defines both callbacks itself after the import, so they are overwritten locally and no additional helper callback collision is present.
  A separate birth-version grid-size defect is historical-only: the direct dictionary mixed Arena key `0` with old hardcoded `WorldSizeTypes` values after Arena shifted runtime world-size indices. Practical 5855 explicitly repairs Water's grid keys and documents empirical Large/Huge size validation; current Water uses the corrected XML-aligned world-size keys. This repaired lineage is not counted as a new survivor.
  KI#243 is an inherited third-party Water shared-state bug broadened by SAS practical 5083. Water's `addRivers()` mutates the shared global defines `PLOTS_PER_RIVER_EDGE`, `RIVER_SOURCE_MIN_RIVER_RANGE` and `RIVER_SOURCE_MIN_SEAWATER_RANGE`, including non-idempotently replacing `PLOTS_PER_RIVER_EDGE` with half of its *current* value, and then calls `allowDefaultImpl()` so the DLL river generator continues. The original downloaded Water.py contains the same unscoped mutation. Base AdvCiv reads the three values per call, so Water works but leaks its values into later generations and compounds on repeated Water maps. SAS practical 5083 additionally made the reads function-local `static const`: if Water runs first, its denser values become process-cached for subsequent maps; if a normal map initializes the statics first, Water mutates `CvGlobals` but its intended override never reaches the already-cached default generator. `CvGame::regenerateMap()` resets neither function statics nor these globals. The repair snapshots the original defines before the Python callback, consumes post-callback values only for the current generation, restores changed globals afterward and removes process-static caching from these three map-script-mutable reads. The DLL compiled successfully; screenshots 0183 and 0184 show Water generating with many rivers and continuing normally through turn 101.
  KI#244 is an inherited third-party Water topology bug. Water declares `getWrapX() == True` and `getWrapY() == False`, but original and current `WaterFractalWorld.isSurroundedByLand()` compute every cardinal neighbor with modulo on both coordinates. Civ4's map topology wraps an axis only when its wrap flag is true, so Water invents north/south edge adjacency during its one-tile-lake cleanup. A north-edge water tile can remain solely because an unrelated south-edge tile at the same X is water. The repair preserves X modulo wrapping but treats north/south out-of-bounds neighbors as no connected water, matching the script's declared topology. Focused topology checks confirmed that opposite Y edges are separated while the X seam remains wrapped; the compiled Water runtime smoke test completed without an observed issue.
  During this checkpoint, the C093 helper-export explanation was corrected: `SAS_WorldSizes.py` already exported playable-map `getDescription/getGridSize` callbacks at 5460; Large Facing Islands remained safe because it overwrote both locally. This correction changes rationale only, not C093's KI#242 conclusion or certified cursor.
  No additional surviving source defect was established in practical 5458. Certified cursor advances through practical 5458 / 936bb50818. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5457 / 3fffd5ea98.


SAS-C096 - practical 5457 -> 5456 map-classification / SAS_Spiky_Avenues birth audit - COMPLETE; KI#245 FOUND / FIXED AND VALIDATED LATER
  Reviewed 2 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5457 / 3fffd5ea98 and 5456 / 15aa086e93.
  Practical 5457 is a small follow-up. `Mixed_Continents.py` changes only `isAdvancedMap()` from 0 to 1, deliberately removing a multi-option map from Simple Game; `SAS_Longworld.py` removes an unused `CvMapGeneratorUtil` module import while retaining its direct `TerrainGenerator`/`FeatureGenerator` imports; and `SAS_Spiky_Avenues.py` removes an unused local `CyGlobalContext`. The GlobalDefine map-name classification change is XML-only. No runtime behavior defect was established in these Python hunks.
  Practical 5456 introduces `SAS_Spiky_Avenues.py`. Its generator was audited as executable map logic rather than inferred from the visual design. Plot generation creates one land `house` for each custom start slot, two houses per spike; slot coordinates are unique; the slot assignment cache and `sas_used_start_plots` prevent custom-slot reuse; invalid/missing custom slots call `allowDefaultImpl()` rather than falling through to the last slot; the safety search is bounded to radius 1..2 around the intended house and refuses an already-used plot; plot-array indexing stays within the map dimensions produced by the same profile formula; and X-only wrap plus Y-edge margins are internally coherent. The later `SAS_WorldSizes`/world-size-constant refactors do not alter the profile values discussed below.
  KI#245 candidate: the map's own layout contract says `house = ... where one player starts`, `1 spike = 2 houses = 2 player starts`, the profile `scales with world size to match default player counts`, and the public description says `One player starts per house`. However the birth profile uses Tiny `(1 street, 1 spike)` = only 2 houses for `WORLDSIZE_TINY`'s 3 default players, and Small `(1, 2)` = only 4 houses for `WORLDSIZE_SMALL`'s 5 default players. Those world-size defaults were already in force at practical 5456 and remain 3/5 in current `CIV4WorldInfo.xml`. No later Spiky profile edit repairs the mismatch: practical 5855 only replaces raw world-size keys with SAS constants, 5864 renames those constants, 5897 moves the helper import, and 5971 replaces the constants with `WorldSizeTypes`; all preserve Tiny `(1,1)` and Small `(1,2)`. Current SAS retains the same under-capacity profiles.
  The overflow guard is therefore safe against KI#238-style duplicate-last-slot behavior but cannot preserve the map's stated layout on an ordinary default-player Tiny/Small game. Once `iIndex >= len(slots)`, Civ4's default start finder may place the excess player on some legal generated land, but no missing third/fifth house exists to assign: by simple capacity, at least one default player cannot start in a distinct designed house. This is independent of start-distance tuning and of whether fallback happens to choose an avenue, bridge or an already represented house area. Large intentionally has one spare house (12 for 11 players), showing exact equality is not required; the correctness requirement is only that designed one-player house capacity not fall below the normal default player count.
  Fixed and validated later in commit 6fc4f59e6: Tiny now uses 2 spikes / 4 houses and Small 3 spikes / 6 houses, the next even capacities supported by the symmetric spike primitive. Screenshots 0068 and 0069 confirm both layouts; the refreshed `PythonErr.log` remained empty.
  No additional surviving source defect was established in practical 5457 -> 5456. Certified cursor advances through practical 5456 / 15aa086e93. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5455 / fa79a78fde.


SAS-C097 - practical 5455 SAS_Longworld birth audit - COMPLETE; KI#246 CANDIDATE FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5455 / fa79a78fde.
  Practical 5455 introduces `SAS_Longworld.py`. The generator was checked as a full map/start-placement implementation. `generatePlotTypes()` allocates exactly width*height plot entries; `getStripBounds()` leaves polar water margins and produces a nonempty central band for supported heights; every generated X column keeps at least three land rows; and the top/bottom edge movement is limited to one row from the preceding column, so adjacent columns retain overlapping land and the intended single horizontal landmass stays connected. Hills are added only after land creation and do not make starts impassable. The nominal start formula uses the actual sorted alive-player list rather than world-default capacity and, by itself, produces unique `(X,Y)` pairs even at 48 players on the smallest supported layout because repeated X values are staggered through strip rows.
  The birth-version `getGridSize()` does contain the older Arena/`WorldSizeTypes` key-offset problem: with Arena inserted into XML while the old Python enum values remained shifted, several dictionary keys/fallback comparisons address the wrong runtime world size. Practical 5854/5855 explicitly repairs that lineage (including the observed Huge wrong-size/KeyError behavior) and current Longworld uses XML-aligned `WorldSizeTypes` keys. That repaired grid-size defect is historical-only and is not counted again here. Later option/import/world-size refactors also leave the finding below's start fallback semantics unchanged.
  KI#246 candidate: `findStartingPlot()` has no per-player assignment cache or used-start set. A nominal start that lands on a wavy strip edge trimmed to water enters a fallback that scans `strip_rows` at the same X first and immediately returns the first valid land plot, without checking whether another player has already been assigned that plot. High-player-count small maps make repeated X coordinates ordinary under the script's own spacing formula. In the current default Arena profile the actual map width is 36 plots, giving `iSpan = 32`; with 48 alive civ players, players 0 and 1 both receive X=2 while their nominal Y rows are 6 and 7. The default strip height is four. The generator's normal top-trim RNG can remove row 6 at X=2 while leaving row 7 land (the first top-trim branch occurs with 35% probability, and a one-row trim still satisfies the minimum-band-height rule). Player 0 then sees `(2,6)` as water and its fallback checks `(2,6)`, then returns `(2,7)`; player 1's nominal start is already `(2,7)`. The same class of collision exists in the birth version (which also ships the 48-civ DLL) despite its then-different grid-size profile.
  The engine does not rescue this duplicate. As established independently during KI#238, a non-null Python `findStartingPlot` result is returned directly by `CvPlayer::findStartingPlot`, and `setStartingPlot` does not deduplicate Python-supplied plots. This is therefore a source-reachable duplicate starting-site path, not merely a spacing/aesthetics concern. Current SAS retains the same fallback body; practical 5816 only reformats around it, and no later Longworld commit adds used-start tracking.
  Surgical direction for KI#246: make Longworld start assignment collision-aware and stable across repeated callback calls, preferably mirroring the already-defensive Spiky pattern with a per-generation `player -> plot` assignment cache plus a used-plot set reset in `beforeGeneration`. When the nominal edge tile is invalid, choose an unused valid strip tile (same/nearby X first) rather than blindly returning the first valid tile in that column. Do not merely change the horizontal spacing formula: repeated X is acceptable because different strip rows are intentional; the defect is reuse during fallback.
  No additional surviving source defect was established in practical 5455. Certified cursor advances through practical 5455 / fa79a78fde. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5454 / 093b59bae4.


SAS-C098 - practical 5454 BTG_Grid import/adaptation audit - COMPLETE; KI#247 -> KI#253 CANDIDATES FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5454 / 093b59bae4.
  Practical 5454 imports/adapts `BTG_Grid.py` and makes several much smaller map-related changes. The non-Grid runtime hunks close cleanly: `BTG_Cross.py` consistently removes the BTG-only Desert->Marsh choice by reducing option 11 from three values to two, making Normal the default and leaving the remaining Desert-removal branch as Grassland; `Peirce.py` changes only the displayed author string; and `PerfectMongoose.py` / `Terra.py` deliberately switch `isAdvancedMap()` so they leave Simple Game while remaining available in Custom Game. The accompanying map-name classification / WorldPicker changes are XML/art rather than runtime-source-denominator changes and are consistent with the intended map-pool update.
  `BTG_Grid.py` was audited as a full executable imported generator: all ten custom-option declarations/defaults/random flags were mapped to their runtime option reads; `beforeGeneration`, static template selection/clamping, `GridMultilayeredFractal.generatePlotsByRegion`, `generatePlotTypes`, both custom-start branches, default-fallback behavior, normalization/mirroring, world-size/grid callbacks and the current C++ Python-callback contracts were traced. Birth practical 5454 was compared with current SAS rather than assuming later lint/world-size refactors repaired old logic. Seven independent current survivors were established; several other suspicious paths were explicitly rejected or left unnumbered below rather than inflating the count.
  KI#247 candidate (SAS-specific imported Grid adaptation defect): the custom-start capacity guard counts `len(regions_in_use)`, but generation always appends the chosen source region once and then appends the complete set of active region IDs again. The source region is therefore present twice, so list length overstates *distinct* hub capacity by exactly one. This defeats the guard on one-over-template configurations. With 11 actual players, 1-Line Flat and default `Empty Land = Yes`, the template clamps from 11 requested regions to 10 real regions, `regions_in_use` nevertheless has length 11, the guard sees `11 < 11` as false, `iTotLoop = iNumRegions = 10`, and Python assigns only ten shuffled players before returning success. Current `CvGame::assignStartingPlots` returns immediately when the Python callback succeeds, so player 11 is not filled by the C++ default path. With `Empty Land = No`, the same 11-player/10-region case loops over all eleven players; the final `reg = 10` lookup fails and the generic fallback in the script reuses `region_data.keys()[0]`, allowing the extra player to reuse region 0 (and in the mirrored-position branch, the same copied offset/start). Equivalent one-over-template reuse exists for 13 players / 2-line / Empty Land No and 16 players / 3-line / Empty Land No. The guard, duplicate list construction and fallback survive current SAS.
  Surgical direction for KI#247: capacity checks must use distinct usable region IDs / actual assignable slots, not raw `regions_in_use` length; never return successful Python assignment unless every actual non-spectator civ player received a unique valid start. If requested players exceed custom region capacity, call `allowDefaultImpl()` before assigning any custom starts (or provide enough actual templates) rather than under-assigning or reusing region 0.
  KI#248 candidate (SAS-specific imported Grid data defect): the 9-region `1 Line Flat` template defines region 5 as `[0.755, 0.666, 0.0, 1.0]`, so west longitude is greater than east longitude. All neighboring entries form the expected monotonic sequence (`...0.444->0.555`, then this entry, then `0.666->0.777`), and a scan of every Grid template rectangle found this is the only west/east or south/north inversion. At runtime the region therefore has negative width. In non-mirrored generation that invalid dimension reaches `generatePlotsInRegion`/`CyFractal.fracInit`; in mirrored modes the target-copy loops for the negative-width region perform no regional copy while the region is still retained for starting logic, so a player can receive an invalid/out-of-hub copied offset or the custom start path can encounter a region with no land areas. The exact malformed rectangle is present in the 5454 birth file and survives current SAS.
  Surgical direction for KI#248: repair the region-5 west boundary to the contiguous intended boundary (the sequence strongly indicates `0.555`, yielding `[0.555, 0.666, ...]`) and validate the 9-player 1-line layout under all four Mirrored Hubs choices. Also keep a lightweight template invariant check (`west < east`, `south < north`, in [0,1]) in development tooling so this class cannot silently recur.
  KI#249 candidate (broader inherited third-party/Firaxis-style map-script defect): three Grid branches use `if pPlot.isFreshWater:` instead of calling `pPlot.isFreshWater()`. `CyPlot.isFreshWater` is exported to Python as the method `bool ()`; in Python 2 the bound-method object itself is truthy. Grid's coastal regional scoring therefore always takes the freshwater branch for every adjacent water tile, and both custom-start branches add the intended +1000 freshwater preference to every land candidate. That erases freshwater preference, distorts regional yield scoring and can promote plots whose underlying found value is zero. This is not unique to Grid: current `BTG_Cross.py` has the same three missing calls, and current `Archipelago.py`, `Islands.py` and `Equal_Islands_V2_beta.py` each contain the same predicate form. The archived practical-5437 BTG_Cross birth patch already contains it, so the defect is classified as inherited third-party/Firaxis-style map-script logic rather than seven separate SAS regressions; upcoming source-commit audits that introduce/copy those files should treat it as this same KI lineage, not re-count it.
  Surgical direction for KI#249: audit all runtime map scripts for method objects used as Boolean predicates and change every live `pPlot.isFreshWater` condition to `pPlot.isFreshWater()`. Re-check analogous `isX`/`canX` method predicates rather than fixing only the five currently known files.
  KI#250 candidate (SAS-specific Grid option-index drift): the visible custom option 7 is `Start Distance` with values Normal/16 tiles, Far/20 tiles and Far Mixed/20 horizontal + 16 vertical. `getGridSize()` is the only code that implements those three physical spacing tables, but its first branch tests `getCustomMapOption(5) == 0`; option 5 is `Empty Land`, not Start Distance. With default Empty Land = Yes, *every* Start Distance selection is forced through the Normal/16-size table, so Far and Far Mixed do nothing. With Empty Land = No, Start Distance Far selects the 20-size table, while both Normal and Far Mixed fall into the mixed table, so Normal becomes incorrectly enlarged/mixed. Option 7 has no other runtime use that could restore its advertised behavior. The wrong option-5 test is already present in practical 5454 and survives current SAS; sibling `BTG_Cross.getGridSize()` provides a control by reading its Start Distance option directly before selecting its size table.
  Surgical direction for KI#250: select all three Grid size branches solely from custom option 7 (`0` normal, `1` far, `2` far-mixed); keep Empty Land option 5 limited to whether unused template regions are generated/filled and to the corresponding player-region bookkeeping.
  KI#251 candidate (SAS-specific Grid table-completeness defect): `assignStartingPlots()` directly indexes `minimums[iNumRegions]`, but the dictionary contains only 2..12 plus 15,16,18. Exact control-flow simulation against the script's own line/empty-land configurations shows reachable supported missing keys, notably one-player 1-line and 3-line layouts (plus one-player 2-line with default Empty Land), and 14 players / default 3 Lines Tall / default Empty Land, which reaches `iNumRegions == 14` after the inflated capacity guard passes. The resulting `KeyError` happens before Grid assigns custom starts; `callMapFunction` therefore reports the Python callback unsuccessful and C++ safely falls back to ordinary starting plots, so this is not a turn-0 NULL-start catastrophe. It is nevertheless a real Python error and silently discards the map's intended regional start placement for those supported setups. Sibling `BTG_Cross.py` already uses the robust pattern `if iNumRegions in minimums ... else [0.05, 0.08]`; Grid does not. The direct lookup survives current SAS.
  Surgical direction for KI#251: make the distance lookup total for every reachable custom region count, preferably with explicit tuned values where meaningful and a conservative fallback for unusual counts. Do not rely on an exception to trigger C++ placement.
  KI#252 candidate (SAS-specific high-player/mirroring state defect): with 19..48 actual players and `Empty Land = No`, `beforeGeneration()` deliberately sets `iPlayers` to the actual count and returns early because Grid's custom regional generator supports at most 18. `generatePlotTypes()` then safely uses generic `FractalWorld`, and `assignStartingPlots()` safely calls the default C++ implementation. However, if the user also selects `Mirrored Hubs = Yes - Mirrored` (option 1 == 3), `normalizeAddExtras()` later unconditionally calls `mirrorizeMap()`. That function immediately reads global `region_duplicated` and `other_regions`, but those globals are initialized only inside `GridMultilayeredFractal.generatePlotsByRegion`, which this >18 fallback path never ran. On a fresh module generation the names are undefined and can raise; in a persistent/reused map-script interpreter they can instead retain stale geometry from an earlier custom generation. The high-player generic fallback therefore is not self-contained under the fully mirrored option. Current SAS retains the unconditional normalization call and the generator-only initialization.
  Surgical direction for KI#252: reset per-generation Grid globals in `beforeGeneration` and track whether custom Grid regional generation actually completed; gate `mirrorizeMap()` on that explicit state. Generic-fractal/default-start fallback must never execute regional mirroring against uninitialized or stale coordinates.
  KI#253 candidate (SAS-specific Grid design/capacity defect): Grid's public contract says `Each player has its own hub` and the SAS adaptation description explicitly says `SAS48/high player counts supported`, but the static templates top out at 10 one-line hubs, 12 two-line hubs and only 15 hubs for the default 3-line layout. Current Huge has 16 default players and SAS24/32/40/48 have 24/32/40/48. Under the default `Empty Land = Yes` path, a default Huge or any SAS-size high-player game therefore generates at most the 15-hub 3-line template and then the script's safety guard hands starting placement to C++; generic placement can keep the game playable, but it cannot create the absent 16th through 48th hubs, so the map cannot satisfy its defining one-player-per-hub layout. This under-capacity exists from practical 5454: the birth script already advertises high-player support while shipping the same template ceilings. Later SAS world-size/grid refactors expand physical dimensions but do not add high-count hub templates.
  Surgical direction for KI#253: either generate region templates dynamically to at least the actual/default supported player count (preferred if the advertised SAS48 Grid identity is retained), or explicitly cap/disable the custom per-player-hub mode above its real region capacity and adjust the description. Merely calling default starting-plot placement does not satisfy a missing-hub geometry contract, just as with KI#242/KI#245.
  Current repair and validation: KI#248 corrects the sole inverted static rectangle. KI#249 calls all nine inherited `isFreshWater()` predicates across Grid, Cross, Archipelago, Islands and Equal Islands; a focused runtime-Python predicate scan found no additional map-script callable in the same form. KI#250 reads Start Distance option 7 for every physical-size branch. KI#251 retains tuned minimum-distance values and uses Cross's conservative fallback for every other supported region count. KI#252 resets mirror geometry per generation and gates final mirroring on explicit completion of regional generation. KI#253 derives row capacity from the actual player count, generates equal rectangular rows whenever a static template is absent and removes the old 18-player regional-generation/start cap. SAS-size extrapolation now holds the selected row count/height fixed and adds horizontal columns in proportion to default players, preserving Huge's per-hub area rather than reshaping a fixed-row map through generic aspect-ratio calibration. An isolated structural check verifies valid `[0,1]` rectangles and exact capacities for 1-48 players under every line mode; all five edited scripts pass Python AST parsing and `git diff --check`. Runtime tests generated nine-player Standard 1-Line Grid maps under all four mirroring modes with exact Normal/Far/Far-Mixed dimensions `84x16`, `104x20` and `104x16`; a 14-player Huge 3-Line map at `84x48`; and a default 48-player SAS48 3-Line fully mirrored map at `252x48`. Turn-11 autoplays completed without an observed map issue after one unrelated recurrent autoplay-start crash and restart. Open and mirrored Cross both generated at `48x48`; Archipelago, Islands and Equal Islands generated at `78x56`, with Equal Islands placing each player on a separate landmass. The high-player Grid and subsequent script tests ran successively in the same Civ4 session without a stale mirroring-state error.
  Two additional Grid code smells were deliberately *not* promoted. Both custom-start branches reset `iPass = 0` inside the retry loop, whereas sibling BTG_Cross initializes it outside; current AdvCiv's Python-compatibility `startingPlotWithinRange` always returns false and Grid's own `min_dist` does not change across retries, so the retry is logically unable to relax as written. However no independent ordinary valid-geometry configuration was source-proven to require a second pass without another already-confirmed Grid defect, so it remains an unnumbered robustness flaw rather than a manufactured KI. Likewise the optional Precious Metal normalizer can index `plotsboundaries[0]` without proving that the stochastic diagonal boundary list is nonempty (same pattern exists in BTG_Cross); no deterministic supported configuration forcing an empty list was established here, so it remains an audit note rather than a confirmed defect.
  Several broader candidates were positively rejected. The initial theory that all >18-player Grid games leave players unassigned is false: default high-count paths hit the explicit capacity/default-start fallback, and `Empty Land = No` uses generic FractalWorld plus C++ starts. The strange `region_duplicated` comparison effectively selects the widest source region, but within each live Grid template row heights are equal and no current wrong output was established from that predicate alone. The birth-version Arena/world-size dictionary issue is historical lineage later repaired by practical 5854/5855 and is not counted again.
  No additional surviving source defect was established in practical 5454 outside KI#247 -> KI#253. Certified cursor advances through practical 5454 / 093b59bae4. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5453 / 866bab2de4.


SAS-C099 - practical 5453 Peirce import/adaptation audit - COMPLETE; INHERITED THIRD-PARTY KI#254 + KI#255 FIXED/VALIDATED
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5453 / 866bab2de4.
  Practical 5453 imports/adapts `Peirce.py` as a full executable quincuncial map generator and makes two much smaller runtime changes: `BTG_Cross.py` corrects the invalid `BONUS_GEMSTONESTONES` lookup to the real `BONUS_GEMSTONES`, and `Equal_Islands_V2_beta.py` only narrows its descriptive text by removing the stronger "equalized starts/resources" wording. Those smaller hunks are clean. Peirce was audited across its two custom options, world-size square-grid callback, wrap modes, plot/terrain/feature post-processing, latitude/frame/crease helpers, arbitrary-polygon region generation and custom starting-area filter. Path history after 5453 contains only the 5454 author-string correction, formatting/blank-line/print/BOM cleanup and the later SAS world-size-helper import rename; none changes either finding below, so both survive current SAS.
  KI#254 candidate (inherited third-party Peirce math defect): all four distance-squared tests in `isPeirceCrease()` use expressions of the form `(iX - centerX)^2+(iY - centerY)^2`. In Python `^` is bitwise XOR, not exponentiation, and operator precedence makes `dx^2+dy^2` parse as `(dx ^ (2 + dy)) ^ 2`, not even as the sum of two XOR terms. The function therefore does not compare Euclidean distance squared with `iRadius2`, despite calculating `iRadius2 = iRadius*iRadius` immediately above and documenting the crease as the area outside the relevant circular disc. The result materially changes map topology: on the current Standard Pangaea square (side 14), the toroidal XOR predicate marks 0 crease plots where the corresponding `dx*dx + dy*dy` predicate marks 56; no-wrap marks 96 instead of 124. On larger current layouts the defect can erase the crease entirely as well (for example SAS48 Pangaea side 35 marks 0 rather than 236 toroidal and 0 rather than 506 no-wrap). `generatePlotTypes`, `generateTerrainTypes` and `afterGeneration` all rely on this helper to force/clean the crease ocean, so this is a live geometry error rather than dead arithmetic. The four `^2` expressions are already present both in the independently downloaded original `Peirce.py` and in the 5453 SAS birth file, and remain byte-for-byte in current Peirce.
  Surgical direction for KI#254: express the squared distance explicitly as `dx * dx + dy * dy` (or `dx ** 2 + dy ** 2`) in all four quadrant tests, preferably via one tiny local/helper calculation so precedence cannot recur. Re-test all five map types under both toroidal and no-wrap layouts because crease classification is consumed by plot, terrain and post-generation passes.
  KI#255 candidate (inherited third-party Peirce E-W anchor copy/paste defect): in the E-W-continent branch, each of the four northern quadrant cases creates a landscape `generatePlotsParallelContinent` arm from `iRefX1/iRefY1`, then computes a *different* `iRefX2/iRefY2` for the portrait-oriented companion arm. The following portrait call nevertheless passes `iRefX1/iRefY1` again. The same four mistakes are duplicated in the second wrap-mode implementation, for eight live sites total (current lines around 897/907/917/927 and 1433/1443/1453/1463). The computed second anchors are overwritten later without being read anywhere else, making the copy/paste error explicit. `iPrim` omits only one of the four northern quadrant cases, so every E-W generation executes three of these wrongly anchored portrait pieces. On a comfortably large current no-wrap E-W square (side 37), for example, the top-right first anchor is `(16,19)` while the code explicitly computes the portrait anchor `(20,13)`; the live call instead generates that portrait polygon around the first anchor, shifting/overlapping the intended orthogonal piece by several plots. The public/local Peirce design describes E-W as a northern continent wrapping around the central pole, which is consistent with the distinct computed anchors rather than discarding them. The eight bad calls exist both in the independently downloaded original `Peirce.py` and in practical 5453; no later Peirce commit changes them.
  Surgical direction for KI#255: in each of the eight E-W companion-arm calls, pass the freshly computed `iRefX2, iRefY2` instead of `iRefX1, iRefY1`; then visually/regression-test E-W under both wrap modes and multiple `iPrim/iSec` rolls. Keep the first landscape calls on `iRefX1/iRefY1`; only the portrait companion calls are wrong.
  Current repair and validation: KI#254 now uses explicit delta multiplication in all four crease-distance tests. KI#255 now passes the computed second anchor to all eight portrait companion-arm calls while leaving the first landscape calls unchanged. The independently downloaded original at `C:\Users\PC\Downloads\Peirce_3\Peirce.py` confirmed that both defects predate SAS integration. User runtime smoke tests successfully generated E-W maps with both toroidal and no-wrap layouts; no visible issue was found at a glance.
  Two additional Peirce concerns were deliberately not promoted. The new SAS world-size scaling allows small Arena/Duel squares where legacy continent formulas yield negative or reversed nominal lengths/widths, but exhaustive call tracing across all five map types, both wrap modes and all primary/secondary quadrant rolls still produced multiple nonzero polygons intersecting the map without a deterministic exception; `MapAreaPolygon` derives a positive padded bounding box from arbitrary vertex order and clips whole-world writes, so awkward small-map geometry alone is not enough to call a correctness defect. `getCustomMapOptionDefault()` returning `-1` for both options is also unusual, but `NO_CUSTOM_MAPOPTION/-1` participates in Civ4's implicit random-custom-option convention in other stock-style scripts; without a source-proven setup failure it remains unnumbered rather than speculative. No out-of-bounds square-array, zero latitude denominator, invalid `TERRAIN_PEAK` lookup or separate custom-start failure was established.
  No additional surviving source defect was established in practical 5453 outside KI#254 and KI#255. Certified cursor advances through practical 5453 / 866bab2de4. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5452 / 031a95cc7c.


SAS-C100 - practical 5452 Equal Islands import/map-cleanup audit - COMPLETE; KI#256 CANDIDATE FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5452 / 031a95cc7c.
  Practical 5452 imports/adapts `Equal_Islands_V2_beta.py`, adds it to the naval-heavy map classification/world picker, changes Earth2 and Lakes to Advanced-map-only visibility, performs quote normalization across runtime Python, and updates legacy map resource tags to current SAS names. The large Equal Islands script was audited across player/region templates (1..48), actual plot dimensions, island generation, custom starting-plot assignment, terrain/features/bonuses generation order, source-region selection, resource balancing, whole-region cloning, river cloning and cleanup outside used regions. The existing missing-parentheses `pPlot.isFreshWater` occurrence belongs to already-recorded broader inherited KI#249 and is not counted again.
  KI#256 candidate (SAS-specific imported Equal Islands cloning/fairness defect): the map's own purpose/description says all players get the exact same island, and `normalizeAddExtras()` copies the selected best source region's plot type, terrain, bonus, improvement, feature and rivers to every other player's corresponding region. Features are generated before bonuses (`CvMapGenerator::addGameElements`: `addFeatures()` then `addBonuses()`), so high-latitude target regions can legitimately contain `FEATURE_ICE` before the clone. The clone then calls `p.setBonusType(p0.getBonusType(-1))` but explicitly refuses to replace/remove Ice: `if p.getFeatureType() != ice: ... p.setFeatureType(...)`. For water-to-water coordinates, `setPlotType(PLOT_OCEAN, ...)` is a no-op when both source and target are already water, so pre-existing target Ice survives. `CvPlot::setBonusType()` simply assigns the requested bonus and updates counts/yields; it does not call `canHaveBonus()` or remove an invalid feature. Current Fish/Molluscs/Crab/Whale BonusInfos have Coast/Ocean terrain validity but no feature-valid entries, while `FEATURE_ICE` is Coast/Ocean-only, impassable and no-city. A normal ice-free source seafood coordinate can therefore be forcibly cloned onto a corresponding polar target water tile while that target's Ice is retained, yielding e.g. Fish under impassable Ice; current Work Boats have `bCanMoveImpassable=0`, so the copied resource can be inaccessible to that player. Even without a bonus, independent retained Ice barriers make the supposedly identical player islands/topology differ. The Ice-preservation block is present from the 5452 birth file and survives unchanged in current SAS; 5453 only removes the stronger phrase `equalized starts/resources` from the description but retains the explicit `exact same ... island` contract.
  Surgical direction for KI#256: make feature cloning authoritative for the used player regions instead of preserving each target region's independently generated Ice. The simplest equality-preserving repair is to copy the source feature exactly (including source Ice/no-Ice) after plot type/terrain have been cloned, with legality/order checks as needed. At minimum, never leave target Ice in place while forcibly copying an ice-incompatible source bonus; validate the final feature/bonus combination and preserve the map's equal-island premise. Re-test cold-climate/high-latitude configurations and water resources.
  Two initially strong Equal Islands candidates were positively rejected. The region-yield sorter does not overrun `sorting_regions`: after the real player regions are consumed, `region_numbers` is empty, so the inner loop executes zero times and the suspicious `sorting_regions[regionTestLoop]` expression is never evaluated for unused template regions. `region_numbers = regions_in_use` does alias and empty the global list, but later normalization reconstructs its work from `region_coords` and player starts and no current behavioral consequence was established. A second apparent zero/negative-width fractal failure came from confusing XML/world-size grid cells with actual map plot dimensions; recomputing every current world size against 1..48 players in real plot coordinates keeps generated region/island dimensions positive, so that candidate is discarded.
  Practical 5452 also contains two genuine but historical-only regressions. Its BTG_Cross resource cleanup mistypes the already-correct `BONUS_GEMSTONES` as nonexistent `BONUS_GEMSTONESTONES`; strict `getInfoTypeOrFail` makes that a real transient failure, but practical 5453 immediately restores `BONUS_GEMSTONES`. Fantasy Realm's six resource-list assignments are accidentally indented one level too deep in 5452; practical 5892 / 0e6854abcd (`repair legacy map script indentation`) explicitly repairs that parse/indentation break. Neither survives and neither receives a new KI. The remaining map-resource replacements were mechanically checked against current XML and use valid SAS tags (e.g. Maize, Gemstones, Grapes, Molluscs, Cattle, Elephants); quote-only Python changes are semantically neutral. Earth2/Lakes `isAdvancedMap` flips match the commit's explicit removal-from-Simple-Game intent.
  No additional surviving source defect was established in practical 5452 outside KI#256 (plus existing KI#249 lineage). Certified cursor advances through practical 5452 / 031a95cc7c. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5451 / 1e8acff71e.


SAS-C101 - practical 5451 through 5450 Simple Game stale-option warning / map-helper callback-leak lineage audit - COMPLETE
  Reviewed 2 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5451 / 1e8acff71e and 5450 / 0dcc98613c.
  Practical 5450 deliberately reverts temporary BTG_Lagoon/Boreal guards that had returned harmless-looking fallback values for stale/foreign custom-map-option indices and records inherited Base AdvCiv KI#106 instead. This restores the ordinary dictionary KeyError rather than silently substituting an empty label, one option value, default 0 or false randomization for an option that the selected map does not actually own. The change does not create the stale Simple Game option index; the commit documentation independently reproduced that cache problem in minimally modified Base AdvCiv. Practical 5451 then adds one shared diagnostic wrapper: on the first missing option key it raises an explanatory KI#106 KeyError containing the requested and real option counts, while later stale accesses return from the helper and therefore reach the same original per-map dictionary KeyError. The shared boolean is intentionally process/module-global so the explanatory notice is emitted once rather than once per callback/map; valid indices never enter the warning helper. No malformed option-count argument, accidental valid-index rejection or surviving warning-state defect was established.
  A genuine historical SAS regression is present in practical 5451 but is already repaired and therefore is not counted as a new current KI. To make the shared warning helper available, 5451 newly adds `from SAS_WorldSizes import *` to fourteen stock/ported maps (including Archipelago, Big_and_Small, Continents, Custom_Continents, Fractal, Hemispheres, Islands, Medium_and_Small, Mixed_Continents, Pangaea, PerfectMongoose, Shuffle, Terra and not_too_Big_or_Small). At this point `PrivateMaps/SAS_WorldSizes.py` was itself a playable helper map and exported Civ4 map-callback names `getDescription()` and compact `getGridSize()`. Import-star therefore made those callbacks part of importing map scripts that did not override them, allowing unrelated scripts to inherit the helper's compact grid sizing. Practical 5897 / 731d4fb5e9 explicitly repairs this architecture by moving reusable helpers to `Assets/Python/SAS_WorldSizeUtils.py`, removing map callbacks from the helper module and leaving a separate `SAS_Simple_Flat_Grass.py` playable wrapper. That repair documents an empirical RandomScriptMap Huge change from the leaked 60x44 result back to 120x84 and states that Archipelago/other importers were likely affected. Current map imports use the callback-free helper, so the 5451 leak does not survive.
  The remaining 5451 runtime hunks are the same missing-key warning insertion in map option name/value/default/random/description dictionaries; a mechanical pass found no executable additions beyond those guards, the fourteen helper imports and the shared warning helper (Team_Battleground only also gains blank-line spacing). No additional surviving source defect was established in 5451/5450. Certified cursor advances through practical 5450 / 0dcc98613c. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5449 / eaa9a887f9.


SAS-C102 - practical 5449 Planet Generator 0.68 import/adaptation audit - COMPLETE; KI#257-KI#261 FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5449 / eaa9a887f9. This commit imports and adapts the approximately 3,700-line `PrivateMaps/Planet_Generator_0_68.py`; the audit therefore treated the map as executable generator code rather than certifying the commit from its import title. Current descendants were traced through practicals 5816/5865/5867/5868/5897: those later Planet edits are formatting/Python-syntax/import-helper maintenance and do not semantically repair the findings below.
  Confirmed KI#257 (inherited Planet/C2C feature-spread alias): `addFeatures()` retains `plot = map.sPlot(x,y)` as the source seed, then obtains neighbors with another `map.sPlot(tx,ty)` and subsequently expects both Python objects to refer to different plots. Current `CyMap::sPlot` uses one process-static `CyPlot`, retargets it on every call, and is exposed with `reference_existing_object`; after the neighbor lookup both references therefore point to the neighbor. In the live Forest/Jungle spreading branch, `isRiverCrossing(tilePlot, plot)` effectively compares the neighbor with itself and returns no crossing, so intended river barriers are lost; source-terrain reads used when assimilating neighboring Desert/Tundra similarly read the retargeted neighbor terrain instead. Upstream C2C Planet contains the same retained-source `sPlot` pattern and the same static/reference-existing-object binding, so this is inherited rather than created by SAS. Preferred repair: use independent `map.plot(...)` objects (or otherwise snapshot all source state before any `sPlot` reuse) wherever source and neighbor must coexist. The inherited helper `isRiverCrossing()` must be repaired in the same change: Planet wraps X, but the helper derives direction from raw `x2-x1`; seam-adjacent x=0/x=W-1 plots therefore select the wrong E/W direction. That direction bug is real but currently masked in this caller by the stronger shared-`sPlot` alias, so it is recorded as the second required half of KI#257 rather than inflated into another KI.
  Confirmed KI#258 (SAS-specific Smart Selection unit regression): upstream Planet 0.68 computes `mapArea = worldInfo.getGridHeight() * worldInfo.getGridWidth() * 16`, converting WorldInfo terrain-cell area to plot-space before comparing against the following 40x24, 48x28, 52x32, 64x40, 84x52 and 128x80 plot-space buckets. SAS 5449 removes `* 16` while retaining those exact thresholds (and current source even documents them as plot-space). The default Smart Selection therefore systematically classifies a different size unit. Concrete current examples: Standard + separation <4 + 13 continents is classified Tiny and selects three rows although its plot-space bucket is Large and selects two; Huge + separation >=4 + 1-2 continents is misclassified Standard and loses the Huge rule's special center distribution. Preferred repair: restore the terrain-cell-to-plot-area conversion (or equivalently convert the thresholds consistently) and re-test the smart 2-row/3-row/center boundaries across all current SAS world sizes.
  Confirmed KI#259 (inherited Planet latitude callback / AdvCiv True Starts interaction): Planet derives `top = 30 - 2 * polarTemperature` and `bottom = -top`. Six supported climate-option combinations make `top < bottom` (examples include -14/+14, -38/+38, -62/+62 and -86/+86). `CvMap::reset()` clamps each result to +/-90 but does not reorder them and explicitly asserts `top >= bottom`. Ordinary `CvPlot::calculateLatitude()` damage is narrower than first suspected because it later uses absolute latitude, so the earlier broad climate-catastrophe theory was rejected. AdvCiv True Starts, however, explicitly treats the map as latitude-aware only when `getTopLatitude() > getBottomLatitude()`; on the reversed Planet combinations it therefore sets its map-has-latitudes state false and disables latitude-sensitive civilization start fitness, high-latitude water/climate handling and latitude restrictions in its start/resource evaluation. Upstream C2C Planet contains the same latitude formulas. Preferred repair: make the callbacks always return ordered latitude extremes while preserving the intended symmetric absolute bands, then validate both ordinary map latitude and True Starts behavior for every temperature/variation combination.
  Confirmed KI#260 (SAS-specific low grid-percent contract failure): 5449 replaces upstream Planet's explicit positive map-size override/world-size path with `SAS_PLANET_GENERATOR_GRID_PERCENT` and documents/clamps the define as valid in `[1,100]`. The selected WorldInfo terrain-cell dimensions are rounded by `int((dimension * percent + 50) / 100)`, so the lower documented range is not safe. Merely keeping both dimensions positive already requires Arena 5%, Duel 4%, Tiny 3%, Small 3%, Standard/Large/Huge/SAS24 2%, and SAS32+ 1%; e.g. Standard 1% yields `fW=fH=0`, after which the spacing-expansion path divides by `float(fW)`. Other low-but-positive combinations can create 4-plot-high maps and supported distribution seeds on a north/south edge, exposing inherited helpers such as `getTilesAround()` whose ascending deletion of invalid Y-neighbor indices is unsafe at the poles. These are consequences of the same too-small scaling contract rather than separate ordinary-default defects: an exhaustive current 40% check across every world size, 1-18 continents, separation 1-10 and all explicit distributions found no edge seed. Preferred repair: enforce a generator-safe minimum resulting grid (not merely nonzero arithmetic), validate dimensions after scaling/spacing expansion, and keep the default 40% behavior unchanged.
  Confirmed KI#261 (inherited Planet/C2C builder-pruning index defect): `TileBuilder._getPossibleGrowTiles()` scans `self.builders`, appends each exhausted builder's positional index to `rem_ind`, then ignores those saved indices. With `x = len(rem_ind)` it repeatedly executes `self.builders.pop(x)` while decrementing x, which removes positions `len(rem_ind)-1 ... 0`, not `rem_ind[...]`. Thus a mixed set such as productive builders at positions 0/1 and an exhausted builder at 2 records `[2]` but deterministically removes productive position 0 and leaves exhausted position 2. This helper is live in ordinary continent growth whenever the possible-tile cache is refreshed; the surrounding fallback searches can sometimes recover productive active tiles later, so the defect is not described as a guaranteed generation crash, but it corrupts the intended frontier-pruning state and biases/needlessly degrades continent growth. Upstream C2C Planet contains the identical loop. Preferred surgical repair: remove the recorded indices in descending order, e.g. pop `rem_ind[x]`, while keeping `builderList` bookkeeping consistent with any direct builder removal.
  Current repair and validation: KI#257 retains the feature-spread source through an independent managed `CyMap.plot()` wrapper while keeping transient neighbor/standalone reads on `sPlot()`, and derives river direction from shortest wrap-aware deltas. KI#258 classifies the percentage-scaled Planet grid in plot-space by restoring the 4x4 terrain-cell area conversion. KI#259 derives both callbacks from one ordered/clamped absolute latitude extent. KI#260 centralizes percentage scaling and floors its output at the smallest default-supported `6x4` terrain-cell grid. KI#261 removes recorded exhausted-builder positions in descending order and synchronizes `builderList`. The script passes Python AST/compile and `git diff --check`; focused checks confirm Standard/Huge Smart Selection boundaries, the 1% Arena minimum, both X-seam river directions, the former `-14/+14` latitude case and mixed productive/exhausted builder pruning. Runtime tests produced correct `64x44` Standard and `96x68` Huge maps and completed turn-11 autoplay; the 1% Arena test produced the exact safe `24x16` minimum and likewise ran through turn 11. `PythonErr.log` remained empty.
  Several additional Planet candidates were deliberately not promoted. `probabilityArray` initializes `_maxpower` to a `bArray`, but its malformed fallback is mathematically unreachable for the live positive-weight/getRand range; `ContinentPart.deepMaxTiles` has a mutable default but every live caller supplies its own used-set; the nonstandard fair-resource implementation contains obvious defects but is dead because current `addBonuses()` hardcodes `bonusGeneration = "standard"`; the internal `worldArray` wraps Y although the actual map does not, but normal continent/pole constraints yielded no independent north-south gameplay connection; and the apparently reversed `world._dimensions[0]` use is correct because `worldArray` deliberately stores reversed dimensions.
  Two real inherited robustness oddities are retained as audit notes rather than extra KIs. `getTilesAround()` removes invalid Y-neighbor entries with ascending `pop` indices, which can itself mis-remove or overrun at an actual pole, but normal 40% Planet geometry does not place generated land there; demonstrated reachability is through KI#260's unsupported-in-practice tiny results. `peakIntersect`/`hillIntersect` use `ax != ox and ay != oy`, which ignores more than the single `(ox,oy)` tile named by the comment, but upstream Planet has the same predicates and its v0.42 changelog explicitly says the hill-intersection change was intended to produce more hills close together; no harder supported-game failure was established, so this remains inherited generator behavior rather than a manufactured KI.
  The center-distribution grid-expansion branch can also return a Python float width because `required_width` is floating-point. Civ4's sequence-to-`vector<int>` bridge asserts that returned sequence items are `PyInt` before coercing with `PyInt_AsLong`; many explicit Around Center combinations at the current 40% scale exercise that branch. This violates the debug/type contract and release coercion truncates the sub-cell fraction, but no substantial release-game failure beyond that rounding was proven; it is kept as a repair/audit note rather than KI#262. Similarly, `getSelValue()` computes but fails to return its stored default for an out-of-range custom-option value, but valid current indices do not take that branch and stale/foreign option state belongs to the already-recorded Base AdvCiv KI#106 Simple Game lineage.
  The remaining live map callbacks close cleanly around these findings: rivers/lakes/bonuses/goodies and starting-plot assignment intentionally delegate to the default implementation; standard resource generation keeps the broken fair-resource branch unreachable; normalization delegates under that standard mode; `getTopLatitude()` is invoked before `getBottomLatitude()` by the DLL so the latter's lack of its own climate-init call is harmless; map-option persistence is name/value based and broad exception handling prevents a missing cfg from becoming a generation failure; WrapX=True/WrapY=False matches the intended topology. No sixth current Planet root met the evidence threshold.
  Certified cursor advances through practical 5449 / eaa9a887f9. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5448 / 3dbee874c3.


SAS-C103 - practical 5448 through 5445 map curation / stock-map import audit - COMPLETE; KI#262-KI#266 FOUND
  Reviewed 4 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5448 / 3dbee874c3, 5447 / 30d39de13d, 5446 / c5cc188373 and 5445 / a1db61111f.
  Practical 5448 closes clean-current. Its Simple Game visibility / WorldPicker changes are deliberate map-pool presentation choices. The temporary Boreal/Lagoon stale-option guards are the older harmless-default masking lineage deliberately removed by practical 5450 so Base AdvCiv KI#106 surfaces instead of silently continuing with stale options; they are not a separate survivor. The shared almost-all-land grid profile is reduced to Arena 2x2 through Huge 15x11 terrain cells and those values survive current `SAS_WorldSizeUtils.py`; AdvCiv multiplies custom map-script grid cells by four into actual plots, so even Arena is 8x8 rather than a zero/single-row geometry. No consumer-specific sizing invariant failure was established. Practical 5447 and 5445 are likewise deliberate WorldPicker/Simple Game curation changes (Earth2/Big_and_Small/Hemispheres and Boreal respectively) with no gameplay-state defect.
  Practical 5446 cannot be treated as mere alphabetical-ordering XML: it copies seven complete stock/Firaxis-style scripts into `PrivateMaps` (`Archipelago`, `Balanced`, `Big_and_Small`, `Continents`, `Custom_Continents`, `Islands`, `Medium_and_Small`), making roughly 3,200 lines of executable Python part of the mod-local runtime surface. All seven birth copies were audited against their current descendants and, where needed, Base AdvCiv 1.14 / stock controls. `Balanced.py` and `Continents.py` close cleanly. The already-known missing-call `if pPlot.isFreshWater:` predicates in Archipelago/Islands belong to inherited KI#249 and are not counted again. Five independent current roots survive the broader audit.
  Confirmed KI#262 (broader inherited tiny-island coordinate copy/paste defect): current `Big_and_Small.py`, `Medium_and_Small.py`, `Custom_Continents.py`, `Mixed_Continents.py` and `not_too_Big_or_Small.py` each independently roll `tinyWestLon` and `tinySouthLat`, but then compute `tinySouthY = int(self.iH * tinyWestLon)` instead of using the latitude roll. `tinySouthLat` is therefore dead, and every one-to-four supposedly random tiny-island patch origins is forced onto an X/Y-correlated diagonal band rather than receiving independent longitude/latitude placement. This is live map topology, not an unused cosmetic value: the multilayered-fractal pipeline lays these tiny-island patches first and later water in overlapping region passes does not erase already-created land. The exact mistake is inherited from stock Firaxis Big/Medium/Custom Continents code; Base AdvCiv 1.14 also carries it in its `Mixed_Continents` / `not_too_Big_or_Small` descendants. Current `RandomScriptMap.py` provides a clean control by rolling the same latitude and correctly using `tinySouthY = int(self.iH * tinySouthLat)`. Preferred repair: change all five live copies to use the latitude variable and preserve their existing RNG calls/order so only the intended coordinate dependence changes.
  Confirmed KI#263 (inherited `Medium_and_Small` eastern-continent grain copy/paste defect): option 0 defaults to Random. In that path the script makes two explicit independent rolls, `iContinentsGrainWest = 1 + dice.get(4, ...)` and `iContinentsGrainEast = 1 + dice.get(4, ...)`. The western continent correctly consumes the western roll, but the eastern continent's `generatePlotsInRegion` call also passes `iContinentsGrainWest`; `iContinentsGrainEast` is never read afterward. Thus the advertised/randomized two halves are forced to share one continent grain, discarding the east roll; with independent 1..4 rolls they would differ 75% of the time. The stock BtS source has the same copy/paste line and current SAS retains it. Current `RandomScriptMap` again supplies a useful control: its Medium-and-Small implementation rolls west/east grains independently and passes `iContinentsGrainEast` to the eastern region. Preferred repair: pass `iContinentsGrainEast` in the eastern continent call without changing the surrounding shift/island logic.
  Confirmed KI#264 (SAS-specific 48-civ compatibility regression exposed by practical 5743, latent inherited table assumption): `Custom_Continents.beforeGeneration()` defaults custom option 0 to Random and reads the actual `countCivPlayersEverAlive()`. Its `byPlayerIndex` weighting table, inherited from stock, contains keys only 2..18 and is indexed directly. The original 18-civ engine kept ordinary high-player setups within that range; practical 5743 changed SAS `MAX_CIV_PLAYERS` from 18 to 48 without extending this table. A normal 19-48-player Custom Continents game using the default Random continent count therefore raises `KeyError` before `iNumConts` / `cont_data` are initialized. On a clean generation the subsequent Python `generatePlotTypes()` also fails because those globals were not established; AdvCiv's `CvPythonCaller::generatePlotTypes` reports no successful override and `CvMapGenerator::generatePlotTypes` explicitly fills every plot with `PLOT_LAND`. In a reused Python process stale globals are also possible, so silent reuse is no safer contract. This corrects the earlier C032 certification of 5743: the DLL-capacity change itself is valid, but it exposed an inherited fixed-18 map-script assumption that the earlier pass did not trace. Preferred repair: define deterministic Random-continent weighting for every supported current player count (or a documented generalized/clamped rule) and guard generation so an unsupported count can never fall into the all-land error fallback.
  Confirmed KI#265 (SAS-specific 48-slot sparse/high-ID compatibility regression exposed by practical 5743, broader inherited fixed-ID assumption): several current inherited maps correctly keep their custom topology capped at <=18 actual players/<=6 teams, but then incorrectly assume those alive entities must occupy IDs 0..17. Archipelago and Islands build `player_list` only with `for plrCheckLoop in range(18)` and then shuffle `countCivPlayersEverAlive()` entries. Tectonics has the same 0..17 player enumeration inside its <=18-player custom start path. A legitimate sparse Custom Game with e.g. two alive civs in slots 0 and 18 passes the topology-count gate but produces only one enumerated player; the next shuffle indexes an empty list. Archipelago/Islands then fail `assignStartingPlots` before custom assignment and AdvCiv falls back to the generic C++ start path; Tectonics' per-player `findStartingPlot` callback likewise fails before its all-player custom assignment and `CvPlayer::findStartingPlot` falls back to the generic search. Custom Continents One-Per-Team similarly builds `team_num` only for team IDs 0..17; a live team ID 18..47 can index beyond that list in `findStartingPlot`, losing the intended team-continent constraint for that player. Practical 5743 made IDs 18..47 legitimate by raising `MAX_CIV_PLAYERS` to 48 (and the corresponding civil-team range), so this is a SAS compatibility regression even though the literal loops are inherited. Preferred repair: enumerate the full current max player/team ID ranges or construct actual alive-ID lists, while retaining the scripts' intentional <=18-region / <=6-team topology limits rather than pretending their static templates support 48 custom regions.
  Confirmed KI#266 (inherited `Custom_Continents` six-continent template shift-axis typo): supported template `(6,3)` has paired outer/inner layers whose variance fields are structurally mirrored. Continent 0's outer and inner layers both use horizontal `+0.63`; continent 5's pair both use horizontal `-0.63`; continent 4's outer layer also uses horizontal `-0.63`, but its corresponding inner layer uniquely stores `[... horzVar=0, vertVar=-0.63 ...]`. With no binary shifts the inner layer starts nested inside its outer shell. Once shifts are enabled, the outer shell moves horizontally while the inner layer does not, and/or the inner layer is shifted through the south edge instead; three of the four `(xShiftRoll,yShiftRoll)` states therefore break the intended nested/mirrored pairing. The exact bad field placement is present in stock Warlords/Firaxis Custom Continents and survives current SAS (and even later third-party "improved" descendants), so it is inherited rather than SAS-created. The surrounding template symmetry makes the intended entry unusually low-ambiguity: the inner layer should use horizontal `-0.63`, vertical `0`, matching its outer layer and the other mirrored pair. Preferred repair: correct that one axis field and validate all four binary-shift states for explicit six-continent variant 3.
  Several tempting 5446 candidates were deliberately not promoted. Islands resets `iPass = 0` inside its retry loop, making the fallback machinery objectively incapable of relaxing its first-pass constraints (and current AdvCiv's compatibility `startingPlotWithinRange` no longer adds an independent relaxation), but no ordinary supported configuration was proven to require that retry; it remains a robustness note like the analogous Grid branch. Custom Continents One-Per-Team reconstructs vertical but not horizontal template shift in its start-region constraint; balanced template geometry still leaves a large overlapping valid region and no hard start failure was established. Two `(2,4)` Custom Continents layers can shift slightly beyond the eastern edge, but intended wrap behavior/fix direction is less clear than KI#266 and no independent hard failure was proven. These remain audit notes rather than manufactured KIs.
  A final current-map sparse-ID scan found no other `range(18)` player/team enumerator beyond Archipelago, Islands, Tectonics and Custom Continents. One separate `range(iPlayers)`/`getPlayer(assignLoop)` shape exists in `BTG_Cross.py`, whose own practical source commit is still ahead of this backward cursor; it is intentionally deferred to that file's proper audit rather than half-reviewed here.
  Local reconciliation against current source confirmed all five C103 roots: the five longitude-for-Y expressions and clean RandomScriptMap latitude control, Medium and Small's discarded east-grain roll and clean control, Custom Continents' 2..18-only weighting table, all four fixed-ID loops, and the asymmetric `(6,3)` inner-layer shift entry. Certified cursor advances through practical 5445 / a1db61111f. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5444 / c02570f328.


SAS-C104 - practical 5444 through 5440 Deer-option / map-state / fail-fast / Military Advisor tranche - COMPLETE; no new defect
  Reviewed 5 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5444 / c02570f328, 5443 / b90811a0d3, 5442 / be75be8cf7, 5441 / accf8702eb and 5440 / 955b2298bd.
  Practical 5444 closes clean-current. Arboria and Boreal each gain a visible Deer Percentage option with exact 0/10/25/50/75/100 values while their pre-existing wrap choice moves consistently to custom-option index 1 and remains the one hidden trailing option. `dice.get(100) < iDeerPercent` is exact for Civ4's 0..99 map RNG: 0 never passes, 100 always passes and intermediate values have the advertised percentage. The current descendants retain this option plumbing; later 5448/5450/5451 stale-option changes belong to the already-recorded Base AdvCiv KI#106 Simple Game lineage rather than a 5444 regression.

  Practical 5442's broad `getInfoTypeOrFail` conversion was audited as executable code rather than accepted from the title.   Practical 5440 is a clean Military Advisor geometry correction. At the historical hardcoded 1920x1080 layout, the previous minimap height cap inherited `H_TEXT = 797` px and could overlap the bottom Great General bar. The new cap is derived from that bar's actual Y position: `H_MAP_MAX = Y_GREAT_GENERAL_BAR - 10 - Y_MAP = 757` px, leaving the intended 10 px separation. If the aspect-ratio-derived minimap is clipped, its width is recomputed from the map aspect ratio and the Great General bar width is then synchronized to that final minimap width. No negative geometry or mismatched minimap bounds occurs in the historical supported layout; later resolution-aware rewrites supersede the hardcoded shell without evidencing a repair to this 5440 calculation.

SAS-C106 - practical 5438 BTG Lagoon birth/import - COMPLETE; four current defects confirmed
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5438 / 551ef0b93a. This imports the modified `BTG_Lagoon.py` from Beyond the Game 2.43 into SAS. The full executable map contract was audited against the current descendant, the original BTG PublicMaps archive supplied by the user, AdvCiv/Civ4 map-generator/DLL callback behavior and every later Lagoon history hunk through the current tree. Four independent current roots survive; several initially alarming resource/null/team candidates were explicitly rejected after engine-level tracing.
  KI#267 is an inherited BTG Lagoon compact-player-ID/custom-start mapping bug. `beforeGeneration()` creates `player_list = [0, 1, ..., countCivPlayersEverAlive()-1]`, shuffles those compact template IDs, and stores a list whose length equals the alive-player count. `findStartingPlot(playerID)` later indexes that list with the real player ID through `shuffledPlayers[playerID]`. Sparse but legitimate active slots therefore break even under the original 18-slot engine: e.g. actual alive player IDs 0 and 2 create a two-element assignment list, then player 2 indexes beyond it. AdvCiv's Python-callback wrapper treats the failed override as unsuccessful and `CvPlayer::findStartingPlot()` falls back to the generic C++ finder, so the game need not crash but Lagoon silently loses its handcrafted start-region contract for the affected player and can mix custom and generic starts. The original BTG 2.43 Lagoon contains the same compact-list/real-ID mismatch verbatim, and no later SAS Lagoon commit repairs it. Surgical direction: enumerate the actual ever-alive player IDs and store/look up template assignments by those IDs rather than by compact count position.
  KI#268 is an inherited BTG Lagoon Top-v-Bottom unequal-team unbounded-retry bug. Start Position defaults to Top v Bottom. `BTPTopBottomTwoTeams(False)` correctly returns unless there are exactly two civ teams, at least four players and an even total player count, but it never requires the two teams to have equal sizes. It randomly selects one team as `teamOne`, then for every `teamOne` player currently in the top half repeatedly reshuffles all players until it finds a different, opposite-team player whose start is in the bottom half. In a legitimate 4-player 3-vs-1 game, Lagoon's four-player start template has two top and two bottom starts; if the three-player team is selected as `teamOne`, at least one of its players must remain top while at most one opposing player exists. Once that lone opponent is top (or has already been swapped top by an earlier repair), no opposite-team bottom candidate exists and the `while` condition can never become false. The same impossibility generalizes to unequal even-sized two-team games when the larger team is selected. There is no attempt bound or eligibility precheck, so map normalization can hang indefinitely. Original BTG 2.43 contains the same helper. Surgical direction: require equal team sizes for this normalization or construct finite eligible top/bottom swap lists and fall back safely when a complete separation is impossible.
  KI#269 is a SAS-specific Lagoon world-size adaptation regression left behind when Arena was inserted before Duel. The inherited generator comment/formula assumes the original BTS ordering in which Large had raw world-size value 4: `iShiftBias = 4; iShift = iShiftBias - int(CyMap().getWorldSize())`. Arena already existed at SAS practical 5311 before Lagoon was imported at 5438, so current runtime indices are Arena=0, Duel=1, Tiny=2, Small=3, Standard=4, Large=5, Huge=6 and the later SAS sizes continue upward. Yet `iWidthSize`/`iSmallWidthSize`, which control Lagoon's solid/partial outer land bands and bridge geometry, still use the old raw index. Compared with the original BTG tuning, Tiny through Huge therefore use the next-larger legacy bucket (e.g. Large computes 8/6 rather than the original Large=4 tuning of 7/5 at default Size Factor), with SAS24+ drifting farther beyond the old range. Practical 5854/5855 repaired Lagoon's separate direct `getGridSize()` key/Arena problem but never touched this internal arithmetic; it survives current source. Surgical direction: derive the tuning from aligned `WorldSizeTypes`/SAS world-size helpers or an explicit per-size table rather than subtracting the raw enum index.
  KI#270 is an inherited BTG Lagoon wrapped-plot shift/desynchronized-coordinate bug. Lagoon uniquely calls `FractalWorld.shiftPlotTypes()` after hand-building its central disk, outer land bands and bridges when the user selects Cylindrical or Toroidal wrapping. `shiftPlotTypes()` intentionally chooses a best seam and can relocate the entire `plotTypes` array by a nonzero X/Y offset. Lagoon never applies that offset to its later coordinate-dependent logic: `findStartingPlot()` still searches fixed fractional template regions such as 10%/50%/90% X and 25%/75% Y; `addRivers()` still sculpts the six-tile central peak/desert patch around the unshifted map center; and the default Extra Resources option still calls `BTPresourceFromCenter()` around that same old center. Thus a supported nonzero wrapped shift moves the handcrafted Lagoon geography while leaving starts and center follow-up features/resources anchored to pre-shift coordinates. The original BTG 2.43 file has the same `shiftPlotTypes()` call and fixed-coordinate follow-up logic; sibling hand-designed BTG Cross/Grid do not rely on this shift. Surgical direction: either disable plot shifting for Lagoon's wrapped modes or persist/apply the chosen shift to every downstream handcrafted coordinate/template.
  One separate Lagoon birth regression is historical-only and therefore not assigned another current KI: the imported `getGridSize()` dictionary did not account for SAS's already-existing Arena/runtime-index shift. Practical 5854/5855 explicitly repaired the direct world-size lookup and later refactors moved it to aligned SAS helpers. KI#269 is distinct because the internal `iShiftBias` shape arithmetic was missed by that repair and remains live.
  Fixed/validated with GPT-5.6-Sol after ChatGPT-5.6-Sol's archaeology: Lagoon now maps actual ever-alive player IDs directly to shuffled template IDs for its portion of KI#267; KI#268 replaces the unbounded retry with equal-team validation and finite misplaced-player pairing; KI#269 replaces raw enum subtraction with an explicit aligned Arena-through-SAS48 width table; and KI#270 keeps Lagoon's handcrafted wrapped plot array fixed so all downstream coordinate logic remains aligned. Static parsing, the focused Ruff error gate, exact Duel-through-Huge width comparison and finite unequal/equal-team logic cases pass. A Huge Lagoon game generated and completed an 11-turn autoplay with an empty refreshed `PythonErr.log`; the sparse-ID, unequal-team and wrapped-alignment paths were validated from focused source/logic checks rather than separate targeted runtime reproductions. Later checkpoints broaden KI#267 to Team Battleground, Ring, Hub and Inland Sea; the Team Battleground portion is also fixed/validated, while Ring/Hub/Inland Sea remain deferred.
  Several plausible candidates were rejected rather than promoted. The apparent Barbarian-team leak in `BTPTopBottomTwoTeams` is false because `BARBARIAN_TEAM == MAX_CIV_TEAMS` and the Python loop iterates only `range(MAX_CIV_TEAMS)`, excluding that sentinel ID. `BTPForceEnrichFood()` lacks its sibling's explicit null-plot guard, but every live Lagoon call uses `bMainLandOnly=True`; AdvCiv's null CyPlot returns neutral values and area -1, so the biggest-land-area predicate rejects an out-of-bounds candidate before mutation. The 5442 Center Resources Whale concern remains rejected after deeper checking: setting Coast on a land plot converts its plot type to water, and AdvCiv has an explicit resource-bearing-lake exception allowing an adjacent city to train a Work Boat. Forced nonstandard resource/terrain combinations are part of BTG's explicit hand-sculpting routine and no independent broken gameplay contract was established. Finally, the inherited option label `Balanced - Including Marble` is stale under AdvCiv 1.14 because BonusBalancer explicitly no longer eliminates Marble; option 2 still differs by adding extra food balancing, so this is retained only as a wording/audit note rather than a generator defect.
