SAS-C107 - practical 5437 BTG Cross birth/import - COMPLETE; KI#267 broadened and KI#271-KI#274 FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5437 / 3f17c40b84. This imports and substantially adapts `BTG_Cross.py` from Beyond the Game 2.43: BTG-only options are removed, the option surface is reshaped, grid sizing is replaced, player limits are lifted to `gc.getMAX_CIV_PLAYERS()`, and region assignment is changed to reuse the fixed 4/5/7/9 Cross templates through modulo indexing for high-player/SAS48 support. The full birth file, current descendant, every later Cross history hunk, current AdvCiv DLL/map-generator contracts and the user's original BTG 2.43 PublicMaps archive were compared. That original archive was particularly useful for separating dormant/inherited BTG behavior from defects created when SAS exposed or scaled it.
  KI#267 is broadened from Lagoon to an inherited BTG Lagoon/Cross compact-player-ID/custom-start mapping root. Cross actually begins correctly by enumerating the real ever-alive IDs into `player_list` and `shuffledPlayers`, explicitly commenting that open slots must be handled. But its Numerical Order and the two team-positioning branches discard those real IDs and use `playerID = assignLoop`. A legitimate sparse setup with alive IDs 0 and 2 therefore assigns a start to compact/dead player slot 1 and can leave real player 2 without any start. This is more severe than Lagoon's per-player lookup failure because Cross overrides the global `assignStartingPlots()` callback: current `CvGame::assignStartingPlots()` returns immediately when that Python callback reports success, and `CvPlayer::setStartingPlot()` contains no alive-player or cross-player uniqueness guard. Thus C++ does not subsequently fill the omitted real player. Original BTG Cross contains the same compact-ID use in its selectable Numerical/Together branches, while the correctly built real-ID shuffle proves the intended open-slot-aware contract. Surgical direction for the shared KI#267 root: carry actual ever-alive player IDs through every assignment mode; never substitute compact loop positions for `PlayerTypes`, and do not return successful global Python assignment until every actual participating civ has a valid start.
  Cross's KI#267 portion is fixed/validated with GPT-5.6-Sol after ChatGPT-5.6-Sol's archaeology: preserve Cross's ordered actual-ID list before shuffling and use those IDs in both duplicated Numerical/team-positioning assignment blocks. Full-shuffle behavior remains unchanged. Static parsing and the focused Ruff error gate pass. A Huge Cross game generated and completed an 11-turn autoplay with all 16 players alive and an empty refreshed `PythonErr.log`; the sparse-slot branch itself was validated from focused source/logic checks rather than a dedicated sparse-slot runtime setup. The Team Battleground portion is also fixed/validated, while later Ring/Hub/Inland Sea portions remain deferred.
  KI#271 is a SAS-specific BTG Cross high-player modulo-region/positional-mirroring duplicate-start regression. Practical 5437 deliberately replaces original direct region indices with `assignLoop % iNumRegions` (or modulo `best_regions`) so player counts can exceed the fixed physical 4/5/7/9 hub templates. In `Mirrored Hubs = Shape and position` and `Mirrored`, however, Cross finds only the first player's relative `(dX, dY)` inside its hub and then assigns every later player the same relative point in that player's selected region. Once modulo assignment cycles back to a region already used, the same region plus the same `(dX,dY)` is the exact same plot. For example, with the default five-region template, six FFA players and positional mirroring, assignments 0 and 5 both select region 0; ordinary current region widths give the first start a nonzero 20%-inset `dX`, so the sixth player is deterministically placed on the first player's plot. `CvPlayer::setStartingPlot()` does not reject a plot already used by another player. Original BTG did not contain the modulo high-player reuse; this is part of the SAS48 adaptation. Even outside positional mirroring, modulo reuse contradicts the map's new SAS description that each player receives its own small hub with an extra central prize hub. Surgical direction: do not reuse a positional-mirrored hub as though it were a distinct start slot; either provide enough distinct regions, independently search an additional valid start within a reused region, or safely fall back before assigning when physical hub capacity is exceeded.
  KI#272 is a SAS-specific activation/exposure regression in the Cross Positioning option. Original BTG 2.43 already contains label index 3, `Outside positions, crossed`, and a dormant `else` branch, but `getNumCustomMapOptionValues()` reports only 3 values for Positioning, so only indices 0..2 are selectable. Practical 5437 changes that count to 4 and exposes Crossed in SAS without implementing distinct behavior. In the live two-team positioning branch, Together and Crossed both execute exactly `playerID = assignLoop` and `reg = assignLoop % iNumRegions`; `iTeam` is computed immediately beforehand but never used. The two now-selectable choices are therefore behaviorally identical. Surgical direction: either implement a genuinely crossed team/region mapping using actual player/team IDs, or restore the three-value option surface until such behavior exists.
  KI#273 is a SAS-specific Cross source-mirroring dimension regression caused by replacing BTG's template-aligned grid contract. Original BTG derives grid dimensions as exact multiples of 3 for the 4/5/9-region thirds templates and multiples of 4 for the seven-region quarter template, so every source/target hub rectangle has matching dimensions. Practical 5437 replaces that with new world/high-player sizing while retaining the literal fractional templates and copy loops. The script chooses one widest region as `region_duplicated`, then for every target iterates the target's full `iWidth/iHeight` and reads `sourceWest + x, sourceSouth + y`; `mirrorizeMap()` later repeats the same source-relative scheme for plot types, terrain, rivers, features, bonuses and improvements. When rounding produces e.g. a 10x9 source and a 9x10 target, the target's tenth row reads a row outside the selected source rectangle and imports unrelated neighboring map data rather than a mirror. This is already reachable in the 5437 birth sizing for selectable distance modes, and current tuning broadens it: exhaustive current Arena-through-SAS48 checks across all four Start Distance tables and 4/5/7/9 layouts found 45 affected `(distance, world-size, region-layout)` combinations spanning 15 distance/world-size pairs; the quarter-based seven-region layout remains aligned. Current Normal Arena, for example, is 28x28 plots and its default five-region template contains a 10x9 source/9x10 target mismatch. Surgical direction: preserve equal mirror-source/target dimensions by construction, or explicitly normalize/crop/transform coordinates within each rectangle instead of assuming identical rounded sizes; assert this invariant before copying.
  KI#274 is an inherited BTG Grid/Cross source-region double-generation/ranking/bookkeeping defect discovered while using the original BTG archive as Cross provenance. Both original BTG scripts first choose a `region_duplicated` source hub, append it to `regions_in_use`, and generate its outer/core fractals. With `Mirrored Hubs = No`, they then iterate the complete physical region set through `remaining_regionsTwo`, which still includes that same source, append it again and independently generate it a second time. `MultilayeredFractal.generatePlotsInRegion()` deliberately ignores ocean when applying a regional pass, so the second source pass cannot restore first-pass land to water; it layers an additional independent land/core fractal onto the source while every other hub receives only one such pass. Under BTG's equal-sized regions the selected source is normally region 0, not the central prize hub, so this is not the intentional central-hub advantage. Mirrored modes avoid the second independent fractal but still append the complete region set after the already-recorded source, leaving `regions_in_use` with N+1 entries for N physical hubs. `assignStartingPlots()` then aliases `region_numbers = regions_in_use`, calculates the source yield twice and ranks only `iNumRegions` entries; the source can therefore occupy two ranking slots while a real hub is omitted, and the global list itself is mutated down while sorting. The same inherited machinery survives current Grid and Cross. This is distinct from SAS-specific KI#247: KI#247 is the later hard Grid capacity/start-loss regression created when a SAS safety guard trusted the inflated raw list length; KI#274 records the older gameplay/map-generation and ranking defect that made that list non-unique in the first place. Surgical direction: generate each open-mode hub exactly once; in mirrored mode record the source only once and keep target IDs distinct; rank a copied list of unique physical region IDs rather than an aliased duplicate-bearing global.
  Several adjacent candidates were explicitly rejected or kept below KI threshold. Cross's inherited missing `isFreshWater()` calls are already part of broad KI#249 and are not recounted. `AI_updateFoundValues(True)` intentionally invalidates cached values; `CvPlot::getFoundValue(playerID)` lazily recomputes them, so the scorer does not remain at -1. Cross's retry counter is initialized outside the retry loop in current SAS, unlike the independently broken Grid version, and can reach its fallback. All 12 current option indices were mechanically checked and are in range. `mirrorizeMap()` is called only when its mirrored-generation globals are initialized. Resource-tail list indexing can be fragile if a malformed/no-start state has already occurred, but no deterministic independent supported configuration was proven beyond the recorded start roots. AdvCiv's compatibility `startingPlotWithinRange()` now always returns false, and Cross's own `min_dist` check uses raw non-wrap-aware axis differences; a theoretical seam-distance discrepancy remains on some toroidal 4/9-region layouts, but no independent hard start collision/launch failure was established, so it is retained only as a robustness note rather than KI#275.
  A final current/history grep for modulo assignments, Positioning option exposure, source-relative copy loops, `region_duplicated`, `regions_in_use`, list indexing, retry loops, resource forcing and map callbacks found no fifth independent current Cross root. The four new mechanisms survive the current descendant. Certified cursor advances through practical 5437 / 3f17c40b84. SAS-C108 - practical 5436 RandomScriptMap import/adaptation audit - COMPLETE; KI#275-KI#277 FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5436 / 393a82a296. Despite the short commit title (`Fix RandomScriptMap.py`), this creates a roughly 2,200-line runtime `PrivateMaps/RandomScriptMap.py`. The birth file was reconstructed from the commit mirror and compared line-by-line with the current descendant, Refar's archived Random Script Chooser 1.25, the BTG-distributed original map, stock BTS map-script controls, every later RandomScriptMap history hunk, current `CvMapGeneratorUtil.py`, world-size helpers/XML and the relevant AdvCiv Python/DLL callback contracts. The executable body is overwhelmingly inherited Refar code; SAS's birth changes mainly import shared world-size sizing and adapt the old six-size assumptions.
  KI#275 is an inherited Refar start-option defect in `isValidBoth()`. The function begins with `gl_isValidCalls += 1`, but `gl_isValidCalls` is never initialized anywhere and the function has no `global gl_isValidCalls` declaration. Python therefore treats the augmented-assignment target as local and attempts to read that uninitialized local immediately, raising `UnboundLocalError` before any map/coast validity test. The exact line is present in Refar 1.25, the BTG-distributed copy, practical 5436 and current SAS. It is directly reachable through RandomScriptMap's human `Player Start` choice `Coastal on Biggest Land`, whose `findStartingPlot()` path passes `isValidBoth` to `CvMapGeneratorUtil.findStartingPlot`. AdvCiv's map callback failure path can continue with generic C++ starting-plot placement, so the practical result is a Python exception plus silent loss of the user's requested custom coastal/Old-World placement rather than necessarily a failed game launch. The counter has no other live use. Surgical direction: remove the obsolete increment, or explicitly initialize/globalize the counter only if diagnostic counting is still desired.
  KI#276 is an inherited Refar embedded Medium-and-Small control-flow typo. The generator rolls `yShiftRoll1` from two values and `yShiftRoll2` from four, then when the latter is greater than 1 tries to coerce it to the opposite binary orientation of `yShiftRoll1`; however both supposed assignments are written as standalone comparisons: `yShiftRoll2 == 1` and `yShiftRoll2 == 0`. They have no effect. Downstream `if yShiftRoll2` tests therefore use the original truthy 2/3 value. When `yShiftRoll1 == 1` and `yShiftRoll2` originally rolls 2 or 3, the intended second orientation is false but the live one remains true, so both halves take the same vertical shift instead of the intended opposite relation. Independent rolls make this 1/2 * 2/4 = 25% of embedded Medium-and-Small generations. Refar 1.25, the BTG copy, the 5436 birth and current SAS all contain the typo; later SAS reformatting commits touched this exact block without changing the comparisons. A current AST scan found these are the file's only standalone comparison-expression statements. Surgical direction: restore the intended assignments (`=`) while preserving the same RNG sequence and orientation logic.
  KI#277 is an inherited Refar Terra quadrant-randomization bound defect. Firaxis' stock Terra uses fixed safe margins before its optional quadrant flips: the New World's eastern longitude 0.35 becomes 0.95 after a +0.60 shift, and Eurasia's southern latitude 0.45 becomes 0.05 after a -0.40 shift. Refar randomizes those base margins but keeps the full shifts: `newworldEastLon` can be 0.36..0.42 and `eurasiaSouthLat` 0.38..0.46. When the relevant flip rolls fire, the New World east edge can therefore become 0.96..1.02 and Eurasia's south edge -0.02..0.06. The horizontal overflow alone is ordinary and world-size-independent: the horizontal flip occurs half the time and 3 of the 7 east-margin rolls are 0.40/0.41/0.42, so 3/14 (about 21.4%) of Terra generations request an eastern region boundary at or beyond map width. Larger maps can also turn the negative fractional south values into negative integer Y coordinates.
  KI#277's consequence was traced through the actual region compositor rather than inferred from comments. `CvMapGeneratorUtil.MultilayeredFractal.generatePlotsInRegion()` explicitly requires regions to remain inside the map, then writes regional results into `wholeworldPlotTypes` using the flat index `wholeworldY * self.iW + wholeworldX`; it does not normalize or coordinate-wrap out-of-range region coordinates. An X coordinate at/above width therefore spills into a following linear row, while negative Y can use Python negative list indexes near the end of the map array. The result is corrupted/misplaced Terra quadrant geometry rather than intentional cylindrical/toroidal wrapping. A deterministic geometry harness preserving the current formulas ran 20,000 RNG seeds for every current Arena-through-SAS48 tier across all embedded RandomScriptMap generator families; Terra was the only family that issued out-of-bounds `generatePlotsInRegion()` rectangles. Fractal, Archipelago, Pangaea, Big+Small, Medium+Small and Hemispheres remained positive and in bounds. Surgical direction: retain Refar's randomized quadrant flavor but constrain the randomized base margins so the subsequent fixed shifts preserve the stock safe bounds, or explicitly transform/normalize region coordinates before compositor indexing.
  Two SAS-side 5436 adaptation problems seen in the birth file are historical lineage already repaired by newer commits and are not recounted. First, `from SAS_WorldSizes import *` leaked the helper's `getGridSize()` into this map-script namespace; the later 5897 helper split/callback-leak repair covers that family. Second, the initial Arena/world-size conversion corrected several obvious legacy tables but left raw `getWorldSize()` threshold assumptions inside embedded generators; practical 5899 later repaired those remaining raw runtime-index sites and the associated current world-size mapping. Those repaired SAS defects are recorded only as birth-history context here.
  Several adjacent candidates were deliberately rejected. `shiftRegionPlots()` computes an ineffective upper clamp for `stripRadius`, but every live RandomScriptMap caller supplies strip widths already within 3..15. Fractal's continent-grain random roll is collapsed to grain 2 but matches the routine's intended/default contour behavior and no broken option contract was established. Terra's small-subcontinent retry loop is probabilistic but has a nonzero valid solution interval on every supported current map size; the apparent `scsWestX` definite-assignment warning is false because the loop executes at least once. Pangaea's zeroed `scVertRange`, several unused local variables and RNG intermediates, and coastal-peak terrain sculpting have no demonstrated runtime failure. The custom `R_TerrainGenerator` remains compatible with current AdvCiv because base `TerrainGenerator.processCustomizations()` detects the overridden `initFractals()` and disables the Earthlike specialization before choosing the subclass's expected fractals. Dynamic RNG bounds and custom-option accesses were also stress-checked; no nonpositive `dice.get()` bound, fixed-18 player enumeration, KI#249-style missing `isFreshWater()` call or fourth independent current defect was established.
  Current-history tracing confirms KI#275-KI#277 all survive the current descendant. The Medium-and-Small typo is preserved even through later commits that reformat that exact block, and the Terra quadrant shifts survive later line-splitting cleanup unchanged. Certified cursor advances through practical 5436 / 393a82a296. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5435 / 2270a70c79 (`Fix Rainforest.py; move it to almost_all_land`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.

SAS-C111 - practical 5433 Earth2 import / staged-start-distribution audit - COMPLETE; KI#280-KI#282 FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5433 / 202d3dbbaf. The mixed commit also contains a Sevopedia indentation-only source hunk and XML/docs, but the substantive runtime change is the 1,320-line `PrivateMaps/Earth2.py` import/adaptation. The birth file was compared against the supplied original BTS Earth2 script, the current SAS descendant, every later Earth2 history hunk, current `CvMapGeneratorUtil.findStartingPlot()`, current world-size helpers/XML/enums and the generated Earth2 region geometry. The explicit 5433 design is unusually clear from the commit message, Python comment, defines and still-current Main Changes Guide: keep classic biggest-landmass starts at low player counts, from 16 players also populate Africa, from 32 populate the full world, and for Huge-and-beyond avoid further map enlargement because Earth2 is already large enough.
  KI#280 is a SAS-specific 5433 middle-stage start-distribution predicate bug. For fewer than 16 players, the new validator correctly preserves the stock behavior by requiring `pPlot.getArea() == iBiggestAreaID`. It then computes an explicit Africa rectangle (`iAfricaWestX/iAfricaEastX/iAfricaSouthY/iAfricaNorthY` and `bInAfrica`) consistent with the stated "then unlock Africa" stage. However the only condition that references `bInAfrica` is `if iNumPlayers < iAfricaMinPlayers and bInAfrica`, which is unreachable because the function has already returned for every `< iAfricaMinPlayers` call. For 16-31 players the live code instead rejects only `bInAustralia or bInAmericas` and accepts every other candidate plot. This therefore unlocks all other non-Americas/non-Australia land areas, not merely the original biggest landmass plus Africa: Earth2 explicitly generates separate British-Isles/Emerald and Japan regions inside that admitted coordinate space. `CvMapGeneratorUtil.findStartingPlot()` applies the supplied validator directly and has no hidden biggest-area restriction; any admitted plot with the highest positive found value can be selected. Surgical direction: in the middle stage admit `(pPlot.getArea() == iBiggestAreaID) or bInAfrica`, and reserve unrestricted `True` for the full-world threshold, preserving the define-driven 16/32 staging.
  KI#281 is a later SAS-specific Earth2 map-size policy regression introduced at practical 5854. The 5433 birth intentionally calls ordinary `sas_lookup_world_size()` on a table ending at Huge `(40,24)`, so absent larger tiers fall back to Huge exactly as the commit states: solve higher-player crowding by widening eligible start landmasses rather than enlarging this already-large map. Practical 5854 replaces that with `sas_lookup_world_size_with_calibrated_sas()` and explicitly says SAS24-48 should scale up from Huge. That generic calibration survives current source even though the 5433 `findStartingPlot()` comment still says better distribution is preferred to forcing larger grids and the current Main Changes Guide still promises "for Huge and beyond world sizes, keep Earth2 at its existing Huge-scale sizing". With current XML default players (Huge 16, then 24/32/40/48), the helper expands the `(40,24)` grid-unit Huge anchor to approximately SAS24 `(48,29)`, SAS32 `(57,34)`, SAS40 `(63,38)`, SAS48 `(70,42)`; map-script grid units are multiplied by four by the engine, so SAS48 is about 280x168 = 47,040 plots versus Huge 160x96 = 15,360 plots, over three times the plot count. This is not merely stale documentation: it directly negates the rationale that created the staged start system. Surgical direction: keep the corrected Arena-through-Huge keys but return them through ordinary `sas_lookup_world_size()` (or otherwise explicitly cap `>= Huge` at Huge) so SAS24-48 reuse `(40,24)` while the 16/32 player thresholds distribute starts.
  KI#282 is a SAS-specific Earth2 Arena regional-grain world-size adaptation bug. `EarthMultilayeredFractal.generatePlotsByRegion()` retains the stock six-entry Duel..Huge grain matrix and changes only the direct lookup to `sas_lookup_world_size(sizekey, sizevalues)`. At 5433, runtime XML already had Arena inserted before Duel while the hardcoded enum was still the old six-value sequence, so this incomplete adaptation initially produced shifted buckets (notably runtime Tiny selected the old Small grain tuple). Practical 5971 correctly aligns `WorldSizeTypes` with XML, but the Earth2 grain table itself remains untouched. In current source Arena is therefore the missing key and `sas_lookup_world_size()` falls back to the largest present key, giving the smallest map Huge's `(ScatterGrain, BalanceGrain, GatherGrain) = (5,2,1)` instead of the Duel/Tiny small-map tuple `(3,2,1)`; Duel and Tiny immediately drop back to `(3,2,1)`. This bottom-tier non-monotonicity is the same incomplete-world-size-table failure mode seen in adjacent imported scripts. Because Earth2's intended policy is to cap geometry at Huge, the existing Huge fallback for SAS24+ grain is not independently classified as wrong here. Surgical direction: add Arena explicitly as `(3,2,1)` (and optionally explicit SAS aliases to Huge for clarity) while preserving the legacy Duel..Huge progression.
  The remainder of the imported Earth2 runtime body was audited without establishing a fourth defect. The current 30 fixed geographic region rectangles are all positive and in bounds across the supported geometry; even Arena's smallest generated region remains positive, and `CvFractal::fracInitInternal()` handles positive one-row/one-column regional dimensions without a separate zero-dimension path. The western/eastern hemisphere coordinate filters cover the generated Americas/Australia footprints as intended for the full-world gate; the slight broadness of those rectangles is companion geometry to KI#280 rather than an independent failure. The stock repeated `generatePlotsInRegion()` calls are deliberate layered landmass shaping, unlike the unintended BTG source-region duplication in KI#274. The terrain generator uses XML terrain-grain change rather than stale raw world-size arithmetic; current strict terrain tags exist; no custom fixed-player-ID enumeration, `sPlot` aliasing, unbounded retry, invalid RNG bound or region-array overflow was found. The inherited `NiTextOut("Generating Terrain (Python Terra) ...")` label is cosmetic only.
  Later-history tracing cleanly separates the three roots. KI#280's staged-start code is never semantically edited after 5433. KI#281 begins specifically in 5854's grid-calibration hunk and is carried through later helper-key/import refactors (5855/5864/5897/5966/5971) without restoring the fixed-Huge policy. KI#282's grain matrix is likewise never semantically edited; 5971 fixes the global enum alignment but leaves this legacy subset missing Arena. Certified cursor advances through practical 5433 / 202d3dbbaf. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5432 / 2addf7daeb (`Fix base AdvCiv bug of most bonuses ... first Civ4 launch`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.


SAS-C112 - practical 5432 Arboria/Boreal KI#105 first-launch bonus-fractal guard audit - COMPLETE; no new defect
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5432 / 2addf7daeb. Its executable changes are in two runtime files, `PrivateMaps/Arboria.py` and `PrivateMaps/Boreal.py`. The accompanying Known Issues documentation describes an intermittent first-Civ4-launch case in which custom forest/boreal bonuses such as Deer could be absent until returning to the main menu and generating again. The commit adds the same defensive state pattern to both scripts: module-level `food = None` plus stored `food_iW/food_iH`, `_initFoodFractal()` called from `beforeGeneration()`, and `_ensureFoodFractal()` immediately before the custom `food.getHeightFromPercent(...)` bonus-placement thresholds. The ensure helper recreates the fractal only when it is missing or its remembered dimensions differ from the active map.
  This closes clean-current rather than establishing another KI. The normal-generation semantics were traced through the DLL rather than inferred from the empirical issue description. `CvMapGenerator::generateRandomMap()` invokes the map script's `beforeGeneration()` unconditionally before plot/terrain generation, and bonus callbacks occur later through `addGameElements()` after rivers/lakes/features. Thus on every normal fresh/regenerated map the `food` fractal has already been freshly initialized with the active map dimensions and map RNG before the first custom `addBonusType()` call; `_ensureFoodFractal()` is a no-op on that live path. The parent scripts already initialized `food` in `beforeGeneration()`, so the 5432 change does not alter ordinary placement thresholds, RNG consumption or bonus predicates when the documented callback order holds.
  A historical diagnosis nuance is preserved without promoting it to a defect. `_ensureFoodFractal()` can detect absent state and dimension mismatch, but cannot prove freshness when a hypothetical stale fractal came from a previous map with the same dimensions. Therefore the 5432 comment that the guard removes dependence on callback/state timing is broader than what the guard alone can establish. That limitation is immaterial under the actual engine callback contract because `beforeGeneration()` refreshes the fractal regardless of dimensions. No supported callback path was found that reaches Arboria/Boreal custom bonus placement without that initialization.
  Practical 5441 later deliberately removes the temporary module-level `None`/dimension cache and `_ensureFoodFractal()` while preserving direct `food` initialization in `beforeGeneration()`, alongside a broader KI#105 state-locality refactor. C104 already audited that descendant change against the DLL order and certified it clean-current. No intervening 5433-5440 commit semantically touches these two map scripts, and current Arboria/Boreal retain the simple 5441 form. The empirical KI#105 history therefore remains useful as repaired/debugging lineage, but 5432 contributes no surviving runtime-source defect and does not change the existing defect counters. Certified cursor advances through practical 5432 / 2addf7daeb. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5431 / 30dd3faa8c (`refactor multilists`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.

  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5431 / 30dd3faa8c. Despite the broad file count, the substantive pattern is narrow: replace repeated multilist literals with named constants and remove repetitive local `buttonCalculate = 1` / `columnIndex = 0` assignments. `_sevopedia_helpers.py` defines `SEVOPEDIA_MULTILIST_NUM_LISTS_AUTO_CALCULATE = 1` and `SEVOPEDIA_MULTILIST_COLUMN_INDEX_AUTO = 0`; every affected Sevopedia page already imports that helper with `from _sevopedia_helpers import *`. `CvInfoScreen` intentionally mirrors the same values as `self.iTopCitiesWonderNumListsAutoCalculate = 1` and `self.iTopCitiesWonderColumnIndexAuto = 0` rather than introducing a Sevopedia dependency.

After substituting the new named constants back to literal `1`/`0` and ignoring comments/indentation, every `addMultiListControlGFC` and `appendMultiListButton` call retains the same arguments, order and widget IDs. Building/Unit/Terrain/Feature panels that place numeric modifier text beneath buttons preserve their `iButtonIndex` progression, `maxButtonsPerRow` calculations and text coordinates; no button, prerequisite, unit, promotion, leader or wonder row is removed. Current source still defines the shared constants as exactly `1` and `0`, and later Sevopedia work reuses those constants rather than repairing a semantic mistake from 5431.
  The only visually non-mechanical hunk is `SevoPediaTerrain.placeRelevantUnits()` for Peak. This is readability cleanup, not a branch or display-content change. No missing helper import, name collision, circular dependency or multilist-column behavior change was established. SAS-C114 - practical 5430 SAS define launch-guard lifecycle audit + practical 5429 non-runtime main-menu art step - COMPLETE; no new defect
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5430 / 3bf18c08ec. Practical 5429 / c4fad2d421 contains no runtime C++/Python change (main-menu NIF/DDS, `CIV4ArtDefines_Interface.xml` path/comment and documentation only), so it advances the practical certified cursor without consuming the frozen 1,317 runtime-source denominator.
  Practical 5430 introduces `SASDefineGuard.py`, integer/string sentinel defines in `GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml`, and five verification entry points: BUG/Firaxis `preGameStart`, `CvEventInterface` `OnLoad` and `GameStart`, Civilopedia creation, and Sevopedia show. The guard itself caches only a `CyGlobalContext()` wrapper plus constant names/expected values; sentinel values are read afresh on every `verify_or_raise()` call. `CyGlobalContext` holds a reference to the singleton `CvGlobals::getInstance()`, so constructing the wrapper while Python modules are first imported does not snapshot the pre-XML define table. This matters because `CvEventInterface.initAfterReload()` explicitly documents that the module is initially imported while the global context is not yet ready; no define access occurs during that phase.
  The DLL define-load path was traced directly. `CvXMLLoadUtility::SetGlobalDefines()` loads the normal/developer/AdvCiv define files and then explicitly calls `ReadGlobalDefines("xml\\GlobalDefines_advciv_sas.xml", cache)` before the later BBAI/AI define files and before `GC.cacheGlobals()`. The sentinel therefore resides in the live `FVariableSystem` by the time ordinary game/UI Python entry points can use it. `CvGlobals::getDefineINT` returns its default 0 for a missing integer, while `getDefineSTRING` initializes its return pointer to NULL and returns that missing value; the Python-side comparisons fail safely and the guard's `%s` diagnostic can represent the missing string rather than dereferencing it. In debug builds the DLL's missing-define assertions may surface first, which is complementary fail-fast behavior rather than a release-only semantic difference.
  The earliest decisive runtime hook is safe with respect to load order. BUG extends Firaxis' `CvAppInterface.preGameStart` in `EXTEND_INSTEAD` mode, and the wrapper calls `SASDefineGuard.verify_or_raise()` before invoking the original function. BUG's own lifecycle documentation states that `CyGlobalContext` is not ready during the early `Init` event, but BUG initialization is deliberately deferred until `OnLoad` or `PreGameStart`; `OnLoad` precedes `PreGameStart`, which in turn precedes `GameStart`. Firaxis' original `preGameStart()` is already at the UI/game-start phase: it initializes dynamic font icons, preloads the tech chooser and shows the main interface. Thus a valid launch has completed define loading before the sentinel is checked, while a mismatched/missing sentinel aborts the wrapper before the normal main interface is initialized. No supported valid-start path was found where this creates a false fatal.
  A wording/bridge nuance was investigated and rejected as a separate defect. `CvDllPythonEvents::postEvent()` records the boolean result of `callFunction("CvEventInterface", "onEvent", ...)`, but void reporters such as `reportGameStart()` do not propagate that result farther; therefore an exception raised specifically at the `GameStart` event may be logged/swallowed by the native event bridge rather than terminating the engine process. The same event hook is nevertheless redundant defense: `PreGameStart` already runs the same verification before Firaxis' interface initialization, and save-load sequencing also reaches `PreGameStart` after `OnLoad`. The guard's documented practical outcome (bad configuration cannot continue as a normally initialized/playable SAS session) is therefore established without requiring each redundant event reporter itself to terminate the executable.
  The Civilopedia/Sevopedia hooks are likewise late and side-effect-free. `CvScreensInterface.createCivilopedia()` verifies before constructing either Pedia implementation, and `SevoPediaMain.pediaShow()` verifies again before TraitUtil/category state initialization. Repeated verification only performs two live variable-system reads and comparisons; it mutates no define or Pedia/game state. PitBoss does not create a bypass: BUG can skip some component registration for a PitBoss host, but the globally installed `preGameStart` wrapper itself still exists and runs its guard before Firaxis' original host-aware routine. Current source retains the same sentinel values and runtime verification design; later history adds build/CI checks around the sentinel but no runtime repair of a 5430 lifecycle flaw was found.
  Practical 5429 was inspected because it sits between runtime-source commits. It renames the static main-menu DDS, swaps the background NIF to the Middle-earth-mod-derived static-wallpaper asset, updates the corresponding Interface XML path/comment, deletes no-longer-needed auxiliary DDS files and edits documentation/screenshots. No executable C++/Python is changed, and the XML path points to the renamed asset present in the same commit. It is therefore outside the primary runtime-source denominator and establishes no source defect. Certified practical cursor advances through 5429 / c4fad2d421. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5428 / 0b9ab6d22f (`Fix Donut.py`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.

SAS-C115 - practical 5428 Donut import/adaptation and wrapped-center contract audit - COMPLETE; KI#283 FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5428 / 0b9ab6d22f. The commit adds the complete 330-line Firaxis/Sirian `PrivateMaps/Donut.py` runtime map script to SAS, with SAS world-size helper integration and an explicit Arena-through-Huge square grid table. Current Donut retains the same core generator and follow-up coordinate logic; later changes make strict terrain lookups, Simple Game visibility/stale-option handling, helper relocation, enum-key alignment, and deliberate Large/Huge size reductions, but do not alter the defect below. Stock BtS Donut from the Firaxis/Civ4CE source contains the same core pattern, establishing inherited rather than SAS-specific provenance.
  KI#283 (inherited Firaxis Donut wrapped-plot seam shift/fixed-center terrain desynchronization): Donut hand-builds its circular plot geometry around `iCenterX = width/2` / `iCenterY = height/2`, then calls the generic `FractalWorld.shiftPlotTypes()` whenever the selected map wrap enables X and/or Y wrapping. The generic seam scorer counts only `PLOT_LAND`; randomly generated `PLOT_HILLS` and `PLOT_PEAK` cells contribute exactly like ocean. Although Donut already leaves an ocean border around the circle, the random hill/peak pattern can therefore make the adjacent wrapped seam (`width-1` and/or `height-1`) score below seam 0. `shiftPlotTypesBy()` then rotates the completed plot array by one tile, while `DonutTerrainGenerator` independently recomputes the center, radius and hole from the original unshifted map coordinates and never receives the chosen seam offset. The requested Center Region plot disk and its terrain disk consequently no longer coincide on those wrapped generations. The user-selectable Desert center makes the consequence especially explicit: some ordinary ring cells inside the old fixed hole are forced to Desert while the corresponding shifted center-disk cells on the opposite edge of the hole receive normal Grass/Plains terrain. Peaks/Hills center choices similarly shift the special plot disk away from the fixed terrain disk; Ocean center shifts the structural hole itself away from the map center. Flat wrap is unaffected because no shift is requested.
  Reachability was verified against the actual current algorithms rather than a generic random surrogate. A direct replay of current `CvRandom` (the 32-bit LCG in `CvRandom.cpp`), `CvFractal::fracInitInternal/getHeight/getHeightFromPercent`, Donut's current hill/peak thresholds, and `FractalWorld.findBestSplitX/Y` produces legal wrapped Donut generations whose best split is `width-1`; for example a current Tiny Donut is 32 x 32 plots and a legal fractal-entry RNG state of 0 selects X split 31. The RNG state transition uses odd multiplier 1103515245 and is therefore bijective modulo 2^32, so such entry states are reachable from ordinary game/map seeds after the fixed preceding RNG calls. Replays across current Tiny-through-SAS48 dimensions repeatedly produce either seam 0 or the adjacent `dimension-1` seam; the issue is not dependent on an invalid grid size. On Tiny, shifting the strict Desert-center hole by one X tile makes the original and shifted hole masks differ on 22 plots; the boundary mismatch grows with larger radii. Toroidal mode can independently apply the same one-tile Y displacement.
  Preferred surgical repair is to disable generic plot shifting for Donut (e.g. generate its plot types with `shift_plot_types=False`), because this handcrafted circular map already guarantees an ocean margin at the map boundaries and has coordinate-dependent follow-up generation centered on fixed map coordinates. An alternative would require carrying the exact X/Y seam offsets through every center-dependent follow-up calculation, which is needlessly broader. This root is conceptually similar to inherited BTG Lagoon KI#270 but is not the same code/provenance and was independently proven in Firaxis Donut.
  The remainder of the map was audited without finding a second current root. The smallest current Arena override becomes 20 x 20 actual plots under AdvCiv's custom-map 4-plots-per-cell contract, giving positive radius 6 and hole radius 3; all current SAS calibrated tiers remain safely positive. `waterArea()` on a land plot returns the largest adjacent water area wrapper, and the script's `isNone()` / `isLake()` checks correctly enforce a non-lake coastal start rather than dereferencing null state. Custom-option indices and wrap/resource defaults are internally consistent; balanced bonuses use the stock `BonusBalancer` contract; climate/sea-level controls are intentionally disabled; the no-Ice and optional-Jungle feature overrides are deliberate. No fixed-18 player enumeration, stale raw world-size indexing, unbounded retry, missing predicate call, malformed regional rectangle or additional current Donut defect was established. Historical world-size/helper-key cleanups remain previously audited lineage rather than new 5428 findings. Certified cursor advances through practical 5428 / 0b9ab6d22f. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5427 / 1f51abbd83 (`Fix Boreal.py; move it to almost_all_land maps`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.


SAS-C116 - practical 5427 Boreal import/adaptation + shared CvMapGeneratorUtil Y-seam audit - COMPLETE; KI#284 FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5427 / 1f51abbd83. The commit imports the full Firaxis/Sirian `PrivateMaps/Boreal.py` generator and moves Boreal into the SAS almost-all-land map classification. The birth adaptation already improves one important stock limitation: its custom starting-plot allocator enumerates `range(gc.getMAX_CIV_PLAYERS())` and shuffles the actual alive player IDs rather than retaining Firaxis' literal `range(18)`, so Boreal does not join the KI#265/KI#267 high-ID/sparse-slot families. Its custom Arena-through-Huge compact grid table and later calibrated SAS-size descendants remain positive/capacious through current SAS. The full plot/terrain/feature/start/bonus callbacks and all later Boreal history were audited before closure.
  KI#284 (inherited Firaxis CvMapGeneratorUtil Y-seam strip-size guard dimension typo, whole-world + regional variants): current `FractalWorld.findBestSplitY(stripRadius)` computes `stripSize = 2 * stripRadius`, allocates/scans a Y score array of length `self.iNumPlotsY`, wraps every weighted target with `% self.iNumPlotsY`, but decides whether the strip is too large using `if stripSize > self.iNumPlotsX: return 0`. The symmetric X routine correctly tests `self.iNumPlotsX`. The same copy/paste mistake is independently duplicated in `MultilayeredFractal.findBestRegionSplitY(iRegionWidth, iRegionHeight, stripRadius)`: all scoring/wrapping is over `iRegionHeight`, yet its early guard tests `iRegionWidth`. Base AdvCiv 1.14 and public Firaxis/Civ4 map-utility source retain the same two wrong-width guards, establishing inherited provenance rather than a SAS change.
  The whole-world path has a direct ordinary current-SAS reproduction in Boreal. `FractalWorld.stripRadius` is 15, hence the seam kernel has 30 entries. Current Tiny Boreal is 8 x 5 custom terrain cells = 32 x 20 actual plots and Small is 10 x 6 = 40 x 24; Toroidal wrap is a supported custom option. Correct Y logic must therefore return shift 0 for both because 30 exceeds the map height, but the wrong X guard sees widths 32/40 and runs the scoring kernel instead. A direct replay of the current `CvRandom` 32-bit LCG, `CvFractal::fracInitInternal/getHeight/getHeightFromPercent`, Boreal's continent/hills/peak thresholds and current seam-weight calculation found nonzero Y splits for 91 of the first 100 legal Tiny fractal-entry RNG states and 95 of the first 100 Small states (e.g. entry state 0 chooses Tiny split 19 and Small split 23). Since each LCG transition is bijective modulo 2^32, these are reachable ordinary map-generation states, not synthetic impossible seeds. The 30-entry kernel wraps around a 20/24-row score array and double-counts rows instead of taking the explicit too-small-dimension fallback.
  This shared helper defect is broader than Boreal, so it is deliberately one KI rather than one number per affected map. Confirmed current whole-world dimension/wrap combinations with the same `width >= 30 > height` behavior include Global Highlands Arena/Duel and the shared compact profile's Standard tier in Rainforest, Fantasy Realm and Lakes, in addition to Boreal Tiny/Small. `HintedWorld` inherits the same FractalWorld shift method when callers explicitly request shifting. The regional copy can analogously affect any `MultilayeredFractal` region whose width admits the strip while its height does not; that routine shifts the local regional array, so the wrong dimension is part of the same root even where global map dimensions are large. The neighboring `shiftRegionPlots` upper-clamp oddity (`min(15, iStrip)` followed by `max(3, iStrip)`) was also checked: no current map call supplies `iStrip > 15`, so no independent current defect was promoted from it.
  Preferred surgical repair is the two symmetric one-token dimension corrections: `findBestSplitY` should compare `stripSize > self.iNumPlotsY`, and `findBestRegionSplitY` should compare `stripSize > iRegionHeight`. This preserves the existing explicit small-dimension fallback and leaves all legal X behavior unchanged.
  The remainder of Boreal produced no second current root. Its player shuffle uses the real alive IDs and scales to the 48-civ DLL; the custom start finder/pocket opener is inherited Highlands logic and current calibrated map sizes provide ample candidate area. The opener chooses its nearest prior start with raw non-wrapped Euclidean deltas even when Cylinder/Toroid is selected, so it can carve a longer-than-necessary corridor instead of the shortest wrapped route, but it still crosses/removes an impassable barrier and no independent failed-start, hang or null-start configuration was established; retained as a robustness note only. The feature generator retains only one live `CyMap.sPlot()` wrapper at a time, so it does not reproduce KI#257's simultaneous-static-wrapper alias bug. River terrain upgrades and custom Forest placement are internally coherent.
  The custom bonus subsystem was checked against current XML and its historical renaming. Boreal intentionally hand-places Deer/Gemstones/Wheat/Sheep through regional fractal bands and can bypass ordinary random-spawn terrain restrictions; bonus-linked improvements remain valid and this is inherited regional-map behavior rather than an inaccessible-resource defect. Practical 5427 did import stock resource strings `BONUS_CORN`, `BONUS_CLAM` and `BONUS_GEMS` even though SAS had already renamed those types to `BONUS_MAIZE`, `BONUS_MOLLUSCS` and `BONUS_GEMSTONES`, temporarily defeating the intended custom elimination/placement branches for those renamed resources. Practical 5452 / 031a95cc7c explicitly updates all three Boreal strings, so that is recorded as repaired historical adaptation lineage, not a new current KI. The 5432/5441 food-fractal lifecycle sequence and 5444 Deer Percentage option were already independently certified; no duplicate issue was created here. Certified cursor advances through practical 5427 / 1f51abbd83. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5426 / c4c7d14c2b (`Fix Arboria.py`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.

SAS-C117 - practical 5426 Arboria import/adaptation audit - COMPLETE; no new defect
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5426 / c4c7d14c2b. The commit imports Firaxis/Sirian `PrivateMaps/Arboria.py` into SAS and adapts its square world-size callback to the then-current shared SAS sizing helper. The birth file was compared against the public BTS Arboria source and the current SAS descendant, and the complete topology/terrain/features/options/normalization/custom-bonus surface plus every later Arboria-touching history entry was reviewed before closure. No new current defect was established.
  Arboria's fixed `HintedWorld(16,8)` is a hint lattice rather than a 16 x 8 plot-size assumption. Current `HintedWorld` computes the legal hint-grid shape for the selected wrapping mode before `fracInitHints`: Cylindrical uses 16 x 9 hints, Toroidal 16 x 8, and Flat 17 x 9 after the non-wrapped border rows/columns are added. Those shapes satisfy the hint-fractal grain search, and the hints are interpolated onto the actual map dimensions. The smallest current Arena override is 4 x 4 custom terrain cells = 16 x 16 actual plots, still positive and valid; Duel-through-Huge and the calibrated SAS24/SAS32/SAS40/SAS48 tiers simply provide more interpolation resolution. The continent seed is deliberately constrained away from the forced polar-ocean hint rows, and the requested 50% continent target remains below the available non-polar hint capacity in all wrap modes. No HintedWorld assertion, zero-dimension region, out-of-range RNG bound or malformed plot-array path was found.
  The apparent double-shift concern was also checked rather than inferred from KI#270/KI#283. `HintedWorld.__doInitFractal()` may shift the hint lattice to a low-land seam before constructing its continent fractal, and Arboria then requests the ordinary `FractalWorld.generatePlotTypes(..., shift_plot_types=True)` map-array seam shift. Unlike Lagoon or Donut, Arboria has no later fixed-coordinate center/region contract that must remain aligned with the pre-shift geometry: terrain, Forest/Jungle and Silver/Deer placement are generated from independent full-map fractals/plot properties, and no custom start rectangle is retained. Thus a legal seam shift does not desynchronize downstream logic. Shared KI#284's wrong Y strip-size guard is already recorded separately and is inert for Arboria's square current grids because width equals height.
  Current option/resource behavior was audited through the later 5444 Deer Percentage insertion. Arboria now exposes Deer Percentage values 0/10/25/50/75/100 and keeps wrap as the trailing hidden option; every `getCustomMapOption()` use matches the two-option table. Eligible Deer plots first satisfy the inherited custom fractal bands plus Forest + flatland constraints and are then retained with the requested exact 0..100 map-RNG percentage; Silver placement is independent. The inherited `silver = ("BONUS_SILVER")` / `deer = ("BONUS_DEER")` expressions are strings rather than one-element tuples, but the outer `type_string in forest` check is exact tuple membership over only Silver and Deer before either substring membership is reached, so no other bonus can be misclassified. Hand placement puts Deer on flat Forest and Silver on hills; their associated improvements remain valid, and no inaccessible-resource state was proven. The normalization overrides that preserve Arboria's Forest-paradise character are stock intentional behavior.

SAS-C118 - practical 5425 Wheel import/adaptation + inherited starting-distance/forest-variety audit - COMPLETE; KI#285-KI#287 FOUND
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5425 / 8993525573. The commit imports/adapts Firaxis/Sirian `PrivateMaps/Wheel.py` for SAS world sizes and the 48-civilization DLL, while adding Wheel to the land-heavy map classification. The 1,398-line birth file was reconstructed from the frozen commit mirror and compared locally against the user's archived original Vanilla/Warlords Wheel scripts, current Wheel, current/original sibling Hub and Ring, current `CvMapGeneratorUtil.py`, current start-placement C++ and the exact pre-SAS AdvCiv refactor that moved Python map callbacks into `CvPythonCaller`. Every later Wheel history hunk through the current tree was searched before closure. Three independent current roots survive: one older AdvCiv API-semantics regression exposed by Wheel, one SAS-specific >18-player Wheel adaptation omission, and one low-severity inherited Firaxis forest-art boundary defect shared by Hub/Ring/Wheel.
  KI#285 is an inherited/pre-SAS AdvCiv negative `minStartingDistanceModifier()` contract regression introduced at AdvCivPreSAS practical 1648 / 553b1a0f8d. Before that refactor, Firaxis/BtS `CvPlayer::startingPlotRange()` called the map script and applied `iRange *= max(0, lResult + 100); iRange /= 100`, so negative map values were deliberately meaningful: `-95` means 5% of the ordinary spacing range, `-35` means 65%, etc., while values below -100 clamp at zero. Practical 1648 moved the callback into `CvPythonCaller::minStartingDistanceMod()` but changed the semantics to `return std::max(0, toInt(lResult));`; `CvGame::updateStartingPlotRange()` then still performs `iRange *= 100 + minStartingDistanceMod(); iRange /= 100`. Every negative Python value is therefore clamped to zero before the +100 step and becomes an unintended neutral 100% multiplier. Current SAS retains this exact C++ path. A local current-PrivateMaps sweep finds 30 scripts with at least one negative return, including Arboria, Big_and_Small, Boreal, BTG_Lagoon, Custom_Continents, Donut, Earth2, Fantasy_Realm, Global_Highlands, Great_Plains, Hemispheres, Highlands, Hub, Inland_Sea, Lakes, Mirror, Mixed_Continents, not_too_Big_or_Small, Oasis, Rainforest, RandomScriptMap, Ring, four SAS custom maps, Team_Battleground, Terra, Water and Wheel. Wheel/Hub/Ring's `-95`/`-50` pair is therefore currently indistinguishable to the engine even though the scripts explicitly use it to switch between tightly packed handcrafted-region starts and fallback placement.
  KI#285 has direct current start-selection consequences rather than being dead map metadata. `CvGame::getStartingPlotRange()` feeds `CitySiteEvaluator`/starting-found-value distance scoring through `startingPlotDistanceFactor`, and map-script `CvMapGeneratorUtil.findStartingPlot()` obtains/evaluates those found values while selecting starts. The original reduced-distance contract is especially important for dense regional/team maps that deliberately return values as low as -95. Preferred surgical repair is to preserve the 1648 caller split while restoring Firaxis semantics at the callback boundary, e.g. `return std::max(-100, toInt(lResult));`; the existing caller's `100 + value` then exactly reproduces the original lower clamp while retaining all positive modifiers. This finding adds a fifth targeted cross-lineage trace to the tracker because the SAS Wheel audit led directly to the pre-SAS 1648 source regression.
  Current repair and validation: `CvPythonCaller::minStartingDistanceMod()` now clamps the returned modifier at -100 rather than 0, while `CvGame::updateStartingPlotRange()` retains the refactored `100 + modifier` application. This exactly reproduces the inherited BtS percentage contract for negative, zero and positive values without modifying any map script. Focused boundary checks and `git diff --check` pass; the Release DLL compiled successfully. Turn-11 runtime tests passed for `-95` on 48-player Arena Longworld and 18-player Standard Wheel, inherited `-10` on eight-player Standard Arboria, and a generic Large Pangaea baseline, with complete configured rosters and an empty `PythonErr.log`.
  KI#286 is a SAS-specific Wheel >18-player default-start-fallback omission created by the 5425 48-civ adaptation. Stock Wheel simply sets `bSuccessFlag=False` above 18 players and falls back. SAS intentionally keeps Wheel plot generation usable by capping its handcrafted geometry to 18 regions while retaining the actual player count separately: for `iActualPlayers > 18`, `beforeGeneration()` sets `bSuccessFlag=True`, `bUseDefaultStartPlacement=True` and `iPlayers=18`. `assignStartingPlots()` then builds the 18 region centers and safely enumerates the real ever-alive IDs through `MAX_CIV_PLAYERS`, assigning at most 18 of those IDs to template slots via `playerTemplateByID`, before allowing the C++ default placement pass. `minStartingDistanceModifier()` correctly recognizes `bUseDefaultStartPlacement` and returns the fallback value. However, Wheel's live `findStartingPlot()` tests only `if bSuccessFlag == False` and omits the new fallback flag. C++ default assignment still invokes the map script's per-player `findStartingPlot()` first, so the omission materially overrides the intended fallback: in a 19-player game 18 randomly selected real players remain constrained to Wheel template-center windows while the remaining player is merely constrained to the biggest landmass; at 48 players the split is 18 constrained versus 30 loose. This is neither the advertised/default all-player fallback nor a one-hub-per-player Wheel layout.
  KI#286's intended repair has an unusually strong local sibling control. Current Hub and Ring use the same SAS `bUseDefaultStartPlacement` design and both guard their `findStartingPlot()` with `if bSuccessFlag == False or bUseDefaultStartPlacement == True: return CvMapGeneratorUtil.findStartingPlot(playerID)`. Their birth adaptations contain that guard as well; Wheel alone omitted it. The Wheel comment itself says `Set up for maximum of 18 players! If more, use default implementation.` Preferred surgical repair is therefore to mirror Hub/Ring's two-condition fallback check. KI#285 currently masks Wheel's -95 versus -50 spacing distinction, but it does not mask KI#286's live per-player validity-window constraints; the two defects are independent.
  KI#287 is a low-severity inherited Firaxis Hub/Ring/Wheel forest-variety transition-boundary arithmetic defect. The shared feature generator intentionally assigns forest art variety 2 inside the center rectangle, variety 0 outside `pineWest/East/South/North`, and variety 1 in the intermediate band. Current forest art XML maps those varieties in order to `TreeLeafy`, `TreeEvergreen` and `TreeSnowy`, giving a clear Snowy center -> Evergreen transition -> Leafy outer-region intent. West/south boundaries are computed outward from the center with `pineWestX = int(cenWestX * 0.7)` and `pineSouthY = int(cenSouthY * 0.7)`. East/north instead use `grid - int((centerEdge + grid - 1) * 0.35) - 1`, mixing the center coordinate with the full far-edge coordinate rather than scaling the distance from that edge. On symmetric center geometry these formulas place `pineEastX` and `pineNorthY` inside the Snowy center itself, so the east/north Evergreen transition bands are empty while west/south retain them.
  KI#287 is deterministic across the actual Wheel templates, not a single rounding corner. On current Standard Wheel (64 x 40 plots), all 9 Buffered center templates (2-10 regions) and all 17 Normal center templates (2-18 regions) produce nonempty west/south Evergreen bands and zero-width east/north Evergreen bands. A representative Normal 8-player center is X=23..38, Y=14..23; current boundaries become west/east=16/28 and south/north=9/18, yielding west Evergreen X=17..22 and south Y=10..13 but no possible east band after center X=38 or north band after center Y=23. The same formulas are present in the archived original Vanilla/Warlords Hub, Ring and Wheel and survive all three current SAS descendants, establishing Firaxis provenance. Preferred repair is to compute east/north symmetrically from their distance to the far edge, e.g. `pineEastX = (W-1) - int(((W-1)-cenEastX) * 0.7)` and the analogous Y expression, while preserving west/south behavior. This is classified explicitly as a visual map-generation defect rather than a yield/gameplay-balance defect.
  Existing KI#284 also has a substantial Wheel consumer surface, but no duplicate issue is created. Wheel's custom regional generator repeatedly calls `shiftRegionPlots` with strip radius 3 (and 5 for the center). Across current Arena-through-SAS48 dimensions and the 2-18 region templates, many region/core/buffer/center rectangles are wide enough to pass the erroneous width-based Y-strip guard while shorter than the Y strip itself, exactly the shared `findBestRegionSplitY` condition already recorded under KI#284. The C118 audit therefore broadens KI#284's affected-consumer evidence to Wheel while retaining a single shared utility root.
  The remainder of Wheel was exhaustively rejected as additional current defects. Every Normal/Buffered region, buffer, 25%-inset core and center rectangle remains positive and inside the map across Arena through SAS48 and all supported 2-18 region counts; even the tight Arena Buffered cases have valid dimensions. Every derived template-center starting window remains nonempty after Wheel's two-plot edge clamp. The user-facing `2 areas/player - 10 players max` option is independently gated at generation time for <=10 players, so 11+ gracefully uses the normal one-area geometry rather than reading nonexistent buffer data. All six custom-option indices/defaults match their consumers; random bounds and line/spoke divisions are guarded/positive; no unbounded retry, simultaneous `CyMap.sPlot()` alias, raw stale world-size subtraction, malformed strict lookup or out-of-range regional compositor path survives. SAS also fixed stock Wheel's compact-player-ID assumption at birth by enumerating real alive IDs and using `playerTemplateByID`, so it does not reproduce KI#267. Later Wheel commits are visibility/stale-option diagnostics, strict lookup conversion, helper/world-size key migration, formatting/refactoring and calibration plumbing; none repairs KI#285-KI#287. Certified cursor advances through practical 5425 / 8993525573. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5424 / 620880a0f5 (`Fix Tilted_Axis.py; move it to navalheavy maps`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.

SAS-C119 - practical 5424 Tilted Axis import/adaptation + tilted-latitude contract audit - COMPLETE; NO NEW DEFECT
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5424 / 620880a0f5. The commit imports/adapts Firaxis/Sirian `PrivateMaps/Tilted_Axis.py`, adds SAS square world-size handling and moves the script from the land-heavy classification to naval-heavy. The 225-line birth file was reconstructed from the frozen local commit mirror and compared locally against the user's archived original Vanilla and Warlords Tilted Axis scripts plus current SAS source. The audit covered all custom-option/default consumers, square grid sizes and fractal exponents, permanent Y wrapping, plot shifting, terrain/feature latitude, bonus latitude legality, balanced-resource hooks and later helper/world-size descendants. No current defect was established.
  The SAS world-size adaptation is internally coherent. Arena uses the low-resolution `(6,6)` fractal exponent family already used by Duel/Tiny/Small, Standard through Huge use `(7,7)`, and SAS24/SAS32/SAS40/SAS48 intentionally reuse the Huge 25x25 square grid and Huge exponent profile through `sas_lookup_world_size()`. This is an explicit identity-preservation policy rather than accidental missing-key flattening of the kind recorded for Rainforest or Global Highlands. All actual plot dimensions remain square/even and positive, so the tilted latitude formulas' integer half-width divisions are exact.
  A Donut/Lagoon-style wrapped-plot/fixed-coordinate desynchronization was explicitly rejected. Tilted Axis generates its continent fractal with `FRAC_WRAP_Y` and may rotate the plot array along Y, but both custom climate generators intentionally derive latitude from X (`abs(width/2 - x)`). A Y seam shift therefore cannot move terrain/feature climate away from the intended east-west polar axis. Shared KI#284 is likewise inert for this script because every supported Tilted Axis map is square: the erroneous Y-strip guard and the correct height guard receive equal dimensions.
  Bonus latitude also follows the tilted axis correctly even though the Python script does not implement `isBonusIgnoreLatitude()`. Current AdvCiv `CvPlot::calculateLatitude()` explicitly detects the Tilted Axis topology (`!isWrapX() && isWrapY()`) and switches the latitude coordinate from Y to X before applying the map's top/bottom latitude range. `CvPlot::canHaveBonus()` uses that stored latitude for min/max bonus-latitude legality. Resources therefore obey the same east-west climate orientation as terrain/features rather than silently retaining north-south placement. This closes the last plausible inherited contract concern.
  The naval-heavy classification change is consistent with the generator rather than an option-dependent misclassification: the five Landmass Size choices alter continent grain/rifts (Massive Continents through Tiny Islands), while all use the same inherited `FractalWorld.generatePlotTypes()` water baseline. Massive Continents means larger connected land pieces, not a high land-percentage map. Later descendants add strict/stale-option diagnostics and enum-key/helper migration without changing these runtime contracts. Certified cursor advances through practical 5424 / 620880a0f5. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5423 / 9d01f7598c (`Fix Team_Battleground.py; add it to almost_all_land maps`), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.


SAS-C120 - practical 5423 Team Battleground import/adaptation + circular-start/world-size audit - COMPLETE; KI#288-KI#291 FOUND; KI#267 BROADENED
  Reviewed 1 runtime-source SHA: practical 5423 / 9d01f7598c. The commit imports/adapts the roughly 830-line BTS/Ruff `PrivateMaps/Team_Battleground.py`, adds it to SAS's almost-all-land map family, replaces the stock six-tier grid logic with Arena/SAS-aware helpers and expands team enumeration to the live DLL team range. The birth file was reconstructed from the frozen local commit-diff mirror and compared locally against the user's archived original BTS Team Battleground, Vanilla/Warlords/community variants, every later Team Battleground descendant, current `SAS_WorldSizeUtils.py`, `CvMapGeneratorUtil.py`, and the current AdvCiv start-assignment/SPI callback path. No web source was needed; the supplied local map archive contains the relevant upstream controls.
  Existing KI#267 is broadened, without increasing the defect count, from the BTG Lagoon/Cross compact-ID family to an inherited map-script compact-player-ID/custom-start mapping root that also affects BTS/Ruff Team Battleground Round and Donut. `assignStartingPlots()` sets `player_num = countCivPlayersAlive()` and then constructs/shuffles literal player IDs `0..player_num-1` instead of enumerating the actual alive player IDs. With alive slots 0 and 2, for example, the direct circular assignment operates on slots 0 and 1, assigning a start to dead slot 1 while real player 2 is omitted. Current AdvCiv's StartingPositionIteration recognizes the partial script-set state; when the omitted real player's Round/Donut `findStartingPlot()` obtains its NULL start, converts (-1,-1) to an invalid plot index and returns no Python site, SPI reverts the provisional starts and C++ falls back to generic assignment. The game can therefore launch, but the intended Team Battleground circular/team ordering is discarded. This is the same conceptual root as KI#267: compact alive count is not a valid substitute for real sparse player IDs. Preferred repair is to build the shuffle from actual alive IDs across `MAX_CIV_PLAYERS` while preserving RNG ordering as closely as practical.
  Reconciliation note: the Lagoon/Cross and later Team Battleground repairs fix and validate those three KI#267 surfaces, while C121/C122 broaden the same root to Ring, Hub and Inland Sea. KI#267 therefore remains deferred overall rather than counted as fully fixed.
  KI#288 is a SAS-specific Team Battleground scalar world-size-calibration helper misuse introduced at practical 5423. `sas_lookup_world_size_with_calibrated_sas()` is a grid-size helper: when a SAS24/SAS32/SAS40/SAS48 key is absent, it takes the largest base-tier value and unpacks it as `(iAnchorWidth, iAnchorHeight)` before calibrating a two-dimensional grid. Team Battleground correctly passes tuple-valued square grids to that helper in `getGridSize()`, but the 5423 adaptation also passes scalar integer tables at three live sites: Four Corners bridge `shift`, Donut plot-generation `hole_radii`, and Donut terrain-generation `hole_radii`. Arena through Huge have explicit scalar keys and therefore appear fine; SAS24 through SAS48 miss the keys and reach the tuple-unpack path with Huge scalars 10 or 8, raising a Python TypeError. The same misuse exists in the reconstructed 5423 birth file (against the helper's then-current three-argument contract) and survives current source after the helper's API cleanup.
  KI#288 is directly reachable through supported custom-game combinations. SAS24+ Four Corners reaches the scalar bridge lookup during `generatePlotTypes()`; SAS24+ Donut reaches the hole-radius lookup during plot generation and, if generation is allowed to continue/fall back, the corresponding terrain lookup again. AdvCiv's failed Python plot-generation override can fall back to generic/all-land plot generation rather than necessarily aborting the process, but the requested Team Battleground geometry is lost and Python errors are produced. A current repository sweep found no other map passing scalar tables to `sas_lookup_world_size_with_calibrated_sas`; Team Battleground's three sites are the concrete misuse. Preferred repair is not to make the grid helper silently accept arbitrary scalars: use an explicit scalar world-size progression/calibrator or explicit SAS24-SAS48 scalar values appropriate to bridge/hole radius, so the intended physical scale can be reviewed independently of two-dimensional grid calibration.
  KI#289 is an inherited BTS/Ruff Team Battleground proximity-option collapse. The UI exposes three distinct `Team Setting` values: Start Together, Start Separated and Start Anywhere. For Round/Donut, however, only Start Together changes behavior: it re-sorts the already randomized player list by team. Start Separated and Start Anywhere both leave the same randomized list untouched, use the same circular coordinates, and `normalizeStartingPlotLocations()` suppresses default C++ rearrangement for both, making those two choices behaviorally identical. The same collapse also occurs for every Team Battleground shape when more than four teams are alive: `findStartingPlot()` returns true before consulting the proximity setting, and normalization is suppressed for both Separated and Anywhere. The exact Round/Donut code and option labels are present in the archived BTS source and survive current SAS. Preferred repair is to give Start Separated an explicit ordering/placement policy distinct from random Anywhere (for a circle, e.g. deliberately interleave teammates rather than merely declining the Together sort), and decide/document the corresponding >4-team policy rather than exposing two identical selections.
  KI#290 is an inherited BTS/Ruff Round/Donut angular-spacing bug caused by Civ4 Python 2 integer division. The source comment explicitly says starts are `uniformly spread around the circle`, but computes both `base_theta = 360 / player_num * dice.get(1000, ...) / 1000` and `theta = base_theta + playerLoop * 360 / player_num` with integer operands. `360 / player_num` therefore truncates whenever player count does not divide 360, and the base-angle division is truncated too. The first N-1 angular gaps use `floor(360/N)` degrees while the closing wrap gap absorbs the discarded remainder. This is already visible inside the original <=18-player contract (e.g. 11 players use ten 32-degree steps plus a 40-degree closing gap); SAS48 makes it severe (48 players use forty-seven 7-degree steps and one 31-degree gap instead of 7.5 degrees uniformly). The exact arithmetic is inherited from the archived BTS/Ruff script and survives current SAS. Preferred repair is to compute a floating step once, e.g. `fStep = 360.0 / player_num`, and use floating division for the randomized phase as well. This fixes fairness/symmetry but is intentionally not treated as the repair for KI#291 below.
  KI#291 is a separate SAS-specific 48-player compatibility regression in Round/Donut direct circular starts. The original BTS DLL was capped at 18 civ players, and exhaustive evaluation of all 1,000 possible base-angle rolls shows all 18 starting coordinates remain unique even on the same smallest 16x16 Duel circle. SAS expands the executable player capacity to 48 but leaves Team Battleground's radius-only direct `setStartingPlot()` algorithm without a capacity/uniqueness check or fallback. On current 16x16 Arena/Duel, seed-dependent duplicate coordinates begin at 25 players and all 1,000 base-angle rolls are duplicate by 32 players; at 48 players the current integer-angle algorithm produces only 30-34 unique coordinates. Current 20x20 Tiny is guaranteed duplicate at 48 (40-42 unique), and 24x24 Small duplicates in 429 of the 1,000 possible base-angle rolls at 48. Standard and larger have enough circumference for the current 48-player maximum. Recomputing the circles with a mathematically uniform floating angle confirms KI#291 is not merely KI#290's truncation symptom: 16x16 still has only 32-36 unique rounded coordinates for 48 players even with perfect 7.5-degree spacing.
  KI#291 survives the current C++ start pipeline. `CvPlayer::setStartingPlot()` does not reject a plot already used by another player. For ordinary high-count team games, AdvCiv's StartingPositionIteration deliberately bails when `players^2 > 36 * teams`; e.g. a 32-player two-team Arena/Duel game satisfies that large-team bailout, so SPI does not replace the duplicated direct starts. Team Battleground then suppresses normal team-start rearrangement for the normal 2-4-team proximity modes. Thus duplicate starting plots can reach turn 0 rather than being merely a transient Python assignment. Preferred repair is a circle-capacity/uniqueness guard: either choose distinct nearby circumference/ring plots, enlarge/adapt the radius/grid for the requested player count, or hand start placement back to the generic engine when unique handcrafted starts cannot be guaranteed. A floating angle from KI#290 should be applied too, but is insufficient by itself on Arena/Duel/Tiny.
  Existing KI#285 also affects Team Battleground's inherited `minStartingDistanceModifier() = -65`, but no duplicate finding is created here; current AdvCiv clamps that negative value to neutral until KI#285 is repaired. The Team audit also evaluated a possible StartingPositionIteration side-effect: SPI's discarded probe calls mutate `assignedPlayers` once before it bails on a Python-provided site, but that counter is used only modulo 2/4 to rotate the randomized separated-side/corner cycle. The extra increment changes phase but preserves the separation cycle, so no independent defect was established. Round/Donut's awkward actual-plot-coordinate writes into the smaller `HintedWorld` lattice were also traced: flat out-of-lattice writes safely return false, unset hints are randomized, and the terrain pass converts residual interior water to Plains/land; no independent topology/start failure was demonstrated. All custom-option indices, team-ID compaction for non-Round shapes, random bounds, Four Corners/Left-v-Right base rectangles and current square-grid dimensions were checked; no fifth new Team Battleground root survived.
  Later Team Battleground history consists of strict-lookup/stale-option diagnostics, helper import/API migration and formatting/refactoring; none repairs KI#267/KI#288-KI#291. Certified cursor advances through practical 5423 / 9d01f7598c. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 5422 / f80f6258fc (`Ring.py` import/adaptation), intentionally left unopened at this checkpoint.
