SAS-C122 - practical 5421 through 5406 map/import + worker-AI audit - COMPLETE; KI#293-KI#298 FOUND; KI#267 BROADENED
  Reviewed 16 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5421 / f1180bfc25, 5420 / 2424190053, 5419 / 9e72a66f59, 5418 / 32fe9da7ca, 5417 / 3f862dc357, 5416 / 61937cf0e2, 5415 / a32ad525be, 5414 / 7e70020bbb, 5413 / 74aed0721c, 5412 / 54778f0948, 5411 / a595618dd9, 5410 / d87fe00984, 5409 / 196f83d69c, 5408 / f6f4347655, 5407 / 8d9e5a8e38 and 5406 / f9604bf681. The audit used the local generated commit-diff mirror, current source, archived Firaxis/Warlords map scripts and current C++ map/start contracts; no web lookup was required.
  KI#293 is a SAS-specific Oasis compact-grid river-lane bounds regression. The inherited Warlords Oasis river generator uses fixed every-fourth-column Nile-style lanes plus helper probes at `x+1`, and its original world-size dimensions leave that required headroom. Practical 5421 introduces substantially smaller SAS grid sizes without adapting those hardcoded river bounds; practical 5448 later reduces the shared compact profile further. On several supported compact tiers the old final lane can reach the rightmost column even though the helper still probes the next column, and current Arena/Duel can additionally form a negative vertical random range in the retained lane setup. Release behavior can truncate/misplace intended river work while debug builds can assert on the invalid coordinate/range assumptions. Preferred repair is to derive/cap the river lane endpoints and random ranges from the actual plot width/height instead of retaining dimensions tuned to the Warlords grid table. The separate high-player Oasis starting-band crowding eventually hands individual players back to safe generic placement and was retained as a density limitation rather than a second defect.
  KI#294 is an inherited Firaxis/Warlords Mirror two-team special-case bug. The script first determines that the game contains exactly two equal teams, but its special assignment path then treats literal TeamTypes 0 and 1 as those two teams instead of using the actual alive team IDs. A legitimate setup whose two teams are, for example, IDs 2 and 3 therefore passes the equality test on the empty 0/1 slots and subsequently classifies every real player onto the same side. Depending on the randomized mirror branch, the routine can either iterate an empty counterpart list, assign zero starts and return Python success, or index the empty counterpart and raise. AdvCiv's `CvGame::assignStartingPlots()` returns immediately when the Python assignment callback succeeds, so the zero-assignment branch receives no later DLL fill-in pass. Mirror's ordinary three/four-team and generic paths already compact actual team IDs correctly; surgical repair is to construct/use the two actual alive TeamTypes in the special path rather than hardcoded 0/1.
  Practical 5419 Hub closes without a new defect but broadens existing KI#267. Hub, like Ring, builds a compact shuffled player-position list and later indexes it with a real `PlayerTypes` ID; sparse/high-ID players can therefore lose the handcrafted Hub region validator while compact low-ID players retain it. Hub's >18-player fallback is otherwise wired consistently, and exhaustive Arena-through-SAS48 checks kept ordinary/buffered regions, cores, center regions and starting windows positive and in bounds. Existing shared KI#284, KI#285, KI#287 and KI#292 are not recounted. Practical 5418 Maze also closes clean-current: compact-size cell arithmetic stays positive and generated writes remain bounded.
  Practical 5417 Lakes closes clean-current. A plausible fixed-pole/plot-shift candidate was rejected after tracing `shiftPlotTypes()`: the utility shifts Y only when the map wraps Y, so Flat/Cylindrical Lakes keep the deliberately forced polar water rows at the true north/south edges; Toroidal Lakes intentionally has no fixed polar seam. No Lakes-specific survivor was established in this commit.
  KI#295 is an inherited Firaxis/Warlords Inland Sea small-world start-region bug. The <=18-player custom layout forms each rectangular start window around a fractional template center and then clamps its bounds to a two-plot safety margin. In the inherited 17-player template, extreme 0.95/0.05 centers are already too close to the edge on original Warlords Duel 24x16 and Tiny 32x20 actual-plot grids: the safety clamp can produce `south > north`, making the player's predicate rectangle mathematically empty. For Duel four 17-player template slots are affected and for Tiny two are affected; SAS Arena adds still more affected slots but is not the provenance root. Repeated custom scans cannot find a plot in an empty rectangle, so those players abandon the intended Inland Sea ring region for generic/default placement. Surgical direction is to clamp/recenter the template center inside the safety envelope before constructing min/max bounds, guaranteeing every configured rectangle remains nonempty. Inland Sea also broadens KI#267 because its compact shuffled-player array is indexed by real player ID. A lone inherited `0.677` template asymmetry was quantified separately and causes only a small boundary-row difference on some larger sizes; no invalid region or independent gameplay failure was established, so it remains an audit note rather than another KI.
  Practicals 5415, 5414 and 5413 close clean-current. The map-heaviness helper migration preserves the intended built-in classifications; its exact-name matcher can rely on the normal bare `getMapScriptName()` contract even though an old comment suggested path/`.py` tolerance. Ice Age's SAS grid/fractal-grain adaptation remains positive on the smallest tiers; AdvCiv's `CvFractal` clamps the effective smoothing exponent when requested grain exceeds the available internal exponent. Practical 5413 centralizes the compact almost-all-land profile; its later 5448 size reduction is where KI#298 below becomes reachable for Highlands, but no separate zero-dimension or generic profile arithmetic failure is assigned here.
  KI#296 is a SAS-specific worker-AI stale partial-Farm-progress bug introduced at practical 5412. The new future-food adjustment counted every BFC plot with `getBuildProgress(eBuildFarm) > 0` as a Farm already being built by another worker and immediately credited the full future Farm food. Engine semantics do not make positive build progress an active commitment: interrupted build progress is intentionally retained for `DELAY_UNTIL_BUILD_DECAY = 8` turns before it even begins decaying. The fix requires an actual same-owner unit on the plot whose selection group's active head mission is `MISSION_BUILD` for the Farm. The BFC future-food credit and later irrigation-chain reservation/alternate-source checks share this predicate, so abandoned residual progress no longer supplies imaginary food or irrigation while real concurrent Worker coordination remains recognized. Later practical 5906's separate use of plot/player-adjusted improvement yield remains intact. A full turn-436 autoplay exercised 1,437 Worker assignments and thousands of irrigation-chain evaluations and ended normally by Space Race; the exact interrupted-partial-Farm state remains source-verified rather than deliberately reproduced.
  Practical 5411's low-food-BFC heuristic has a real historical weakness but no current survivor: its birth calculation judges natural terrain food without the compensating food from visible bonuses and their improvements. Practical 5976 explicitly replaces that with the shared potential-food evaluator so bonus-specific improvement food offsets poor terrain. Removing the old `isFoodProduction()` exemptions remains coherent because Settler/Worker production pauses growth without removing the city's structural long-term food needs.
  Practicals 5410 through 5407 close clean-current. Great Plains' SAS grid/grain/maxshift tables were checked against current smallest-grid Mississippi/Ozark bounds, including the `x+1` river helper and the fixed-height Mississippi coordinate list; no overrun or current tag defect survived. Practical 5409's first shared world-size utility contains only SAS-prefixed constants/helpers at this point, so the later import-star callback leak has not yet been introduced; its old 52-player constant matches the then-used player-slot model rather than indexing a 48-civ-only array. Fantasy Realm's imported SAS size adaptation and aDebugMap's fixed tiny debug grid do not establish a current runtime defect; Fantasy's later malformed indentation belongs to the already-repaired 5452->5892 historical lineage.
  KI#297 is a SAS-specific Highlands climate-orientation regression introduced directly at practical 5406. Stock Warlords `beforeInit()` declares `global shiftMultiplier` before randomly assigning 0.0 or 1.0, producing the intended 50/50 north-cold versus south-cold orientation. Practical 5406 adds a safe module default but removes the `global` declaration from `beforeInit()`. In Python the randomized assignment is therefore function-local and discarded; every later latitude reader continues seeing module `shiftMultiplier = 0.0`, so current Highlands always uses the north-cold orientation even though it still consumes the RNG roll. No later Highlands commit touches this scope bug. Surgical repair is simply to restore `global shiftMultiplier` inside `beforeInit()`; read-only latitude callbacks need no declaration.
  KI#298 is a separate SAS-specific current Highlands start-capacity regression whose provenance is practical 5448, discovered retrospectively while closing the 5406 descendant. C103 had previously certified practical 5448's shared almost-all-land Arena reduction from 4x3 to 2x2 map-script cells (16x12 to 8x8 actual plots) without establishing a consumer-specific sizing invariant failure; the Highlands deep audit now supplies one and explicitly corrects that earlier conclusion. Current default Highlands makes water from a 5-percent lake fractal and Peaks from the 77th-percentile terrain threshold before custom start assignment. A source-exact replay of current `CvRandom` plus `CvFractal` over 1,000 legal RNG seeds on the 8x8 Arena grid produced only 23-47 non-water/non-Peak plots; every tested state has fewer than the 48 legal city sites needed by the supported 48-civ DLL. AdvCiv's found-value distance logic strongly penalizes already assigned starts rather than freely reusing them, so after legal sites are exhausted `CvMapGeneratorUtil.findStartingPlot()` returns `-1`; `CyMap.plotByIndex(-1)` wraps NULL, Highlands eventually calls `setStartingPlot(NULL, true)`, and the Python assignment callback still returns success, preventing a DLL rescue pass. Preferred repair is to enforce a Highlands/world-size/player-count capacity floor (larger grid or safe generic fallback before direct assignment) rather than allowing a supported 48-player Arena setup on an 8x8 mountainous/lake grid. This finding does not imply that every map sharing the compact profile is broken; only Highlands is promoted here because its current generator and assignment path were source-replayed and proven.
  Current map follow-up and validation: KI#246 caches Longworld's per-generation player-to-plot assignments and skips already-used plots during trimmed-edge fallback; KI#256 makes Equal Islands copy the source feature authoritatively before its matching bonus; KI#267 completes the already-validated Lagoon/Cross/Team Battleground repairs by mapping actual ever-alive IDs directly in Ring, Hub and Inland Sea; KI#286 makes Wheel's per-player validator honor the script's existing over-18 default-placement flag. All six edited scripts pass Python AST parsing, focused source-contract checks and `git diff --check`. Runtime tests produced a `36x16` 48-player Arena Longworld, visibly equal Cold-climate Standard islands including Ice, ordinary Standard Ring/Hub/Inland Sea maps, and a `132x80` 24-player SAS24 Wheel; all completed turn-11 autoplay. The attempted Ring/Hub/Inland Sea setup still produced compact alive IDs `0-7`, so KI#267's sparse-ID path remains validated through focused source/logic checks rather than a claimed runtime reproduction. `PythonErr.log` remained empty.

  KI#299 is a SAS-specific Sevopedia media-state leak introduced at practical 5390. Opening a Sevopedia movie saves the user's `GRAPHICOPTION_NO_MOVIES` value and temporarily forces the option off; normal `closeMoviePlayer()` restores the saved value. The Movie-to-Music toggle, however, directly stops the media, closes the generic overlay and jumps to Music without calling the movie-specific close/restore path. A user who normally has No Movies enabled can therefore play a Pedia movie, toggle to Music, exit from Music and find the graphics preference silently left disabled for the rest of the session. Current descendants retain the exact bypass, and the only restoration site remains the movie-specific close path. Preferred surgical repair is to restore/clear the movie-scoped saved option before any transition out of Movie mode, ideally through one shared helper used by both ordinary close and Movie-to-Music.
  The surrounding 5398-through-5386 media lineage otherwise closes clean-current. Queue/group header rows are presentation-only and not selectable media actions, so inserted headers do not shift playable track routing. A surviving duplicate `setCurrentLabel()` writes the same label twice and is redundant rather than semantically wrong. The generic `playGeneralSound()` fallback lacks a stoppable returned handle, but no ordinary supported media-script path was established where valid direct 2D/3D playback fails while that fallback alone succeeds; it remains an audit note rather than another defect. Practical 5380's script/ID label enrichment preserves the packed playback key and stored track tuple.   KI#300 is a SAS-specific Info Screen History/Timeline spoiler introduced at practical 5382. Religion founding stores a replay major-event message containing the real holy-city name even for observers whose live notification correctly says the religion was founded in an unknown/distant city. Timeline deliberately allows that globally known religion event on a hidden plot and formerly tried to sanitize it by replacing the *current* city object's current name with `Unknown City`. If the holy city was razed before the observer revealed the plot, `pPlot.getPlotCity()` was NULL; the sanitizer therefore had no name to replace and rendered the old replay text verbatim. The repair recognizes the localized founding template through a sentinel city name and reconstructs `TXT_KEY_MISC_REL_FOUNDED_UNKNOWN` when the plot remains hidden or no city survives, independent of current city existence/name. Screenshot 0216 confirms the ordinary visible-city path remains intact; the exact razed-before-reveal branch is source-reviewed rather than directly reproduced. The ordinary raze message does not rescue the knowledge contract: the live raze notification is sent only to teams that reveal the city, and Timeline suppresses an ordinary raze replay on a hidden plot. Later 5859 fixes a separate birth-cache freshness weakness by keying Timeline cache on game turn.
  Practical 5381's shared in-category chart helpers close clean-current. Ranking-bar arithmetic and Leader/Civ/Tech widget routing are mechanically equivalent to the per-page implementations. The new 16-pixel minimum icon-column width can be wider than the old Improvement-specific 10-pixel floor in unusually dense birth layouts, but later 5571/5573 deliberately replace those dynamic capacities with fixed layout-budgeted icon columns; current SAS therefore retains no 5381 overflow root.
  A separate current Leader-hover defect was source-proven while checking practical 5376 but is intentionally held unnumbered and uncounted until its actual birth commit is reached. Several Sevopedia Leader jump buttons pass literal `data2 = 1`; clicking ignores that field, but `CvDLLWidgetData::parseLeaderHelp()` casts it to `CivilizationTypes` and `CvGameTextMgr::parseLeaderTraits()` uses that civilization to substitute trait-affected units/buildings. CivilizationTypes 1 is current Arabia. Current XML therefore gives concrete wrong hovers for leaders whose actual civilization replaces a fast-production class, e.g. Shaka can show ordinary Barracks instead of the Zulu Ikhanda, Pacal ordinary Worker instead of Maya Quick Worker, and Charlemagne/Peter ordinary Courthouse instead of their UBs. Local history traces the first SAS introduction of this hardcoded convention to practical 4732, which is still outside the certified cursor; practical 5376 merely adds another occurrence in Music. Preserve this as a proven prospective root and assign/count it only when 4732 itself is audited, so the certified-through accounting is not contaminated by an unopened older SHA.

The page enumerates Civic and Tech `SpecialistExtraCommerce` plus Building per-specialist `SpecialistYieldChange`, but omits Building `SpecialistExtraCommerces`. This field is gameplay-active and directly matches the panel's scope: `CvPlayer::processBuilding` calls `changeSpecialistExtraCommerce(c, kBuilding.getSpecialistExtraCommerce(c) * iChange)`, `CvPlayer::specialistCommerce()` adds that player-wide value to every specialist's base commerce, and city specialist totals consume that routine. Current XML contains four live examples: Great Library (+1 research per specialist), Temple of Kukulcan (+1 culture), Natya Shastra (+1 culture) and Rock 'n Roll (+2 culture). The Building `SpecialistExtraCommerce` enum map is not currently exposed through Boost.Python, unlike Civic/Tech extra commerce and Building specialist-yield change, so the surgical fix requires a tiny CvBuildingInfo Python getter exposure (or equivalent helper) and Building-commerce rows in `placeYields()`. The Extra Slots side otherwise closes current-safe: Building specialist slots/free specialists and unlimited-specialist Civics are represented, and practical 5692 later adds the VoteSource-specific free-specialist source that was absent at 5331 and remains present today.   Practical 5314 introduces KI#304, a SAS-specific partial signed-16-bit-to-int migration in the found-value pipeline. The commit correctly widens CitySiteEvaluator/AIFoundValue returns, CvPlot's per-player found-value cache, CvArea best-found storage, CvPlayerAI locals/return type and StartingPositionIteration maps/vectors to int, and current source retains those widened types. It also changes CvPythonCaller::AI_foundValue's declared return type to int, but its body still preserves the old `FAssert(lResult <= MAX_SHORT)` and `return safeIntCast<short>(lResult)`. `safeIntCast<short>` is assertion-checked but non-clamping: debug builds assert above signed-short range and release builds still perform the narrowing conversion. Therefore an enabled official `USE_GET_CITY_FOUND_VALUE_CALLBACK` returning, for example, 40000 can feed a wrapped/narrowed value back into the otherwise-int pipeline instead of 40000. The callback is disabled by default, making normal stock SAS unaffected, but PythonCallbackDefines/BUG still support and register this configuration and no source contract marks it unsupported. Whole-current-source inspection found no second surviving found-value `short` container, so this is a narrow missed conversion rather than a broader incomplete migration. The validated repair removes the obsolete assertion/short cast and returns through the same `toInt` conversion used by neighboring numeric Python callbacks; the DLL compiled and an ordinary game smoke test passed.

  KI#305 is a SAS-specific World Size Chart unit/label defect. Practical 5307 creates the chart and labels `GridWidth * GridHeight` as `Grid Tiles`; practical 5311 then derives `Tiles Per Default Player` from the same product. `CvWorldInfo` grid dimensions are map/terrain-cell units, not the final number of playable `CvPlot`s: ordinary `CvMap::reset()` doubles both axes (4x plots), while custom map-script grid overrides can use a different scaling path. For current Standard, XML 39x28 / 8 default players is reported as 137 `Tiles Per Default Player`, whereas the ordinary map becomes 78x56 = 4,368 playable plots, about 546 plots/player. The same root therefore affects both the 5307 `Grid Tiles` label and the 5311 per-player density row; one KI is used rather than manufacturing a second number. The validated repair labels the raw values as `WorldInfo Grid Cells` and `Cells Per Default Player`, uses the compact `Usual Grid Cells W x H` first-row label after screenshot 0181 exposed clipping, and updates the in-game legend/docs without changing the ratios.

  Reviewed 12 runtime-source SHAs: practical 5289 / cc0c5d1e29, 5288 / 347e62f704, 5287 / 121d7739ae, 5286 / f83dcc927d, 5285 / d8a03aaf84, 5284 / 8d9804971f, 5282 / 1f9b99fd95, 5281 / 3e072ae409, 5280 / b5809dd518, 5279 / a728524ed6, 5278 / 79f376ba1c and 5277 / dfb0c84894.   Practicals 5289 through 5284 close clean-current. 5289 is a Victory Screen table-button-height presentation repair. 5288, 5287, 5285 and 5284 normalize to comment/documentation/prose cleanup in runtime source. Its CvCityAI substitutions cache the same owner/team objects as the replaced repeated calls (`kOwner.getTeam()` is the city's team); old suspicious expressions such as `GET_TEAM(getOwner())` that predate this commit are left untouched rather than introduced by the optimization and therefore remain for their older birth archaeology. Foreign Advisor cached text is explicitly keyed by `CyGame().getCurrentLanguage()` and rebuilt when the advisor is opened after a language change, while Sevopedia's newly reset selectable-index maps directly fix stale arrow-navigation state.
  KI#306 is a SAS-specific stale per-turn player-wide maintenance-ranking cache introduced by practical 5281 in `CvCityAI::AI_buildingValue`. The optimization caches the best/second-best city maintenance (`getMaintenanceTimes100`) and count above the government-center threshold under only `(player, game turn, number of cities)`. Civ4 processes a player's cities sequentially; inside each `CvCity::doTurn`, `doGrowth()` occurs before production choice, and `changePopulation`/`setPopulation` immediately calls `updateMaintenance()`. Consequently an earlier non-capital city can populate the Forbidden Palace/government-center maintenance cache, a later city can grow and change its maintenance without changing game turn or city count, and that later city's `AI_chooseProduction`/`AI_buildingValue` reuses stale empire rankings. This can alter whether a city passes the high-maintenance government-center gate for that turn. The validated repair removes only the maintenance cache and scans live city values on the rare non-Palace government-center candidate path after its existing eligibility/pressure gates; the separate top-production-city cache remains unchanged. The DLL compiled and the game smoke test showed no AI-production or general runtime issue.
  Practical 5280 closes clean-current as the intended Willem -> Willem van Oranje text rename plus broad comment/prose cleanup; apparent removed XML art/data lines inspected in the diff are quotations inside comments rather than live tag removals.
  KI#307 is a SAS-specific Main Interface localization-cache invalidation defect introduced by practical 5279. Before this optimization, labels such as Strength, Air Strength, Movement, Range, Level, Experience, Maintenance and the BUG dead-civ tag were translated with `localText.getText(...)` when rendered. 5279 moves these translated strings into `CvMainInterface.initState()`. That function's own contract says it runs when the main interface is created after starting/loading a game or when Python modules are reloaded; it has no `getCurrentLanguage()` key or `LanguageChanged` handler. The in-game language option calls `CyGame().setCurrentLanguage(...)`, and current Main Interface still consumes the cached strings afterward. The language-ID pattern is not the defect: K-Mod practical 693 introduced it so the Info and Exotic Foreign Advisor text/layout refreshes after a language change, local Base AdvCiv 1.14 retains both implementations, and SAS correctly extended the pattern to its other advisor `initText()` caches. The persistent Main Interface optimization was the outlier because it omitted that established guard. The validated repair keys these translations by the current language and refreshes them from the persistent interface update path; changing language after loading a save confirmed the affected labels updated without recreating the interface. AdvCiv-SAS officially supports English only, so this remains a low-priority robustness repair rather than a multilingual-support promise.
  Practical 5278 is clean-current: its caches are language-independent symbol IDs/yield glyphs and the movement denominator, and the replacements preserve formatting/value semantics. Practical 5277's AI upgrade ROI gates also close clean-current as explicit tuning policy: affordability remains mandatory; the gates are AI-only and applied only when raw strength increases; upgrades without positive strength gain remain allowed for non-strength benefits; experienced units receive configured extra allowance; and later descendants tune the same ROI architecture rather than repair a broken call contract. The same commit temporarily changes the source `MAX_CIV_PLAYERS` setting from 48 back to 18 as build/configuration lineage; practical 5743 later restores the current 48-player source setting, so that toggle is historical rather than a current survivor.

  KI#309 (SAS-specific) is confirmed at practical 5160. AI_religionValue replaced the old equal-player diplomacy term with a power-weighted loop over PlayerIter<MAJOR_CIV,OTHER_KNOWN_TO>. Vassal players were skipped, but every remaining player was assigned iBlocPower = 100 + GET_TEAM(itOther->getTeam()).getPower(true). CvTeam::getPower(true) already sums every player on that team plus all vassal players, so a rival team with multiple non-vassal players had the same full team/bloc power added once for each team member. E.g. a two-player rival team with 2,000 combined power contributed roughly 2*(100+2000), while an equally powerful one-player rival team contributed only 100+2000. This contradicted the feature's stated goal of weighting religions by military power and could materially bias team games. The prepared repair preserves the player/religion loop but weights each known player's own religion by that player's own power, so permanent-team members and vassals are each represented exactly once without requiring an arbitrary single religion for a mixed-religion team.

Practical 5108 adds civic-switch hysteresis and changes the unlimited-specialist anarchy scaling from adoption-only to any civic change. The hysteresis is a tunable anti-oscillation policy.   KI#315 (SAS-specific) is confirmed at practical 5106. AI_civicValue is an absolute civic score used by AI_bestCivic to compare candidates. The new generalized unlimited-specialist block sets iDelta=iS, where iS is +1 for a civic not currently active and -1 for the currently active civic, computes a positive iTotal from the specialists the civic enables, then executes iValue += iDelta * iTotal. Consequently the same unlimited-specialist benefit raises a candidate civic's score before adoption but lowers that civic's score after adoption, creating a systematic pick-then-drop/undervalue-active-civic bias. K-Mod's previous unlimited-specialist component and neighboring intrinsic positive civic benefits add their value positively; iS is appropriate only to genuinely marginal state-change calculations, not to negate the civic's own benefit. Current source retains iValue += iDelta * iTotal, while 5108 merely adds hysteresis around the resulting oscillation. Repair direction: score the civic's intrinsic unlimited-specialist benefit positively in its absolute value and use adoption/drop state only for genuinely state-dependent marginal terms/anarchy costs.
  KI#316 (SAS-specific) is a separate practical-5106 root in the same block. The new Great People Points term states that it values specialist GPP at the player's average GP multiplier but calculates iGPP * max(0, AI_averageGreatPeopleMultiplier() - 100) / 25. AI_averageGreatPeopleMultiplier is the full percentage based on city getTotalGreatPeopleRateModifier and has a normal 100% baseline. Therefore every ordinary specialist's base GPP contributes exactly zero at a normal 100% GP modifier; only bonus multiplier above the baseline is valued. Current ordinary specialists have positive base GPP, so this is reachable without exotic data. The defect is independent from KI#315 because fixing the sign would still leave GPP absent. Repair direction: apply the intended scaling to the full GP-rate multiplier (with deliberate normalization/weighting), rather than subtracting away the baseline before valuing GPP.
  Practical 5104's Emancipation-to-Wage-Labor migration is internally complete for the touched runtime event predicates and civic valuation reference. Interstate and High Warlord callbacks plus their XML civic gates are mechanically redirected because the old civic ID is removed/reworked; unlike Earth Day, the local source does not establish a comparably hard thematic or executable contradiction, so these substitutions are retained as audit notes rather than manufactured KIs. The simultaneous Serfdom/Wage Labor XML redesign is deliberate balance/content work.
  KI#317 (SAS-specific) is confirmed at practical 5103. The replacement iCivicPercentAnger valuation introduces separate SAS scale defines for unhappy and happy cities, but inside the city loop it computes current slack, sets bCityHappy=(iSlack>=0), and immediately continues when bCityHappy is true. The later ternary that selects the happy-city scale is therefore unreachable. More importantly, for an already-active pressure civic the AI is evaluating the consequences of dropping it: a currently happy city with small positive slack can become unhappy after the hypothetical civic-anger increase, yet the code discards that city before computing/applying iDeltaUnhappy. Current source retains this exact ordering, and current Theocracy has a nonzero iCivicPercentAnger value, so the path is live. Repair direction: compute the hypothetical anger/unhappiness and resulting post-change slack first, then value the actual welfare change (and apply happy/unhappy scaling to a reachable post-change state) rather than filtering solely on the city's pre-change happiness.
  No additional current survivor was established in this family. The non-runtime civic/tech XML changes are deliberate balancing/pacing work, and the older cheap-unit production architecture is superseded.   KI#319 is a SAS-specific follow-up regression in practical 5093's valid correction for an inherited Base AdvCiv Scout/Explorer no-progress loop and failed attempt-limit assertion. The new CvSelectionGroupAI::AI_update tripwire records only the current head unit's x/y coordinates, movesLeft and the group's mission-queue length. After the head's CvUnitAI::AI_update returns, if those scalar values appear unchanged it pushes MISSION_SKIP on the group and breaks. This is not a complete progress predicate. CvUnitAI::AI_setUnitAIType, used by ordinary role-conversion paths, changes the unit's AI role and then calls joinGroup(NULL), detaching the acting head from its old group. Current examples include collateral -> ATTACK_CITY in AI_collateralMove, excess PILLAGE -> ATTACK/ATTACK_CITY in AI_pillageMove and excess EXPLORE -> ATTACK in AI_exploreMove. Before 5093, if other units remained in the old group, the enclosing selection-group loop naturally iterated again and let the new head act. With the tripwire, the new head is not compared by identity; stacked units normally share the same plot and can have the same movement allowance, so the old group can satisfy the unchanged x/y/moves/queue test even though its head and group membership changed. The tripwire then pushes MISSION_SKIP onto the remaining stack, consuming that group's turn. The repair also snapshots and compares head-unit ID and UnitAI role, so the original genuinely unchanged Scout/Explorer spin still trips while a head replacement or role conversion continues normally. The compiled DLL completed a quick autoplay without an observed issue.
  KI#318 (SAS-specific) is confirmed at practical 5092. The inherited CvRandom::getInt contract explicitly allows iNum=0 and returns 0 for both iNum=0 and iNum=1. The commit correctly hardens negative ranges in CvRandom, but three CvCityAI opportunistic-Wonder callers simultaneously wrap their entire old calculation in `if (iWonderConstructRand > 0)`. Before the change, a zero value still supplied a zero random contribution while preserving the fixed base terms: the affected paths used +7, +8 or a base iWonderRand of 8. After 5092, zero skips those opportunities entirely. Current leader XML has ordinary zero-valued leaders Montezuma and Sitting Bull (in addition to default/Barbarian), so this is reachable current AI behavior, and current source retains all three guards. A fourth guard in AI_bestBuildingThreshold is harmless at zero because the old random term itself was zero. Repair direction: distinguish invalid negative ranges from valid zero; preserve the inherited zero-random-contribution semantics while guarding/asserting only negative inputs.
  Practical 5091 makes CvBuildInfo::isFeatureRemove(NO_FEATURE) return false before indexing/asserting the Feature array; that is the correct semantic result for 'no feature' and survives as defensive hardening. Practical 5090 clamps forced specialist counts to available slots and refuses to force an invalid specialist in AI_assignWorkingPlots. This is a valid safety repair; later BFC-Artist convenience commits explain why Artist was the observed trigger and add their own lifecycle guards. The old tracker called the observed assert a Base-AdvCiv issue, but current lineage shows the important present behavior is simply the safe validity/clamp guard; no additional survivor is counted here.

The original rollout's empirical documentation therefore should not be read as evidence that every new prefilter branch was already affecting normal major AIs in 4950.
  Practical 4949 deliberately introduces hard promotion-selection policy (forbidden situational promotions and strong first choices for strict city-attack/city-defense UnitAIs) while also consolidating promotion identities such as Navigator and collateral-damage tiers. The current XML/runtime identities used by the surviving rules exist, absent optional lookup IDs safely resolve to NO_PROMOTION rather than matching a real candidate, and the remaining choices are strategy policy rather than a legality/invariant failure. No current defect was established from the hard rules.
  Practicals 4948 through 4940 intentionally remove old `uiFlag` save-format compatibility branches across strength-memory/game/init/map/team/unit/area/plot/city/player serializers. The retained code corresponds to the then-current/latest serialization path while the project explicitly declares cross-version savegame compatibility unsupported. No current same-version read/write mismatch was established; deleted fallback branches are therefore intentional compatibility debt removal rather than runtime defects.
  Practical 4939 is the birth of the unsafe rendering cache already repaired at 4982: it caches live `NiColorA` values/references as process-statics in CvGameInterface. Practical 4982 deliberately removes those live-object statics after the UI/menu-crash concern; current rendering only caches stable ColorTypes IDs where useful and fetches live color values. The other 4939 static additions inspected are configuration/enum IDs rather than equivalent live `NiColorA` references. No new current root is counted.
  Practicals 4938 through 4936 are early specialist-growth and first-city/site-selection policy. Their low-pop specialist guards, coastal/low-food first-city heuristics and free initial site-search window are deliberate AI tuning and have later descendants/repairs in the already-audited first-city and specialist lineages. No current invalid enum, impossible site or deterministic starvation/placement failure was established from these birth forms.
  Practical 4934 partially repairs the land-build-cache split introduced at 4930 by restoring `AI_getImprovementValue` for water plots after the fully-disabled evaluator left Work Boats parked; it also changes `AI_neededSeaWorkers` lookahead from 5 to 0 so future/unbuildable resource technology does not demand premature boats. The later/current duplicate-primary/second-water-area problem is existing inherited KI#216 and was fixed at practical 6170; it is not re-counted here.
  Practicals 4932/4931 are a dense first-generation AIFoundValue/unit-production/Worker-policy rewrite. Their map-heavy offensive/defensive UnitAI weighting, settlement-on-resource/terrain scoring and per-city Worker selection are primarily explicit heuristics; later 4962-4984 and much newer Worker/site-selection architectures repeatedly guard, revert or replace the unstable portions. The exact NO_BUILD-before-sentinel crash contract audited at C141 is not born here: reverse-diff search pins that specific bad condition to practical 4922, which remains below this checkpoint and will be audited explicitly there.
  KI#213 is broadened at practical 4930, and 4930 is now pinned as its architectural birth. That commit functionally disables `CvCityAI::AI_getImprovementValue` entirely so the custom `CvUnitAI::AI_bestCityBuild` owns land-improvement choices; 4934 restores the legacy evaluator only for water. Current source therefore still deliberately returns 0/NO_BUILD for every land plot in the legacy city best-build cache. C006/KI#213 had already proven one live consequence in Worker-demand accounting (worked unimproved Flood Plains can be a valid custom Worker job while the legacy cache says NO_BUILD) and already identified the root as multiple non-unit systems consuming an intentionally-empty land cache.
  A second deterministic supported consequence is now source-proven: current `CvPlayerAI::AI_advancedStartPlaceCity` explicitly calls `pCity->AI_updateBestBuild()` and then purchases land improvements only from `AI_getBestBuildValue`/`AI_getBestBuild`, rejecting NO_BUILD and non-improvement builds. Since every ordinary land improvement is zero/NO_BUILD by design, AI Advanced Start cannot purchase any new land improvements through this loop. The same loop only buys the associated population after successful improvement purchases, so that economic-development/population branch is also suppressed; remaining Advanced Start points can instead be spent elsewhere and are ultimately converted to gold on exit. This is reachable normal supported gameplay: current SAS retains `GAMEOPTION_ADVANCED_START`, and nonhuman players with Advanced Start points automatically execute `AI_doAdvancedStart()` when their turn activates. The already-recorded KI#213 repair direction—one shared city/plot land-build-policy evaluator consumed by both the city cache and Worker selector while keeping unit pathing/mission execution in CvUnitAI—would repair both Worker-demand and Advanced-Start symptoms at their common root. KI#213 is therefore broadened, not duplicated with a new number.

SAS-C143 - practical 4929 through 4920 Worker/citizen/site/production audit - COMPLETE; KI#320 FOUND
  Reviewed 10 runtime-source SHAs: practical 4929 / 523b965b49, 4928 / beb304dcd5, 4927 / 1ce83aadff, 4926 / 4148c4ace7, 4925 / c4ffe69511, 4924 / a6525a8b7c, 4923 / 0b1701c016, 4922 / 7e7a475897, 4921 / cc59407b55 and 4920 / d02f38ffd3. One new current SAS-specific defect, KI#320, is confirmed. Several genuine early Worker regressions are also pinned to their later repairs rather than re-counted.
  KI#320 (SAS-specific) is confirmed at practical 4928 in CvCityAI::AI_yieldValue. The KI#40 growth policy deliberately has two components when a city has spare happiness and is not currently converting food into production: multiply food value by the happiness surplus, and at 3+ surplus reduce hammer/production value so growth can beat a strong mine/hill. The food half works. The hammer half is dead because the function completely normalizes and adds `iProductionValue` to `iValue` before computing `iHappySurplus`; only afterward does the new block divide the now-local `iProductionValue`, and that variable is never used again before return. Current CvCityAI.cpp retains this exact order. Later descendants touching the block preserve the executable order, while current Main Changes Guide and KI#40 documentation still explicitly promise that high happiness 'deemphasize[s] hammers' / reduces hammer-production value. This is therefore an implementation/order-of-operations defect, not merely stale prose or a balance preference. Surgical repair direction: compute/apply the happiness-based `iProductionValue` reduction before production is normalized and added to `iValue`, while keeping the existing food multiplier and `!isFoodProduction()` exception. This is separate from KI#40 itself: KI#40 is the intended policy/feature; KI#320 is the dead second half of its implementation.
  Practical 4929's bonus-roading/Worker-food changes do not establish another current root. One surviving `AI_betterPlotBuild` comment/branch is semantically misleading: `doesImprovementConnectBonus` means an improvement is a valid trade-enabling improvement for that bonus/tech, not that the improved plot needs no route, so its local `bNeedsRoad` naming/test is conceptually inverted. Current active resource-connection paths nevertheless compensate: `AI_improveBonus` and the early Worker-on-improved-bonus check explicitly detect a proper improved but disconnected bonus and call route/connect logic, while normal `AI_improveCity` executes the custom selected build directly rather than passing it through this branch. No deterministic present missed-resource connection was proven, so this remains an unnumbered robustness/comment issue rather than a manufactured KI.
  Practical 4927 introduces a real short-lived SAS Worker regression: its new move-away condition calls `AI_nextCityToImprove` when the current city's `foodDifference(false) <= 0`, so a Worker can be told to leave precisely because its city is stagnant/starving. Practical 4931 removes that backwards food condition and keeps only a bounded unhappiness case. Current Worker architecture is much newer and does not retain this trigger; this is repaired historical lineage.
  Practical 4923 is the birth of another repaired Worker/citizen-allocation weakness. Its starvation gate rejects a proposed reassignment whenever the post-swap city would still have negative food, even when the new job substantially improves food and reduces the deficit. Practical 5996 explicitly repairs this exact overstrict condition by only blocking a still-starving reassignment when `iNextFood < iCurrentFood`, allowing partial food recovery. The contemporary Bursa example/documentation confirms the failure mode (high-food plots remained unused while Artists were retained during severe starvation). This is historical lineage related to the already-known KI#34 family, not a current new root.
  Practical 4922 is now pinned as the exact birth of the Worker sentinel/output-contract bug reconstructed from the newer side at C141. The new `AI_bestCityBuild` tests `!canBuild(kPlot, eBestSupposedBuild) || eBestSupposedBuild == NO_BUILD`, so C++ can evaluate `canBuild` with the NO_BUILD sentinel before short-circuiting to the sentinel test. Practical 4962 reverses the validation order, separately rejects NO_BUILD before calling `canBuild`, initializes output pointers/values defensively and only publishes a valid plot/build candidate after acceptance. Current source retains the hardened architecture. Thus the old reproducible Worker-crash lineage is concretely SAS-state/output-contract provenance, not evidence that the inherited `isSamePlotGroup` predicate itself was intrinsically broken. The large hardcoded bonus->Build table introduced around this rewrite is also superseded by the current data-driven `CvPlot::SAS_getBonusSpecificBuild` cache built from live Build/Improvement/Bonus XML.
  Practical 4925's broad sea-unit devaluation and Settler experiment is mostly temporary policy lineage. Practical 4932 explicitly restores the K-Mod military sea-unit scoring/multipliers after the early experiment proved too broad. The Settler UnitClass one-player-instance limit survives intentionally and is documented as the KI#37 workaround/rebalance, not an accidental legality defect. Practical 4924's nearest-city site-distance shaping is likewise policy ancestry superseded by the current bounded CitySiteEvaluator distance logic.
  Practicals 4926, 4921 and 4920 are early iterations of the custom Worker selector and plot-build rewrite. Their one-worker-per-target preference, feature-removal/route handling and bonus-priority concepts are repeatedly rewritten by 4922-4984 and much later Worker infrastructure; no additional present-day invalid build, duplicate mission or deterministic stranded-worker path was established from their birth forms. Practical 4926's accompanying technology/building/leader XML changes are intentional data/balance edits whose current identifiers resolve normally.

  Practical 4919's original Citizen-specialist guard is safer than its commit title first suggests. `AI_specialistValue` reaches `AI_jobChangeValue`, and the birth guard returns 1 for the default `SPECIALIST_CITIZEN`. `AI_addBestCitizen` initializes `iBestValue` to -1, so Citizen is strongly disfavored compared with productive plots/specialists but remains the engine's guaranteed unlimited last-resort specialist when no ordinary job is available. That matches the KI#44.5 title's intended "unless absolutely necessary" contract.
  Descendant tracing from 4919 exposes the current KI#44.5 failure precisely. Practical 4938 changes the Citizen result from the safe last-resort value 1 to the global hard-forbidden value `SAS_AI_JOB_CHANGE_VALUE_AI_JOB_FORBIDDEN` (currently -100000). Current `CvCity::isSpecialistValid` explicitly keeps the default specialist valid regardless of ordinary specialist-slot caps, but `AI_addBestCitizen` only accepts candidates whose value is greater than its -1 no-candidate sentinel. Therefore a -100000 Citizen cannot be selected even when every workable plot and ordinary specialist slot is exhausted. `AI_assignWorkingPlots` has no final fallback: if `AI_addBestCitizen` returns false while `extraPopulation()` or `extraSpecialists()` is positive, it emits "failed to assign extra population" / "failed to assign extra specialist", breaks and then reaches the extra-population assertion with real productive population unassigned. Current `SPECIALIST_CITIZEN` is not a zero-output dummy (0 food, +1 production, +1 commerce), so this is a gameplay loss as well as a debug-assert cascade. KI#44.5 already records exactly this cascade and explicitly wondered whether it was caused by Citizen being unavailable as fallback; the source now proves that relationship. KI#44.5 is therefore broadened rather than duplicated as KI#321. Preferred root-level repair: preserve the hard strategic deprioritization during normal comparisons, but if `AI_addBestCitizen` finds no plot/ordinary specialist candidate, explicitly assign the valid default Citizen as guaranteed last resort. This also preserves the analogous human convenience policy without making a city fail assignment merely because all preferred jobs are exhausted.
  Practicals 4918 through 4914 are principally Sevopedia/concept/improvement presentation and intentional terrain/balance data. The new Rivers/Road/Railroad concept-routing and improvement Terrain/Feature-valid panels use the exposed current getters and current RouteInfos (Road/Railroad) consistently. Practical 4916's ominous "fix inaccurate code" title is only a small text-spacing/centering correction in `SevoPediaImprovement.py`. Practical 4914's broad Python surface is overwhelmingly comment wording plus the Improvement-page refactor; the live Workshop/Tundra and Tundra-commerce XML edits are explicit balance changes rather than accidental identifier drift.
  Practicals 4912 and 4911 do contain a real historical unit-AI data mismatch. When Robotic Recon Drone and the reworked land-domain Airship are converted from DOMAIN_AIR to DOMAIN_LAND/recon, their `DefaultUnitAI` is accidentally left as `UNITAI_ATTACK_AIR` even though their permitted UnitAI list is Explorer-oriented. Practical 4949 explicitly changes both to `UNITAI_EXPLORE`; current XML retains the repaired values. The other new Future-Era units in 4910/4909/4903 have domain/combat/default-UnitAI combinations consistent with their current land roles and their referenced classes/technologies resolve. No present orphan class, bad default role or impossible prerequisite was established.
  Practical 4908 disables the experimental repeatable-tech flag that 4907 had enabled one commit earlier. Practical 4907's `buildBRepeatString` therefore survives as dormant presentation infrastructure: current CIV4TechInfos has no `<bRepeat>1</bRepeat>` technology. Its player-context `isHasTech` suppression could be questionable for a hypothetical live repeatable tech after the first acquisition, but there is no current SAS surface on which it can misdescribe a tech, so no current KI is manufactured from dormant infrastructure.
  Practicals 4906/4905 close clean-current around their Pedia additions and resource/feature data. The 4905 Feature remove-production/time validator only raises when a later positive value is greater than the first one, so as generic validation code it would miss a lower differing value encountered later; however an exhaustive current CIV4BuildInfos check finds no Feature whose positive removal-production or removal-time values differ among Builds. Thus current SAS data satisfies the stricter intended invariant and the asymmetry has no present consequence. Current source still contains this mismatch, but the function is explicitly marked `*** OBSOLETE ***` and a repository-wide current runtime search finds no call outside its own definition. It is recorded as latent obsolete-helper semantics rather than promoted to a KI. If the helper is ever revived, the original explicit `is True` / `is False` tri-state contract should be restored.

First, the Camel-resource adaptation accidentally inserts a bare `BONUS_CAMEL` identifier into `CvRandomEventInterface.doWiningMonks2()` immediately after fetching the event plot. No Python variable by that name is defined there, so firing that event would raise NameError before the improvement is placed. Practical 4833 removes exactly the stray line. Second, 4832 leaves a large LeaderHead debug dump (`printObjAttrs` plus the flavor/NoWar/contact/memory/BBAI helper) unconditionally inside `SevoPediaLeader.placeLeaderHeadPane()`, meaning every Leader page would emit the expensive debug output regardless of the intended debug flag. Practical 4833 removes those calls as well. The helper's hardcoded debug loop limits therefore do not become normal long-term panel logic. Current source contains neither regression.
  Practicals 4835/4837 are the transition from the generated `leaders_data.py` runtime dependency to direct `CvLeaderHeadInfo` getter-driven AI-Personality data. Later descendants repeatedly refactor the cache/category/normalization pipeline (including the separately certified 5299 family), and current source no longer depends on the removed runtime leaders_data module. The getter-existence guards and excluded-leader handling operate on real current LeaderHeadInfo methods; no stale generated-data or missing-key current root was established from this birth layer. Practical 4837's generated-script key renaming is external tooling/document consistency rather than runtime Pedia state.
  Practical 4832 also exposes the BBAI victory weights and the two missing attitude-threshold getters to Python while introducing Camel content. The C++/Boost-Python bindings are direct forwards to CvLeaderHeadInfo fields and remain the intended source used by later AI-Personality work. The temporary debug exploration of Contact/Memory ranges is removed/gated before becoming production logic. The Camel/Movies event-data replacement contains the above Winning-Monks typo but otherwise updates the relevant runtime event callbacks and XML identifiers together; later resource/map audits cover the current Camel distribution/usage architecture.

  KI#321 is a SAS-specific Sevopedia exact-unit/building civilization-availability misinformation defect. Practical 4755 introduces SevoPediaUnit.placeExclusiveCivs with an explicit branch saying that when the selected unit is the class default, it should show "Available to all civilizations" instead of enumerating civilization mappings. Practical 4764 copies the same architecture to Buildings. Current descendants are named placeCivilizations but retain the same default-vs-unique split and current localization renders the default branch simply as "All". That is false for the exact item displayed on the page: CvPlayer::canTrain rejects a UnitTypes whenever getCivilization().getUnit(unitClass) != that exact unit, and CvPlayer::canConstruct analogously rejects a BuildingTypes whenever the civilization's class mapping is not that exact building. Current CivilizationInfos contain 135 mappings that differ from the class default or are NONE. Concrete ordinary examples include Rome mapping UNITCLASS_SWORDSMAN to UNIT_ROMAN_LEGIONARY instead of UNIT_SWORDSMAN and America mapping BUILDINGCLASS_COURTHOUSE to BUILDING_AMERICAN_FEDERAL_COURTHOUSE instead of BUILDING_COURTHOUSE. Thus the base Swordsman/Courthouse pages can say "Civilizations: All" even though those civilizations cannot train/construct the exact base item. Barbarian NONE mappings make the literal "All" claim still broader. Unit and Building are one shared root rather than two KIs because the later Building code is a direct copy of the same panel contract. The first exact-mapping repair was rejected after screenshot-0148 runtime review because enumerating all ordinary civilization buttons duplicated the Civilization list and Replaced By information and made the panel worse. Screenshot 0218 then showed that the exact replacement phrase `All using this default` overcorrected in the opposite direction by wrapping one word per line in the narrow panel. The final repair uses `AllR`; the existing Sevopedia Legend defines it as All Remaining Civilizations, excluding civilization-specific replacements and NONE mappings and illustrating the Swordsman/Legionary case. Unique pages retain their exact civilization buttons. Runtime review confirmed that the final panel and Legend display correctly.

SAS-C150 - practical 4738 through 4732 first-generation AIP normalization/config/generated-cache consolidation audit - COMPLETE; KI#322 FOUND retrospectively in current shared predump/runtime descendant
  Reviewed 7 runtime-source SHAs: practical 4738 / 7e7ab111c1, 4737 / ca48d0b889, 4736 / 64a633b346, 4735 / 000be3f76c, 4734 / 01d6b62db5, 4733 / a7bba2fed9 and 4732 / 50f7983166. Practical 4731 is documentation-only and is not counted. The historical normalization/shifting/config consolidation itself closes as replaced/refactored lineage; one current cross-runtime AIP normalization defect was discovered while tracing its descendant contract and is promoted as KI#322.
  Practicals 4734/4735 simplify the early normalizer to the mathematically equivalent shift `(0, value-min, max-min)` before scaling to 0..100. Their sanity tests and generated expected-data samples preserve the intended mapping, and that shift helper survives conceptually in current AIP code. Practical 4736 primarily reorganizes displayed attitude fields/headers and fixes explanatory normalization comments; 4737 adds the few varying attitude-change-limit fields with per-field inversion where lower negative limits represent stronger aversion. Later AIP revisions substantially replace these early display configurations and direct getter exposure, so no current omitted/duplicated field root is attributed to the 4736/4737 presentation state.
  Practical 4732 is the first large consolidation into shared AIP helpers plus generated `leaders_data.py`; 4733 removes/deprecates several inaccurate/obsolete aggregate experiments. That generated-data architecture is later replaced by direct CvLeaderHeadInfo getter providers (notably the practical-4835 lineage) and then by the current shared runtime/workflow provider architecture. Current independent `aip_predump_values.py` validation on the supplied tree compares 7,810 effective displayed tuples against the committed predump with 0 ordinary mismatches and 0 missing/unparsed entries. Thus stale generated leaders_data or early aggregate formulas do not survive as the current issue.
  KI#322 (SAS-specific, retrospective current descendant) is a Python-version-dependent AIP normalization rounding defect. Current `ai_utils_shared_with_civ4.py` deliberately serves both the Civ4 runtime and outside-Civ4 workflow Python, but `round_current_runtime(value)` is implemented as `int(round(value))`. Civ4's documented embedded runtime is CPython 2.4, while the predump/checker script is Python 3. Python 2.x rounds exact half ties away from zero; Python 3 changed exact half ties to nearest-even. Therefore the helper does not have shared semantics at `.5` ties even though both consumers call the same source function.
  The defect is directly reachable through the supported manual/runtime fallback: current `SAS_SEVOPEDIA_LEADER_AI_PERSONALITY_CACHE_USE_PREDUMPED=0` bypasses the committed predump and calls the shared cache builder inside Civ4; documentation also retains this as the manual in-game regeneration path. Rebuilding the exact current XML+UWAI cache with the normal Python-3 rounder versus Python-2-style half-away rounding changes 30 displayed tuples: 21 leaders' `iAggregatedContactProbReligionPressure` normalize from committed/predump 52 to in-game-runtime 53 (raw 52 over range 10..90 gives exactly 52.5), and 9 leaders' inverted `getLimitedWarRand` normalize from 88 to 87. The current checker reports 7,810/7,810 matches because it and the committed predump both execute on the Python-3 side, so it cannot detect this runtime-fallback divergence.
  The cross-runtime mismatch crystallizes with practical 5973, which adds the outside-Civ4 Python-3 predump checker/writer and refactors the shared normalizer. Its comment explicitly preserves the committed contact value 52 rather than half-away 53 by using built-in `round()`, but that assumption describes Python-3 behavior, not the Civ4 Python-2.4 runtime. The repair replaces built-in `round()` with explicit Python-2.4-safe ties-to-even arithmetic, adds focused positive/negative half-tie and ordinary-fraction assertions, and invokes them from the predump workflow. The Python-3 effective-value checker reports all 7,810 committed tuples matching with zero missing/unparsed entries, and runtime screenshot 0217 confirms Bismarck's Religion Pressure remains 52 with predumped-cache loading disabled.
  Certified cursor advances through practical 4732 / 50f7983166 as runtime-source SHA 1,158 / 1,317. Confirmed archaeology totals become 120 = 40 inherited + 80 SAS-specific. Next runtime-source SHA: practical 4730 / 3101a1efff.

SAS-C153 - practical 4647 through 4628 Unit-Chart / imported Terrain-Feature Pedia / NewConcept repair audit - COMPLETE; KI#323 and KI#324 FOUND

  Reviewed 14 runtime-source SHAs: practical 4647 / baa641079b, 4646 / f9d9c595d7, 4645 / ac4bc48449, 4644 / 99d3aae70e, 4643 / afb1a9044f, 4642 / 6c107d6fd8, 4641 / 18dc45fc3b, 4640 / f0f55128ea, 4639 / 10c14b0736, 4638 / 2dd4efa3da, 4637 / ce3f71f509, 4634 / 201500d338, 4633 / 3982fb2cde and 4628 / 1bb92cbeee. Practicals 4636/4635 and 4632 through 4629 are XML/text/music/docs/art only and are not counted. Two current Sevopedia Unit Chart misinformation roots are confirmed: inherited RFC/DoC KI#323 and retrospective SAS-specific KI#324.
  KI#323 (inherited third-party, RFC Dawn of Civilization) is the Unit Chart's incomplete First Strike column. Practical 4637 imports RFC Dawn of Civilization's CvPediaUnitChart.py nearly verbatim; the upstream RFC source itself tests and prints only UnitInfo.getFirstStrikes() and never considers getChanceFirstStrikes(). SAS practical 4639 splits First Strike into its own column, 4644 moves the logic into placeTableFirstStrike, and later descendants preserve the same guaranteed-only test. Current source still renders a value only when getFirstStrikes() > 0. This conflicts with Civ4's actual first-strike contract: CvGameTextMgr combines getFirstStrikes()+getChanceFirstStrikes() and renders a guaranteed-to-maximum range whenever chance first strikes exist. Current SAS XML has 14 units with first-strike data. Samurai and Cacador have 0 guaranteed + 1 chance first strike, so the Unit Chart currently shows a blank First Strike cell despite real first-strike capability; Archer, Longbowman, Skirmisher, Bowman and Zhuge Nu have 1 guaranteed + 1 chance and the chart shows only 1 instead of the real 1-2 range. Preferred repair: blank only when guaranteed+chance is zero; otherwise show the guaranteed count when chance is zero and a guaranteed-(guaranteed+chance) range when chance is nonzero, matching the DLL help semantics.
  KI#324 (SAS-specific, retrospective descendant born above this historical cursor) is the Unit Chart's false Collateral display gate. The RFC/4637 import correctly shows collateral only when getCollateralDamage() > 0. Practical 4900 / 56ea046e0d changes that gate to `getCollateralDamage() > 0 or getCollateralDamageLimit()` while changing the cell format from damage-limit to damage/limit. In current Unit XML, iCollateralDamageLimit defaults to 200 even for units with no collateral attack. Across chart-visible units with a real UnitCombat type, 76 have CollateralDamage=0 but a nonzero 200 limit, while only 18 have actual nonzero CollateralDamage. Consequently ordinary units such as Warrior, Swordsman, Samurai, Rifleman, Tank, Frigate and Fighter can be shown as `0%/200% (...)` collateral units even though they inflict no collateral damage. The engine's operative predicate is the nonzero damage value; the surgical repair is simply to restore the original `getCollateralDamage() > 0` display gate while retaining the newer useful damage/limit/max formatting for genuine collateral units. C144 previously certified practical 4900 without noticing this chart/data interaction; C153 records the retrospective correction openly rather than hiding it.
  The remaining 4647-4637 Unit Chart evolution is presentation/refactoring around the imported table: separate Moves/Air Range, Bombard, Evasion and Interception columns, dynamic air/non-air widths, icons, background and per-cell helper extraction. Current Combat uses AirCombat when appropriate, Bombard distinguishes BombardRate/BombRate, air columns use their dedicated getters, Withdrawal uses WithdrawalProbability, and no additional current data/column contract failure was established after checking these paths. KI#323 and KI#324 are independent because one concerns missing chance-first-strike information in the inherited chart and the other is a later SAS-only collateral gate regression.
  Practicals 4634 and 4633 import RFC/DoC Feature and Terrain Sevopedia pages. Their birth relationship panels use FeatureInfo.isTerrain, ImprovementInfo.getFeatureMakesValid/getTerrainMakesValid/isHillsMakesValid and BonusInfo isFeature/isTerrain/isHills predicates consistently with the XML relationship data. Much newer descendants substantially expand these pages (build/remove production, build-capable units, resources, improvements and terrain/feature combat/traversal panels) and were already audited from the newer side around practicals 5459 and 5062/5061. No separate surviving birth-import defect was established here beyond those already-accounted later lineages.
  Practical 4628 is itself a repair: the earlier Sevopedia navigation assumed NewConceptInfo entries belonged to the ConceptInfo list/category ordering. 4628 restores the dedicated NewConcept list/category route. Current centralized category/list infrastructure supersedes this first repair and no stale Concept-vs-NewConcept index mismatch attributable to it survives.

  KI#325 is an inherited BtS past-city-name identity defect exposed while auditing practical 4345 / e490c254d0; practical 4345 broadens the defect but does not introduce its underlying root. Follow-up comparison against current SAS, local Base AdvCiv 1.14 and Civ4CE's BtS source corrected the initial claim that living automatically generated cities stored only translated display identity. `getCivilizationCityName` translates a candidate for validity checking but returns its canonical XML key; `CvCity::setName` stores that key in `m_szName`, and `CvCity::getName()` translates the stored key on demand. A living Aachen and a new `TXT_KEY_CITY_NAME_AACHEN` candidate therefore change display together after a language switch and still collide under the existing visible-name comparison.
  The inherited past-name path is genuinely language-sensitive. BtS disbanding records `kCity.getName()`, freezing the current translation in the destroyed-city-name vector, while future automatic candidates are translated afresh before lookup. Practical 4345 generalizes that vector into all past city names and records `getName()` before a rename, broadening the same root to every former name. Current unchanged data proves reachability: `TXT_KEY_CITY_NAME_AACHEN` displays as English `Aachen` and French `Aix-la-Chapelle`; a past English display no longer equals the later French candidate even though both originate from one canonical key.
  The fixed repair reuses the identity already present in `m_szName`. Generated-name selection passes raw XML keys into validity checks; rename/disband paths store raw canonical keys or literal custom names in the existing past-name vector. Past and living checks compare exact stored identity first and current rendered text second. This preserves canonical reservation across language changes and still rejects a literal/manual name that duplicates the current visible name. The vector's serialized shape remains unchanged, and the current-version-only save policy makes migration of old translated entries unnecessary. The DLL compiled and fresh save file 484 completed its autoplay smoke test without an observed issue.

Practical 4399 fixes Always-War willingness to talk incorrectly treating same-team players as permanent-war opponents. Practical 4397 fixes queued Worker build activation after failed Route-To/Move-To so the build is not executed on the wrong plot. Practical 4385 guards team-leader reassignment when elimination leaves the would-be leader dead. Current descendants retain these repairs.

This is a provenance/contract refinement, not another defect count. Post-completion discarded-hints reconciliation - KI#327 REJECTED; KI#328 PROMOTED; KI#329 HARDENED; broader sweep remains complete
  Re-read ChatGPT-5.6-Sol's dedicated scraps review against current source, Base AdvCiv 1.14, the tracked ancestry and downloaded map references rather than importing its intermediate ranking wholesale. The checksum discussion was stale relative to current HEAD because its diagnostic-only high-player shift had already become fixed KI#326. The other discarded hints remain closed for concrete reasons: the stale-looking Golden Age civic check is protected by the current legal-civic invariant; canal transport theater ownership is ambiguous rather than proven wrong; the later exact-deficit logic prevents the Worker reload edge from causing a wrong delivery or stranded Worker; the human-as-AI-vassal reverse path is disabled by the supported feature contract; current XML keeps the global-warming denominator positive; and the Equal Islands, Peirce, Planet `_dimensions[0]`, `isHasTech(NO_TECH)`, mixed-air-group and income-divisor theories were already disproved by their control flow, types or bounds. These remain useful re-audit notes if their guarding contracts change, not current defects.
  KI#327 is rejected after implementation and runtime review. The inherited advanced BUG Members screen maps candidate teams to arbitrary player representatives and estimates votes through visible player attitude; the actual Base AdvCiv DLL instead averages team-member attitude and applies distinct thresholds, master/vassal substitution, tied-best abstention and stage-4 victory abstention. An exact `CyPlayer` binding was implemented and screenshots 0229/0230 confirmed distinct Resident and Diplomatic Victory allocations. That technical accuracy was not a desirable repair: K-Mod imported this option default-false with its BUG Advisors checkbox already commented out, so screenshots 0222/0224 correctly showed the supported plain Members view; manually enabling the retained poll requires editing the distant user INI. More importantly, calling the exact DLL decision exposes hidden AI strategy/vassal/tie state while the screen still calls itself a recent BUG poll with a statistical margin. The candidate is therefore rejected rather than adding a Python API solely to perfect a dormant, niche and potentially spoiler-like screen; all attempted source/DLL changes were removed.
  KI#328 is promoted as a minor inherited Planet/C2C integer-callback defect. Around center spacing can return a floating terrain-cell width; the concrete 18-continent/separation-10 path yields approximately 64.458. Civ4 requests an integer vector, and `CvDLLPythonIFaceBase.h` asserts `PyInt_Check` before `PyInt_AsLong`, so release coercion/truncation can coexist with a Debug type assertion. The downloaded Planet 0.68/C2C source has the same formula. The repair returns explicit `int` dimensions, preserving the effective release geometry. Runtime screenshot 0225 confirms that the exact extreme configuration generated successfully as a 256-by-188-plot map; its deliberate 18-continent/minimum-10-water spacing explains both the sparse 11-player result and long generation time, neither of which was introduced by the integer fix.
  KI#329 records two source-real but unreproduced inherited BTG hazards without adding them to the confirmed-defect total. Grid's two retry branches reset `iPass` during each search, so a region with land but no acceptable pass-0 plot can repeat rather than reaching default placement; both are changed to finite state matching current Cross and now resolve player/range before the area scan. Grid and Cross also indexed an unproven nonempty diagonal boundary list when forcing Silver. They now fall back to an empty legal land plot already collected within seven tiles, or safely leave the optional normalization unmet if no such plot exists. Screenshots 0226-0228 confirm that the requested ordinary Grid and Cross configurations generated and reached turn 11 without an observed issue. No supported map was proven to reach either old failure, so this remains explicit defensive hardening rather than manufactured runtime evidence.
